# Weil classes and Hodge classes on abelian powers

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-M-0072` | `PM-032.3` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Weil-classes-and-Hodge-classes-on-abelian-powers-October-6-2026` | date 2026-10-06 | derived from the openai/math preprint Weil-classes-and-Hodge-classes-on-abelian-powers-October-6-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the rational Hodge conjecture in every codimension on every self-power of a complex abelian sixfold with an imaginary-quadratic action and a compatible polarization whose rational homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic of signature $`(3,3)`$. We also prove it in every codimension on every self-power of a complex abelian variety of dimension at most five admitting an imaginary-quadratic action. Both results include nonsimple varieties and special periods with additional endomorphisms. The proof uses the companion theorem on the rational Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the rational Hodge conjecture in every codimension on every self-power of a complex abelian sixfold with an imaginary-quadratic action and a compatible polarization whose rational homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic of signature $`(3,3)`$. We also prove it in every codimension on every self-power of a complex abelian variety of dimension at most five admitting an imaginary-quadratic action. Both results include nonsimple varieties and special periods with additional endomorphisms. The proof uses the companion theorem on the rational Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties.

- **claim-split-weil-sixfolds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3ab-split-sixfolds)): Let $E$ be an imaginary quadratic field and let $A$ be a complex abelian sixfold with an $E$-action. Suppose a compatible polarization has hyperbolic rational Hermitian form of signature $(3,3)$. Then the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on $A^m$, for every $m\geq1$.
  - Explanation: On every self-power of a complex abelian sixfold with an imaginary quadratic action and a compatible polarization whose rational Hermitian form is hyperbolic of the split signature, every rational Hodge class is algebraic.
- **claim-quadratic-action-low-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3ab-quadratic-five)): Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety of dimension at most five admitting an action of an imaginary quadratic field. The rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on $A^m$, for every $m\geq1$.
  - Explanation: For abelian varieties of small dimension admitting an imaginary quadratic action, the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on all self-powers, with no separately chosen polarization.
- **claim-cm-supply-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3-cm-operations)): CM supply is preserved by external products, permutations of factors, cup products, and rational algebraic correspondences. It is also preserved by $k$-linear combinations of such correspondences and by scalar conjugation. Every rational Hodge class in a CM-supplied subspace is algebraic.
  - Explanation: Supply from CM abelian varieties is stable under the usual algebraic and cohomological operations, and rational Hodge classes lying in a supplied subspace are algebraic by the CM input.
- **claim-admissible-maps-algebraic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3-admissible-operations)): Every admissible map is induced on the indicated summands by a $k$-linear combination of rational algebraic correspondences. Every pair tensor is algebraic over $k$, and contraction by its inverse pairing is an algebraic cohomological operation. If $W$ is admissible, so is $\overline W$, and there is a pair tensor between $W$ and $\overline W$.
  - Explanation: Admissible degree-one maps, pair tensors and their contractions are algebraic over the coefficient field, and the conjugate of an admissible space carries a pair tensor with it.
- **claim-hodge-group-tensor-stabilizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3-tensor-stabilizer)): Inside the symplectic group of $H$, the group $\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is the pointwise stabilizer of all rational tensors of type $(a,a)$ in $H^{\otimes 2a}$, for all $a\ge0$.
  - Explanation: The Hodge group of a weight-one polarizable structure is exactly the pointwise stabilizer, inside the symplectic group, of all balanced rational tensors.
- **claim-toric-summand-cm-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3-toric-cm-factor)): Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety and let $H\subset H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$ be a rational Hodge summand. If $\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is a torus, then $H$ is isomorphic as a rational Hodge structure to $H^1(B,\mathbb Q)$ for a CM isogeny factor $B$ of $A$.
  - Explanation: A rational Hodge summand of degree-one cohomology with toric Hodge group is the first cohomology of a CM isogeny factor.
- **claim-determinant-criterion-all-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3-tensor-criterion)): Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety, $H=H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$, $J=\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$, and $R=J^{\mathrm{der}}$. Suppose the following data exist.* 1. *A rational Hodge summand $H_0\subset H$ has toric Hodge group. Choose a decomposition of $H_{0,k}$ into CM embedding lines, with repeated occurrences distinguished, and call this finite list $\mathcal E_0$.* 2. *The complement of $H_{0,k}$ is a direct sum of blocks of nonzero admissible spaces. In a symplectic or an orthogonal block, each space is equipped with an admissible isomorphism to a representative $V_i$ chosen as an actual summand of that block, with, respectively, an alternating or a symmetric pair tensor on itself. A linear block has $\dim V_i\ge2$, with $V_i$ again an actual summand, and each space is equipped with an admissible isomorphism to either $V_i$ or $\overline V_i$, paired with each other as in Lemma <a href="#p3:admissible-operations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:admissible-operations">4</a>. The pair identifies $\overline V_i$ with $V_i^*(-1)$ as a Hodge-graded space and with the dual as a group representation.* 3. *Under these identifications the image of $R_k$ contains $$P=\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{Sp}}}\operatorname{Sp}(V_i) \times\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{SO}}}\operatorname{SO}(V_i) \times\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{SL}}}\operatorname{SL}(V_i).$$ Each factor acts by its standard representation on its own copies, by the dual representation on the opposite copies of a linear block, and trivially on every other block and on $H_{0,k}$. The symplectic and orthogonal forms are the inverse pairings.* 4. *Let $W_1,\ldots,W_s$ be any list, with arbitrary repetitions, from $$\{V_i:i\in I_{\mathrm{SO}}\} \ \cup\ \{V_i,\overline V_i:i\in I_{\mathrm{SL}}\}.$$ Whenever there are $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r\in\mathcal E_0$, again with arbitrary repetitions, such that $$\label{p3:eq:determinant-balance} \sum_{j=1}^s\epsilon_\gamma(W_j) +\sum_{a=1}^r\epsilon_\gamma(\ell_a)=0 \quad\text{for every }\gamma\in\operatorname{Gal}(k/\mathbb Q),$$ the line $\bigotimes_{j=1}^sD(W_j)$ is CM-supplied on its separate tensor slots. Empty lists are allowed.* *Then for every $p\ge0$ every rational balanced tensor in $H^{\otimes 2p}$ is the class of a rational algebraic cycle on its separate $A$-slots. The rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension of $A^m$ for every $m\ge1$.* *Call the data *saturated* if every singleton $D(V_i)$ for an orthogonal or linear block is CM-supplied, without any balance condition. For saturated data and any CM abelian variety $M$, the induced data on $A\times M$ are saturated. In particular the same tensor and Hodge-conjecture conclusions hold for every power of $A\times M$.
  - Explanation: If CM lines and classical derived images are available and the balanced determinant products are CM-supplied, then all balanced tensors and all Hodge classes on all powers are algebraic; saturated data stay saturated after adding CM factors.
- **claim-correspondence-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3-correspondence-transfer)): Let $\mathscr S$ be a collection of smooth projective varieties such that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of members of $\mathscr S$. Let $Y_1,\ldots,Y_v$ be smooth projective varieties. Suppose that, for each $i$ and each degree $q$, $H^q(Y_i,\mathbb Q)$ is spanned by images of maps $$\Gamma_*:H^{q-2j}(S,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^q(Y_i,\mathbb Q), \qquad \Gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^{\dim S+j}(S\times Y_i)_\mathbb Q,\quad S\in\mathscr S.$$ Then the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of the $Y_i$, with arbitrary repetitions and with any additional members of $\mathscr S$.* *The same conclusion holds if the spanning is given over $k$ by restrictions of maps induced by $k$-linear combinations of rational algebraic correspondences, provided their images span the full target degree after scalar extension.
  - Explanation: If the cohomology of each target variety is spanned by correspondence images from a family closed under the Hodge conjecture on products, the conjecture holds on all finite products of the targets.
- **claim-hyperbolic-weil-plane-algebraic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-weil-plane)): Let $E$ be an imaginary quadratic field. Let $A$ be a complex abelian sixfold with a unital $E$-action and a compatible polarization whose actual homological Hermitian form $h_E$ is hyperbolic of signature $(3,3)$. Then $$\mathcal W_E(A)\ \subset\ \operatorname{im}\bigl(\operatorname{CH}^3(A)_\mathbb Q \longrightarrow H^6(A,\mathbb Q)\bigr).$$ The assertion holds at every compatible period, including nonsimple varieties and periods with additional endomorphisms.
  - Explanation: On a sixfold with a unital quadratic action and a hyperbolic split-signature compatible polarization, the rational Weil plane consists of algebraic classes at every compatible period.
- **claim-hyperbolicity-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3ab-hyperbolic)): Let $E$ be imaginary quadratic. A nondegenerate $E$-Hermitian form of rank $2b$ and signature $(b,b)$, $b\geq1$, is hyperbolic if and only if $$[\det h]=[(-1)^b].$$ Every hyperbolic form of this rank is isometric to the sum of $b$ planes with matrix $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$.
  - Explanation: A nondegenerate Hermitian form of split signature is hyperbolic exactly when its determinant class is the expected class, and then it is a sum of standard hyperbolic planes.
- **claim-quadratic-determinant-supply** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3ab-determinant-supply)): Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety with an action of an imaginary quadratic field $E$, and let a compatible rational Riemann form have associated Hermitian form of signature $(p,q)$. Put $n=\dim A=p+q$. Both lines $$D\bigl(H^1(A,k)_\sigma\bigr),\qquad D\bigl(H^1(A,k)_{\bar\sigma}\bigr)$$ are CM-supplied in either of the following cases:* 1. *$n=6$, $(p,q)=(3,3)$, and the form is hyperbolic;* 2. *$n<6$ and $p,q\leq3$.* *The second assertion permits every compatible rational Riemann form. Both assertions hold at every period.
  - Explanation: The top exterior lines of the two eigenspaces are CM-supplied for hyperbolic split sixfolds and for lower-dimensional varieties after padding with CM elliptic curves.
- **claim-albert-columns-pairings** (classification, [source](#stmt-p3ab-albert-columns)): In Types I–III let $F=Z(D)$ be totally real of degree $f$. In Type IV let the CM center $K=Z(D)$ have degree $2f$, and write $[D:K]=b^2$. The columns in <a href="#p3ab:eq:columns" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3ab:eq:columns">[p3ab:eq:columns]</a> have the following data: $$\begin{array}{c\|c\|c\|c} \text{Albert type}&\text{necessary divisibility}&\dim_k W& \text{pair tensor}\\ \hline \mathrm I& f\mid g&2g/f&\text{alternating on }W\\ \mathrm{II}&2f\mid g&g/f&\text{alternating on }W\\ \mathrm{III}&2f\mid g&g/f&\text{symmetric on }W\\ \mathrm{IV}&fb^2\mid g&g/(fb)&\text{between opposite columns}. \end{array}$$ The listed tensors are pair tensors in the sense of Section <a href="#p3:sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:tensors">2</a>, and all copy identifications are admissible. In Type IV the opposite column is the dual $R_B$-module.
  - Explanation: The columns of first cohomology for a simple non-CM factor carry the pairing symmetry dictated by the Albert type, with opposite columns paired in the fourth type.
- **claim-two-weights-minuscule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3ab-minuscule)): In the irreducible tensor decomposition of a column $W_\tau$ for the simple ideals of $\operatorname{Lie}(R_B)_k$, every nontrivial simple factor representation is minuscule.
  - Explanation: Because the Hodge circle has only two weights, every nontrivial simple factor representation in a column is minuscule.
- **claim-small-derived-projections** (classification, [source](#stmt-p3ab-small-projections)): An alternating self-paired column of dimension $2,4$, or $6$ has the full symplectic derived image. A symmetric self-paired column cannot have dimension two; in dimensions four and six its derived image is, respectively, the full $\operatorname{SO}_4$ and $\operatorname{SO}_6$.* *If $D=K$ is an imaginary quadratic field, $B$ is non-CM, and $\dim_K H_B\leq6$, then the derived image on either embedding column is the full special linear group, with dual action on the opposite column.
  - Explanation: Self-paired columns of small dimension have the full symplectic or orthogonal derived image, and non-CM imaginary quadratic columns have the full special linear image with dual opposite column.
- **claim-small-column-list** (classification, [source](#stmt-p3ab-small-columns)): Let $A$ have an imaginary-quadratic $E$-action and $\dim A\leq6$. Write $A\sim\prod_B B^{r_B}$ with pairwise nonisogenous simple factors, and discard the CM factors. Put $g=\dim B$. After identifying matrix copies, the remaining columns have the following possibilities.* 1. *In Types I and II they have full symplectic derived images and dimensions $2,4,6$.* 2. *In Type III, $g=4$ or $6$, the center is $\mathbb Q$, and $r_B=1$. The unique column has full $\operatorname{SO}_g$ image. Each eigenspace of the given $E$-action is one copy of this column and has equal Hodge multiplicities $g/2,g/2$.* 3. *In Type IV, $D=K$ is a CM field. If $E$ admits no $\mathbb Q$-embedding into $K$, then $K$ is imaginary quadratic, $g=3$, and $r_B=2$; the $K$-columns have the full $\operatorname{SL}_3$ and dual images.* *If $E$ admits a $\mathbb Q$-embedding into $K$, put $[K:\mathbb Q]=2f$ and $a=g/f$. The possibilities are $$f=1,\quad 3\leq a=g\leq6; \qquad (f,a,g)=(2,2,4),(2,3,6),(3,2,6).$$ Every column has the full $\operatorname{SL}_a$ image, paired with the dual at the opposite embedding. In the cases with $a=2$, some embedding pairs are mixed and some are pure.* *All these are projections of the actual derived Hodge group of $A$.
  - Explanation: For varieties of small dimension with an imaginary quadratic action, the non-CM columns fall in a short explicit list of Albert types with full classical derived images.
- **claim-independent-derived-images** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3ab-independence)): In Lemma <a href="#p3ab:small-columns" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3ab:small-columns">15</a>, the listed symplectic, orthogonal, and special linear derived images are independent, after identifying matrix copies and pairing opposite CM embeddings. In other words, they satisfy assumption <span class="nodecor">(c)</span> of Theorem <a href="#p3:tensor-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:tensor-criterion">7</a>.
  - Explanation: The derived images on the listed columns act independently, so the derived Hodge group contains the full product required by the determinant criterion.
- **claim-simultaneous-abel-seed** (construction, [source](#stmt-p3sw-seed)): For every integer $d\geq3$, put $n=d+1$. There exist a smooth nonhyperelliptic genus-three curve $C$, a point $p\in C$, and cyclic subgroups $G_1,G_2\subset X=\operatorname{Pic}^2(C)$ of order $n$, with $G_1\cap G_2=0$, as follows. There are cosets $\{s_1,\ldots,s_n\}=s_0+G_2$ and $\{t_1,\ldots,t_n\}=t_0+G_1$ such that $$C_i=t_{s_i}C_p,\qquad \Sigma_j=t_{t_j}\Sigma_p,\qquad A=\coprod_{i=1}^n C_i,\qquad \Sigma=\coprod_{j=1}^n\Sigma_j \label{p3sw:support-seed-orbits}$$ are disjoint within each of the two indicated unions, and $$H^0(X,I_A\otimes H^{\otimes2}\otimes N) = H^0(X,I_\Sigma\otimes H^{\otimes2}\otimes N)=0 \quad\text{for every }N\in\widehat X. \label{p3sw:support-uniform-vanishing}$$
  - Explanation: There is a genus-three Jacobian with two disjoint Abel curve orbits under cyclic groups whose ideals have no sections after any degree-zero twist of twice the polarization.
- **claim-trilinear-singular-marks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-support-trilinear)): Let $V_1,V_2,V_3$ be two-dimensional vector spaces and let $0\ne T\in V_1\otimes V_2\otimes V_3$. Suppose a finite indexed collection $$(x_h,y_h,z_h)\in \mathbb P(V_1^*)\times\mathbb P(V_2^*)\times\mathbb P(V_3^*)$$ has at most two indices in every fiber of each coordinate projection. At most three indices satisfy all three equations $$T(-,y_h,z_h)=T(x_h,-,z_h)=T(x_h,y_h,-)=0. \label{p3sw:support-singular-marks}$$ Indices, rather than distinct triples, are counted.
  - Explanation: A nonzero trilinear form on two-dimensional spaces has only a bounded number of marked points where all partial contractions vanish.
- **claim-elliptic-star-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-support-elliptic-star)): Let $J_0=E_1\times E_2\times E_3$ be a product of elliptic curves with origins, and let $Z_0$ be the reduced union of the three coordinate elliptic axes through zero. Let $K\subset J_0$ be a finite subgroup of order at least four whose projection to every $E_i$ is injective. The translates $Z(g)=g+Z_0$, $g\in K$, are pairwise disjoint. For line bundles $D_i$ of degree two on $E_i$, $$H^0\left(J_0,I_{\bigcup_{g\in K}Z(g)} \otimes(D_1\boxtimes D_2\boxtimes D_3)\right)=0. \label{p3sw:support-star-vanishing}$$ The same vanishing holds for any closed subscheme with that support.
  - Explanation: Sections of degree-two box products vanishing along enough disjoint translates of the three-axis star must be zero.
- **claim-finite-model-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-support-finite-model)): Let $B$ be a regular Noetherian scheme of finite dimension, let $f:V\to B$ be projective, and let $\mathcal F$ be a coherent sheaf flat over $B$. Then $Rf_*\mathcal F$ is locally represented by a bounded complex $K^\bullet$ of finite-rank locally free sheaves. For every morphism $h:T\to B$ there is a derived base-change isomorphism $$Lh^*Rf_*\mathcal F \simeq Rf_{T,*}(\mathcal F\|_{V\times_B T}), \label{p3sw:support-derived-base-change}$$ where the restriction on the right is ordinary, and the left side is represented locally by $K^\bullet\otimes\mathcal O_T$. The functions $b\mapsto\dim_{\kappa(b)}H^i(V_b,\mathcal F_b)$ are upper semicontinuous. If all derived fibers have cohomology in degrees $[c,e]$, the local model can be chosen in precisely those degrees.
  - Explanation: Pushforward of a flat coherent sheaf has a local finite free model compatible with arbitrary base change, and fiber cohomology dimensions are upper semicontinuous.
- **claim-local-tor-product-ideals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-support-local-tor)): Let $V$ be a smooth threefold and let $D_1,D_2\subset V$ be smooth curves with finite, possibly empty, intersection. Then $$\operatorname{Tor}^{\mathcal O_V}_k(I_{D_1},I_{D_2})=0 \quad(k>0), \qquad I_{D_1}\otimes I_{D_2}\ \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\ \mathcal O_V \label{p3sw:support-tor-product}$$ by multiplication, with image $I_{D_1}I_{D_2}$. The quotient from $\mathcal O_V/I_{D_1}I_{D_2}$ to the structure sheaf of the reduced union has zero-dimensional kernel. The same statements hold when each $D_i$ is a disjoint union of smooth curves.
  - Explanation: For smooth curves meeting in finitely many points on a smooth threefold, higher Tor of the ideals vanishes and the tensor product of ideals is the product ideal.
- **claim-relative-kernel-flat** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-relative-kernel)): For the seed of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:seed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:seed">17</a>, the derived tensor in <a href="#p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf">[p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf]</a> is the ordinary coherent sheaf $\mathscr I\mathscr J\subset\mathcal O_{X\times M}$. Both this ideal and its quotient in $\mathcal O_{X\times M}$ are flat over $M$. Ideal multiplication and both ideal sequences commute with every base change $T\to M$. In particular, $\mathscr F$ is $M$-flat, and at $m=(x,N)$ it has the ordinary fiber $$\begin{split} \mathscr F_m&=I_{Z_x}\otimes\mathcal M_{x,N},\\ I_{Z_x}&=I_A I_{\Sigma_x},\qquad \Sigma_x=\coprod_jt_{-x}\Sigma_j,\qquad \mathcal M_{x,N}=H^{\otimes2}\otimes a(-x)\otimes N^{-1}. \end{split} \label{p3sw:support-fiber-formula}$$ Moreover $G=R\pi_*\mathscr F$ has a local finite locally free model, and for every $h:T\to M$, $$Lh^*G\simeq R\pi_{T,*}(\mathscr F\|_{X\times T}). \label{p3sw:support-kernel-base-change}$$
  - Explanation: For the seed, the derived tensor kernel is an ordinary flat ideal sheaf and its derived pushforward commutes with every base change.
- **claim-coherent-dual-sheaf** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-coherent-sheaf)): Let $D_M(-)=R\mathcal Hom_M(-,\mathcal O_M)$. Every derived fiber of $G$ has cohomology only in degrees one and two, and its degree-two cohomology vanishes on a nonempty open set. Consequently $$E=D_M(G)[-1] \label{p3sw:support-dual-sheaf}$$ is an actual coherent perfect sheaf on $M$, locally with a length-one locally free resolution, of rank $$r=8d>0. \label{p3sw:support-rank}$$ No reflexivity or codimension assertion is required for this claim.
  - Explanation: The shifted derived dual of the transform is an honest coherent perfect sheaf of positive rank.
- **claim-coherent-translation-action** (construction, [source](#stmt-p3sw-coherent-action)): For $g=(g_1,g_2)\in G_1\times G_2$, put $$\begin{split} b_g&=(g_1-g_2,\ a(-g_1-g_2))\in M,\\ N_g&=\operatorname{pr}_X^*a(-g_1) \otimes\operatorname{pr}_{\widehat X}^*\mathcal P_{-g_2}, \qquad \mathcal P_v=\mathcal P\|_{\{v\}\times\widehat X}. \end{split} \label{p3sw:support-conjugated-action}$$ The conjugation of the source translations by $\widetilde\Phi$ has explicit functors $$T_g(-)=N_g\otimes t_{b_g,*}(-). \label{p3sw:support-action-functors}$$ There are unital associative line isomorphisms $$N_g\otimes t_{b_g,*}N_h\simeq N_{g+h} \label{p3sw:support-line-compositors}$$ and compatible isomorphisms $T_gG\simeq G$. Perfect duality gives actual sheaf isomorphisms $$N_g^{-1}\otimes t_{b_g,*}E\simeq E \label{p3sw:support-dual-action}$$ with the corresponding unital associative line compositors. The homomorphism $g\mapsto b_g$ is injective. Its image $\bar G$ has order $n^2$, acts freely by translations, and $$q:M\longrightarrow Y_0=M/\bar G \label{p3sw:support-quotient}$$ is a finite étale isogeny of abelian sixfolds.
  - Explanation: The sheaf carries a coherent line-twisted translation action of a finite group that acts freely, giving a finite etale isogeny quotient.
- **claim-hochschild-atiyah-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle)): Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety of dimension $d$, and let $Q\in\operatorname{Perf}(A)$. Use the raw first-jet class $a_Q$ from <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension">[p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension]</a>, and the left-interior and HKR conventions <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:left-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:left-action">[p3sw:hh-eq:left-action]</a> and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr">[p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr]</a>. Define $$\operatorname{ob}_Q=\chi_Q\circ\operatorname{HKR}_A: HT^2(A)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q).$$ For $\alpha=\sum_{a+b=2}\alpha^{a,b}$, this evaluation is $$\operatorname{ob}_Q(\alpha) =\sum_{a+b=2}\alpha^{a,b}\mathbin{\lrcorner} \frac{a_Q^b}{b!}.$$ On the full group $\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q)$, define $$\begin{split} \sigma_Q&=(\sigma_{Q,j})_{0\leq j\leq d-2}: \operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q) \longrightarrow H\Omega_{-2}(A) =\bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}H^{j+2}(A,\Omega_A^j),\\ \sigma_{Q,j}(v)&= \operatorname{Tr}_{Q,\Omega_A^j[j+2]} \left(\left(\frac{a_Q^j}{j!}\right)[2]\circ v\right). \end{split} \label{p3sw:hh-eq:sigma}$$ The direct sum is zero if $d<2$. Set $$c_Q(\alpha)=\alpha\mathbin{\lrcorner}\operatorname{ch}_H(Q) \in H\Omega_{-2}(A).$$ Then $$\sigma_Q\circ\operatorname{ob}_Q=c_Q. \label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle}$$ More precisely, with $\operatorname{ch}_{H,k}(Q)=0$ for $k\notin[0,d]$, each component is $$\sigma_{Q,j}(\operatorname{ob}_Q(\alpha)) =\sum_{a+b=2}\alpha^{a,b}\mathbin{\lrcorner} \operatorname{ch}_{H,j+b}(Q) \quad\text{in }H^{j+2}(A,\Omega_A^j). \label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components}$$
  - Explanation: Contraction with the Hodge Chern character factors as the semiregularity map composed with obstruction evaluation.
- **claim-hh-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-hh-transport)): For the fixed enhanced Fourier–Mukai equivalence $\Phi:\operatorname{Perf}(A)\to\operatorname{Perf}(B)$, there exists an isomorphism $$\Phi^H_{HH}:H\Omega_{-2}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}H\Omega_{-2}(B)$$ depending only on that enhanced equivalence, not on a choice of perfect complex, such that for every $Q\in\operatorname{Perf}(A)$, $$\begin{split} \Phi_{\mathrm{Ext}}\circ\operatorname{ob}_Q &=\operatorname{ob}_{\Phi Q}\circ\Phi^{HT},\\ \Phi^H_{HH}\circ c_Q&=c_{\Phi Q}\circ\Phi^{HT}. \end{split} \label{p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares}$$ Here $\operatorname{ob}$ and $c$ have the conventions of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>, and $\Phi_{\mathrm{Ext}}$ is the induced enhanced map on degree-two Ext. Consequently $$\begin{split} \Phi^{HT}(\ker\operatorname{ob}_Q) &=\ker\operatorname{ob}_{\Phi Q},\\ \Phi^{HT}(\ker c_Q)&=\ker c_{\Phi Q}. \end{split} \label{p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport}$$ In particular $\ker\operatorname{ob}_Q=\ker c_Q$ implies $\ker\operatorname{ob}_{\Phi Q}=\ker c_{\Phi Q}$.
  - Explanation: An enhanced Fourier-Mukai equivalence transports evaluation and contraction compatibly, preserving the equality of their kernels.
- **claim-abel-curve-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-abel-character)): Let $D\subset X$ be an Abel curve. Its normalized Betti class and Chern character are $$[D]_{\mathrm B}=\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^2}{2}, \qquad \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(\mathcal O_D) =\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^2}{2} -\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^3}{3}.$$ Normal restriction of translation-invariant vector fields is an isomorphism $$H^0(X,T_X)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^0(D,N_{D/X});$$ both spaces have dimension three. If $Z=\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ is a disjoint union of $n$ Abel curves, then its ideal satisfies the full normalized Betti identity $$\label{p3sw:invariant-betti-character} \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(I_Z) =1-\frac n2\theta_{\mathrm B}^2+\frac n3\theta_{\mathrm B}^3.$$
  - Explanation: An Abel curve has the stated Betti class and character, and global normal sections are the translation-invariant vector fields.
- **claim-orbit-ext-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext)): Let $\Gamma\subset X$ be a finite subgroup of order $n$, and let $Z=\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ be a disjoint union of Abel curves permuted transitively by $\Gamma$. Give $I_Z$ its canonical linearization. There is an isomorphism of $\Gamma$-representations $$\label{p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation} \operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z) \simeq H^1(X,\mathcal O_X) \oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^n H^0(X,T_X).$$ The first summand is trivial, and $\Gamma$ acts on the second by its regular permutation action on the components and the identity on each translation space. In particular, $$\dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\Gamma=6,\qquad \dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{Ext}^2_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\Gamma=6,\qquad \operatorname{Hom}_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\Gamma=\mathbb C.$$
  - Explanation: The first derived Ext of an orbit ideal splits equivariantly, and the invariant parts of the first and second Ext groups have equal size.
- **claim-invariant-ext-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-ext)): For the source object $\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A$ and its canonical $G_{\mathrm{src}}=G_1\times G_2$ linearization, $$\dim_{\mathbb C} \operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)^{G_{\mathrm{src}}} =6+36+6=48.$$ This is the invariant subspace of the full derived Ext group.
  - Explanation: The invariant second derived Ext of the product ideal has dimension given by the sum of three Kunneth contributions.
- **claim-one-factor-contraction-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-one-factor-rank)): For either orbit ideal $I_Z$, the map $c_{I_Z}^1$ is injective of rank six, and $c_{I_Z}^2$ is surjective of rank six.
  - Explanation: On one orbit ideal, degree-one contraction is injective and degree-two contraction is surjective, both of the same rank.
- **claim-product-contraction-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-product-rank)): For $\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A$, the map $$c_{\mathcal I}^2:HT^2(X^2)\longrightarrow H\Omega_{-2}(X^2)$$ has rank $48$.
  - Explanation: Contraction on the product ideal has rank equal to the sum of three non-cancelling Kunneth images.
- **claim-invariant-injectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-injectivity)): For the source $\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A$ with its canonical $G_{\mathrm{src}}=G_1\times G_2$-linearization, $$\label{p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion} \operatorname{im}(o_{\mathcal I}) =\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)^{G_{\mathrm{src}}}, \qquad \ker(o_{\mathcal I})=\ker(c_{\mathcal I}^2),$$ and $\sigma_{\mathcal I}$ is injective on this full invariant group.* *Let $M=X\times\widehat X$ and use the fixed enhanced equivalence $$\widetilde\Phi=\Phi\circ\bigl((H\boxtimes H)\otimes-\bigr), \qquad \mathcal G=\widetilde\Phi(\mathcal I)[-3].$$ Give $\mathcal G$ the coherent line-translation action from Proposition <a href="#p3sw:coherent-action" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:coherent-action">24</a>, and denote its underlying translation group by $\bar G\simeq G_{\mathrm{src}}$. Then $$\label{p3sw:invariant-target-conclusion} \operatorname{im}(o_{\mathcal G}) =\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G}, \qquad \ker(o_{\mathcal G})=\ker(c_{\mathcal G}^2),$$ and $$\sigma_{\mathcal G}\big\|_{ \operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G}} \quad\text{is injective}.$$
  - Explanation: Evaluation surjects onto the full invariant Ext group and the trace map is injective on it, for the source and for its transform.
- **claim-fourier-matrix** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-fourier-matrix)): For $P\subset\{1,\ldots,\ell_X\}$, $k=\|P\|$, one has $$F_-(e_P)=(-1)^{k(k+3)/2}\epsilon_{P,P^c}f_{P^c}.$$ In particular $F_-$ is a rational isomorphism preserving parity.
  - Explanation: The fixed cohomological Fourier transform sends each basis monomial to a signed complementary monomial and is a parity-preserving rational isomorphism.
- **claim-clifford-spin-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-spin-identity)): For every algebraic spin element $g$, one has the exact equality of rational representations $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:identity} \rho'_g=e^{p/2}\Lambda\rho(g)e^{-p/2} =e^{(p-\Lambda^2\rho(g)p)/2}\Lambda\rho(g).$$ The exponentials denote exterior multiplication. In particular $$[\rho'_g(1)]_0=1,\qquad [\rho'_g(1)]_2=\tfrac12\bigl(p-\Lambda^2\rho(g)p\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The transported diagonal spin action equals the exterior exponential conjugate of the standard exterior action.
- **claim-polarized-quadratic-seed** (construction, [source](#stmt-p3sw-polarized-seed)): On the actual sixfold $M=X\times\widehat X$, define $$\eta(u)(x,N)=\bigl(a^{-1}(N),[-d]a(x)\bigr) =\bigl(-\lambda^{-1}(N),[d]\lambda(x)\bigr).$$ This gives a unital $K$-action. The ample line $$\mathscr L_M=\operatorname{pr}_X^*H^{\otimes d} \otimes\operatorname{pr}_{\widehat X}^*(a^{-1})^*H$$ has a class $\theta_M=c_{1,\mathrm B}(\mathscr L_M)$ fixed by the vector images of $J$ and $\widetilde L_K$. The action is of Weil type $(3,3)$, is compatible with this polarization, and its actual homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic.* *More precisely, the pullback $f=\eta(u)^*$ on $V=L\oplus U$ and its two eigenspaces are $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:oriented-action} \begin{split} f(w,y)&=(d\vartheta y,-\vartheta^{-1}w),\qquad f^2=-d,\\ W_+&=\operatorname{Ann}(\lambda_+) =\{(-u\vartheta y,y):y\in U_K\},\\ W_-&=\operatorname{Ann}(\lambda_-) =\{(u\vartheta y,y):y\in U_K\}. \end{split}$$ The eigenvalue on $W_+$ is $+u$, and that on $W_-$ is $-u$.
  - Explanation: The product of the Jacobian and its dual carries an explicit unital quadratic action compatible with an ample polarization whose homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic of split signature.
- **claim-ordered-spinor-stabilizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-ordered-stabilizer)): Let $W=W_+$. Under $V_K=W\oplus W^*$, the ordered line stabilizer and the pointwise stabilizer are $$\widetilde L_K =\{(g,t)\in GL(W)\times\mathbb G_m:t^2=\det g\},\qquad m(g,t)=t^{-1}\Lambda g,\qquad J_K=\{(g,1):g\in SL(W)\}.$$ The characters on $\lambda_+$ and $\lambda_-$ are $t,t^{-1}$. For $0\leq j\leq6$, $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:invariants} H^{2j}(M,\mathbb Q)^J= \begin{cases} \mathbb Q\theta_M^j,&j\ne3,\\ \mathbb Q\theta_M^3\oplus\mathcal W_K(M),&j=3, \end{cases} \qquad H^{\mathrm{odd}}(M,\mathbb Q)^J=0 .$$ The ordered line stabilizer fixes every $\theta_M^j$ and acts on the two scalar Weil lines by $t^2,t^{-2}$.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer invariants are powers of the polarization class, plus the Weil plane in the middle degree.
- **claim-exceptional-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-exceptional-character)): The normalized character $\kappa_M$ in <a href="#p3sw:spin-eq:normalized-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:spin-eq:normalized-character">[p3sw:spin-eq:normalized-character]</a> is a rational $J$-invariant even class, and its degree-six component has a nonzero rational Weil component: $$(\kappa_M)_3=a_M\theta_M^3+w_M,\qquad a_M\in\mathbb Q,\quad 0\ne w_M\in\mathcal W_K(M).$$
  - Explanation: The normalized character of the actual sheaf has a nonzero Weil component in the middle degree.
- **claim-hodge-circle-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-hodge-circle)): Keep the rational homology of the seed $M$, the action $\eta$, and the Riemann form of $\mathscr L_M$ fixed. At every compatible marked complex structure of Weil type $(3,3)$, every component $(\kappa_M)_j$ has Hodge type $(j,j)$.
  - Explanation: Every component of the normalized character has balanced Hodge type at each compatible marked period of the split type.
- **claim-descent-duality-sign** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-descent-duality)): Even transpose induces an isomorphism $$d_2:\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G} \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \operatorname{Ext}^2_M(E,E)^{\bar G}.$$ With the convention <a href="#p3sw:descent-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:descent-character">[p3sw:descent-character]</a>, its components obey $$\label{p3sw:descent-dual-sign} \sigma_{E,j}(d_2v)=(-1)^{j+1}\sigma_{\mathcal G,j}(v) \quad\text{for every }j\geq0.$$ Consequently, injectivity of $\sigma_{\mathcal G}$ on the full $\bar G$-invariant $\operatorname{Ext}^2$ implies injectivity of $\sigma_E$ on the full $\bar G$-invariant $\operatorname{Ext}^2$.
  - Explanation: Even transpose gives an invariant isomorphism of second Ext groups, changing semiregularity components by an explicit sign, so injectivity transfers.
- **claim-twisted-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-p3sw-twisted-descent)): There are a class $\alpha\in H^2(Y_0,\mu_r)$, a coherent $\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\mathcal B$ of rank $r$ with $\det\mathcal B\simeq\mathcal O_{Y_0}$, and an invertible $q^*\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\mathcal L$ on $M$, such that $$\label{p3sw:descent-twisted-pullback} q^*\mathcal B\simeq E\otimes\mathcal L.$$ Let $\mathfrak Y_0$ be the $\mu_r$-gerbe of $\alpha$. For every integer $i$, this isomorphism induces $$\label{p3sw:descent-ext} \operatorname{Ext}^i_{\mathfrak Y_0}(\mathcal B,\mathcal B) \simeq\operatorname{Ext}^i_M(E,E)^{\bar G},\qquad \operatorname{Ext}^i_0(\mathcal B,\mathcal B) \simeq\operatorname{Ext}^i_0(E,E)^{\bar G},$$ where the subscript $0$ denotes the kernel of the perfect trace to $H^i(\mathcal O)$. Put $$\lambda_H=\frac{a_{\det E}}r=\frac{\operatorname{tr}(a_E)}r.$$ Under the first isomorphism $\phi$ in degree two, the raw Hodge square $$\label{p3sw:descent-square} \begin{array}{ccc} \operatorname{Ext}^2(\mathcal B,\mathcal B)& \xrightarrow{\ \phi\ }&\operatorname{Ext}^2(E,E)^{\bar G}\\ \big\downarrow{\scriptstyle\sigma_{\mathcal B}}&& \big\downarrow{\scriptstyle e^{-\lambda_H}\sigma_E}\\ \mathcal H_{-2}(Y_0)&\xrightarrow{\ q^*\ }&\mathcal H_{-2}(M) \end{array}$$ commutes, both on full Ext and on its trace-free summand. In particular, full invariant injectivity for $E$ makes $\mathcal B$ semiregular. By Lemma <a href="#p3sw:descent-duality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:descent-duality">39</a>, full invariant injectivity for $\mathcal G$ is sufficient for this conclusion.
  - Explanation: The sheaf descends along the isogeny to a determinant-trivial twisted sheaf whose full Ext groups match the invariant ones upstairs, and it is semiregular.
- **claim-ordinary-conversion** (construction, [source](#stmt-p3sw-ordinary-conversion)): For the class $\alpha$ and the semiregular twisted coherent sheaf $\mathcal B$ in Proposition <a href="#p3sw:twisted-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:twisted-descent">40</a>, put $m=r^{12}$ and $N=6+m-1$. There exist a holomorphic $\mathbb P^{m-1}$-bundle $p:Z\to Y$, a determinant-trivial $\alpha$-twisted vector bundle $Q_0$ of rank $m$ on $Y_0$, and an inverse-twisted tautological line $\ell_0=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(Q_0)}(1)$, with $Z_0=\mathbb P(Q_0)$. The ordinary line $$\label{p3sw:descent-anticanonical} H=\omega_{Z/Y}^{-1}=\det T_{Z/Y}$$ satisfies $H\|_{Z_0}\simeq\ell_0^m$ and restricts to $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^{m-1}}(m)$ on every $p$-fiber. The normalized Betti class and raw Hodge class $$\label{p3sw:descent-zeta} \zeta_{B,s}=\frac{c_1^B(H\|_{Z_s})}{m},\qquad \zeta_{H,s}=\frac{a_{H\|_{Z_s}}}{m},\qquad \iota_{Z_s}(\zeta_{H,s})=(2\pi i)\zeta_{B,s}$$ have the following properties: $\zeta_B$ is a horizontal rational class of type $(1,1)$, and its restriction to every $p_s$-fiber is the normalized hyperplane class. Every $Z_s$ and $p_s$ is projective algebraic, and $a\circ p$ has a relative ample line.* *The sheaf $$F_0=p_0^*\mathcal B\otimes\ell_0$$ is ordinary coherent on $Z_0$, and it is semiregular. More precisely, there is a full derived isomorphism $$\label{p3sw:descent-ordinary-ext} \operatorname{Ext}^2_{Z_0}(F_0,F_0) \simeq\operatorname{Ext}^2_{\mathfrak Y_0}(\mathcal B,\mathcal B)$$ under which $$\label{p3sw:descent-ordinary-sigma} \sigma_{F_0}(p_0^*v) =e^{\zeta_{H,0}}p_0^*\sigma_{\mathcal B}(v).$$ If a rational horizontal section $\kappa_B=\sum_{k=0}^6\kappa_{B,k}$ specializes to $\operatorname{ch}_B(\mathcal B)$ and has type $(k,k)$ in degree $2k$, then $$\label{p3sw:descent-horizontal-character} A_B=p^*\kappa_B e^{\zeta_B}=\sum_{k=0}^{N}A_{B,k}$$ is rational, horizontal, and of type $(k,k)$ in degree $2k$. Its raw Hodge section in that degree is the edge of $(2\pi i)^kA_{B,k}$, and its central value is $\operatorname{ch}_{H,k}(F_0)$. Finally, on every fiber, $$\label{p3sw:descent-extraction} p_{s*}^B\bigl(\zeta_{B,s}^{\,m-1}e^{-\zeta_{B,s}}A_B(s)\bigr) =\kappa_B(s),$$ where $p_{s*}^B$ is normalized Betti fiber integration.
  - Explanation: A projective bundle cancels the twist, giving an ordinary semiregular coherent sheaf and a rational extraction identity for the character.
- **claim-projective-germ-deformation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-projective-germ)): Let $S=(S,0)$ be a smooth complex analytic germ, and let $\pi:\mathcal X\to S$ be a proper holomorphic submersion of relative dimension $n$, equipped with a holomorphic $\pi$-ample line $\mathcal L_{\mathcal X}$. Let $F_0$ be an ordinary coherent sheaf on $X_0$. For $I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$, suppose the following raw semiregularity map is injective: $$\label{p3sw:bf-seed-sigma} \begin{split} \sigma_{I,0}:\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X_0}(F_0,F_0)&\longrightarrow \bigoplus_{k\in I}H^{k+1}(X_0,\Omega_{X_0}^{k-1}),\\ v&\longmapsto \left(\operatorname{Tr}_{F_0,\Omega_{X_0}^{k-1}[k+1]} \left(\left(\frac{a_{F_0}^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\right)[2]\circ v\right)\right)_{k\in I} \end{split}$$ Suppose there are rational flat sections $\beta_{B,k}$ of $R^{2k}\pi_*\mathbb Q$, for $k\in I$, such that $$\beta_{B,k}(0)=\operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F_0),\qquad \beta_{B,k}\in F^k(R^{2k}\pi_*\mathbb C\otimes\mathcal O_S).$$ The second condition means that the flat de Rham class itself lies in the indicated Hodge filtration. Then, after shrinking $S$, there is a coherent $S$-flat sheaf $F$ on $\mathcal X$, with $F\|_{X_0}\simeq F_0$, for which $$\label{p3sw:bf-fiber-character} \operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F\|_{X_s})=\beta_{B,k}(s) \quad(s\text{ near }0,\ k\in I).$$ These classes are rational algebraic cycle classes. No rank, simplicity, stability, local-freeness, reflexivity, or fixed-support hypothesis is imposed on $F_0$.
  - Explanation: A semiregular coherent sheaf on a smooth projective germ deforms to a flat sheaf realizing prescribed flat Hodge-filtered characters, which are then algebraic.
- **claim-bf-coefficient-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-bf-coefficients)): Let $A=A_\nu$, $X=X_\nu$, and let $E$ be an $A$-flat coherent sheaf on $X$ whose central fiber is $F_0$. Then $E$ is absolutely perfect as an $\mathcal O_X$-complex, with local projective dimension at most $n$. If $W$ is a finite $A$-module killed by the maximal ideal and $W_X=\mathcal O_X\otimes_AW$, there are natural identifications $$\label{p3sw:bf-coefficient-identities} \begin{split} \operatorname{Ext}^2_X(E,E\otimes_AW) &\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X_0}(F_0,F_0)\otimes_\mathbb CW,\\ H^q(X,\Omega^j_{X/A}\otimes_AW) &\simeq H^q(X_0,\Omega_{X_0}^j)\otimes_\mathbb CW. \end{split}$$ Under them the coefficient semiregularity map $$\sigma_{I,W}(v)= \left(\operatorname{Tr}_{E,(\Omega_{X/A}^{k-1}\otimes_AW)[k+1]} \left(\left(\frac{a_{X/A}(E)^{k-1}}{(k-1)!} \otimes_A^{\mathbf L}1_W\right)[2]\circ v\right)\right)_{k\in I}$$ is exactly $\sigma_{I,0}\otimes1_W$, and is therefore injective.
  - Explanation: A flat sheaf over an Artinian base is absolutely perfect and its coefficient obstruction groups are tensor extensions of the central ones.
- **claim-bf-one-step-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-bf-one-step)): Let $E$ be an $A$-flat coherent sheaf on $X$ with central fiber $F_0$. Suppose, for every $k\in I$, that its raw relative character $\operatorname{ch}^{\rm rel}_{H,k}(E) =\operatorname{tr}(a_{X/A}(E)^k)/k!$ is the restriction of the Hodge edge of a flat $F^k$ de Rham class on $X'/A'$. Then $E$ has a coherent $A'$-flat sheaf lift on $X'$, with its prescribed restriction to $X$.
  - Explanation: When the relative character comes from a flat filtered class, the sheaf lifts across a square-zero extension.
- **claim-artin-lift-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-bf-artin-character)): For every $A_\nu$-flat coherent sheaf $E_\nu$ on $X_\nu$ with central fiber $F_0$, and every $0\leq k\leq n$, the ordinary flat comparison $$H^l_{\rm dR}(X_\nu/A_\nu) \simeq H^l(X_0,\mathbb C)\otimes_\mathbb CA_\nu$$ satisfies $$\label{p3sw:bf-artin-character-formula} \begin{split} \operatorname{ch}^{\rm rel}_{H,k}(E_\nu) &=\operatorname{edge}_k\bigl((2\pi i)^k \operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F_0)\otimes1\bigr),\\ (2\pi i)^k\operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F_0)\otimes1 &\in F^kH^{2k}_{\rm dR}(X_\nu/A_\nu). \end{split}$$ The assertion holds independently of the choice of the lift.
  - Explanation: Every infinitesimal flat lift has relative character equal to the edge of the flat transport of the central Betti character.
- **claim-local-algebraicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-local-algebraicity)): There is a nonempty analytic neighborhood of the seed in $\mathscr D$ on which every component $\kappa_{B,j}(I)$ is a rational algebraic cycle class on $Y_I$.
  - Explanation: Near the seed in the marked compatible domain, every component of the descended character is a rational algebraic class.
- **claim-two-generators-weil-plane** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-endpoint-two-generators)): Let $A$ be an abelian sixfold with a unital action of the imaginary quadratic field $K$. If a nonzero vector of $\mathcal W_K(A)$ is a rational algebraic class, then every vector of $\mathcal W_K(A)$ is a rational algebraic class.
  - Explanation: A single nonzero algebraic vector of the Weil plane forces the whole plane to be algebraic.
- **claim-analytic-hilbert-spread** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-spread)): Let $\pi:\mathcal X\to S$ be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension $n$ over a smooth quasi-projective complex variety. Let $\nu:T\to S^{\mathrm{an}}$ be holomorphic, where $T$ is a connected complex manifold. Fix $0\leq c\leq n$ and a finite-rank rational local subsystem $$\mathbb V\subset\nu^*R^{2c}\pi^{\mathrm{an}}_*\mathbb Q.$$ If every vector of $\mathbb V_t$ is a rational algebraic codimension-$c$ class on $\mathcal X_{\nu(t)}$ for all $t$ in a nonempty analytic open subset of $T$, then the same is true for every $t\in T$.
  - Explanation: Algebraicity of a rational local subsystem on a nonempty open subset of a connected base extends to the whole base via Hilbert schemes and a Baire argument.
- **claim-all-hyperbolic-lattices** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p3sw-endpoint-all-lattices)): For the fixed $K=\mathbb Q(u)$ with $u^2=-d$, $d\geq3$, let $A$ be any complex abelian sixfold with a unital $K$-action and a compatible polarization. If its actual homological Hermitian form $h_A(x,y)=e_A(x,uy)+u e_A(x,y)$ is hyperbolic, then $\mathcal W_K(A)$ is a rational algebraic plane.
  - Explanation: For any sixfold with a unital quadratic action and a compatible polarization with hyperbolic Hermitian form, the Weil plane is algebraic.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-split-sixfolds",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3-tensor-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety, $H=H^1(A,\\mathbb Q)$, $J=\\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$, and $R=J^{\\mathrm{der}}$. Suppose the following data exist.*\n\n1.  *A rational Hodge summand $H_0\\subset H$ has toric Hodge group. Choose a decomposition of $H_{0,k}$ into CM embedding lines, with repeated occurrences distinguished, and call this finite list $\\mathcal E_0$.*\n\n2.  *The complement of $H_{0,k}$ is a direct sum of blocks of nonzero admissible spaces. In a symplectic or an orthogonal block, each space is equipped with an admissible isomorphism to a representative $V_i$ chosen as an actual summand of that block, with, respectively, an alternating or a symmetric pair tensor on itself. A linear block has $\\dim V_i\\ge2$, with $V_i$ again an actual summand, and each space is equipped with an admissible isomorphism to either $V_i$ or $\\overline V_i$, paired with each other as in Lemma <a href=\"#p3:admissible-operations\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3:admissible-operations\">4</a>. The pair identifies $\\overline V_i$ with $V_i^*(-1)$ as a Hodge-graded space and with the dual as a group representation.*\n\n3.  *Under these identifications the image of $R_k$ contains $$P=\\prod_{i\\in I_{\\mathrm{Sp}}}\\operatorname{Sp}(V_i)\n       \\times\\prod_{i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SO}}}\\operatorname{SO}(V_i)\n       \\times\\prod_{i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SL}}}\\operatorname{SL}(V_i).$$ Each factor acts by its standard representation on its own copies, by the dual representation on the opposite copies of a linear block, and trivially on every other block and on $H_{0,k}$. The symplectic and orthogonal forms are the inverse pairings.*\n\n\n4.  *Let $W_1,\\ldots,W_s$ be any list, with arbitrary repetitions, from $$\\{V_i:i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SO}}\\}\n     \\ \\cup\\\n     \\{V_i,\\overline V_i:i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SL}}\\}.$$ Whenever there are $\\ell_1,\\ldots,\\ell_r\\in\\mathcal E_0$, again with arbitrary repetitions, such that $$\\label{p3:eq:determinant-balance}\n     \\sum_{j=1}^s\\epsilon_\\gamma(W_j)\n     +\\sum_{a=1}^r\\epsilon_\\gamma(\\ell_a)=0\n     \\quad\\text{for every }\\gamma\\in\\operatorname{Gal}(k/\\mathbb Q),$$ the line $\\bigotimes_{j=1}^sD(W_j)$ is CM-supplied on its separate tensor slots. Empty lists are allowed.*\n\n*Then for every $p\\ge0$ every rational balanced tensor in $H^{\\otimes 2p}$ is the class of a rational algebraic cycle on its separate $A$-slots. The rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension of $A^m$ for every $m\\ge1$.*\n\n*Call the data *saturated* if every singleton $D(V_i)$ for an orthogonal or linear block is CM-supplied, without any balance condition. For saturated data and any CM abelian variety $M$, the induced data on $A\\times M$ are saturated. In particular the same tensor and Hodge-conjecture conclusions hold for every power of $A\\times M$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3-tensor-criterion",

The symplectic and orthogonal forms are the inverse pairings.*\n\n\n4.  *Let $W_1,\\ldots,W_s$ be any list, with arbitrary repetitions, from $$\\{V_i:i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SO}}\\}\n     \\ \\cup\\\n     \\{V_i,\\overline V_i:i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SL}}\\}.$$ Whenever there are $\\ell_1,\\ldots,\\ell_r\\in\\mathcal E_0$, again with arbitrary repetitions, such that $$\\label{p3:eq:determinant-balance}\n     \\sum_{j=1}^s\\epsilon_\\gamma(W_j)\n     +\\sum_{a=1}^r\\epsilon_\\gamma(\\ell_a)=0\n     \\quad\\text{for every }\\gamma\\in\\operatorname{Gal}(k/\\mathbb Q),$$ the line $\\bigotimes_{j=1}^sD(W_j)$ is CM-supplied on its separate tensor slots. For saturated data and any CM abelian variety $M$, the induced data on $A\\times M$ are saturated. In particular the same tensor and Hodge-conjecture conclusions hold for every power of $A\\times M$.",
      "explanation": "If CM lines and classical derived images are available and the balanced determinant products are CM-supplied, then all balanced tensors and all Hodge classes on all powers are algebraic; saturated data stay saturated after adding CM factors.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3-correspondence-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr S$ be a collection of smooth projective varieties such that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of members of $\\mathscr S$. Let $Y_1,\\ldots,Y_v$ be smooth projective varieties. Suppose that, for each $i$ and each degree $q$, $H^q(Y_i,\\mathbb Q)$ is spanned by images of maps $$\\Gamma_*:H^{q-2j}(S,\\mathbb Q)\\longrightarrow H^q(Y_i,\\mathbb Q),\n \\qquad\n \\Gamma\\in\\operatorname{CH}^{\\dim S+j}(S\\times Y_i)_\\mathbb Q,\\quad S\\in\\mathscr S.$$ Then the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of the $Y_i$, with arbitrary repetitions and with any additional members of $\\mathscr S$.*\n\n*The same conclusion holds if the spanning is given over $k$ by restrictions of maps induced by $k$-linear combinations of rational algebraic correspondences, provided their images span the full target degree after scalar extension.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3-correspondence-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr S$ be a collection of smooth projective varieties such that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of members of $\\mathscr S$.           "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathscr S$ be a collection of smooth projective varieties such that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of members of $\\mathscr S$.       "explanation": "If the cohomology of each target variety is spanned by correspondence images from a family closed under the Hodge conjecture on products, the conjecture holds on all finite products of the targets.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-weil-plane",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be an imaginary quadratic field. Let $A$ be a complex abelian sixfold with a unital $E$-action and a compatible polarization whose actual homological Hermitian form $h_E$ is hyperbolic of signature $(3,3)$. Then $$\\mathcal W_E(A)\\ \\subset\\\n \\operatorname{im}\\bigl(\\operatorname{CH}^3(A)_\\mathbb Q\n                    \\longrightarrow H^6(A,\\mathbb Q)\\bigr).$$ The assertion holds at every compatible period, including nonsimple varieties and periods with additional endomorphisms.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-weil-plane",

Then $$\\mathcal W_E(A)\\ \\subset\\\n \\operatorname{im}\\bigl(\\operatorname{CH}^3(A)_\\mathbb Q\n                    \\longrightarrow H^6(A,\\mathbb Q)\\bigr).$$ The assertion holds at every compatible period, including nonsimple varieties and periods with additional endomorphisms.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $E$ be an imaginary quadratic field.       "explanation": "On a sixfold with a unital quadratic action and a hyperbolic split-signature compatible polarization, the rational Weil plane consists of algebraic classes at every compatible period.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-weil-plane",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-hyperbolic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be imaginary quadratic. A nondegenerate $E$-Hermitian form of rank $2b$ and signature $(b,b)$, $b\\geq1$, is hyperbolic if and only if $$[\\det h]=[(-1)^b].$$ Every hyperbolic form of this rank is isometric to the sum of $b$ planes with matrix $\\left(\\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\\\1&0\\end{smallmatrix}\\right)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-hyperbolic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be imaginary quadratic.           "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $E$ be imaginary quadratic.       "explanation": "A nondegenerate Hermitian form of split signature is hyperbolic exactly when its determinant class is the expected class, and then it is a sum of standard hyperbolic planes.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety with an action of an imaginary quadratic field $E$, and let a compatible rational Riemann form have associated Hermitian form of signature $(p,q)$. Put $n=\\dim A=p+q$.         "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-albert-columns",
        "exact_quote": "In Types I–III let $F=Z(D)$ be totally real of degree $f$. In Type IV let the CM center $K=Z(D)$ have degree $2f$, and write $[D:K]=b^2$. In Type IV the opposite column is the dual $R_B$-module.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-small-projections",
        "exact_quote": "An alternating self-paired column of dimension $2,4$, or $6$ has the full symplectic derived image. A symmetric self-paired column cannot have dimension two; in dimensions four and six its derived image is, respectively, the full $\\operatorname{SO}_4$ and $\\operatorname{SO}_6$.*\n\n*If $D=K$ is an imaginary quadratic field, $B$ is non-CM, and $\\dim_K H_B\\leq6$, then the derived image on either embedding column is the full special linear group, with dual action on the opposite column.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-small-projections",
          "exact_quote": "An alternating self-paired column of dimension $2,4$, or $6$ has the full symplectic derived image.           "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "An alternating self-paired column of dimension $2,4$, or $6$ has the full symplectic derived image.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3ab-small-columns",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ have an imaginary-quadratic $E$-action and $\\dim A\\leq6$. Write $A\\sim\\prod_B B^{r_B}$ with pairwise nonisogenous simple factors, and discard the CM factors. Put $g=\\dim B$. After identifying matrix copies, the remaining columns have the following possibilities.*\n\n1.  *In Types I and II they have full symplectic derived images and dimensions $2,4,6$.*\n\n2.  *In Type III, $g=4$ or $6$, the center is $\\mathbb Q$, and $r_B=1$. The unique column has full $\\operatorname{SO}_g$ image. Each eigenspace of the given $E$-action is one copy of this column and has equal Hodge multiplicities $g/2,g/2$.*\n\n3.  *In Type IV, $D=K$ is a CM field. If $E$ admits no $\\mathbb Q$-embedding into $K$, then $K$ is imaginary quadratic, $g=3$, and $r_B=2$; the $K$-columns have the full $\\operatorname{SL}_3$ and dual images.*\n\n    *If $E$ admits a $\\mathbb Q$-embedding into $K$, put $[K:\\mathbb Q]=2f$ and $a=g/f$. The possibilities are $$f=1,\\quad 3\\leq a=g\\leq6;\n     \\qquad (f,a,g)=(2,2,4),(2,3,6),(3,2,6).$$ Every column has the full $\\operatorname{SL}_a$ image, paired with the dual at the opposite embedding. In the cases with $a=2$, some embedding pairs are mixed and some are pure.*\n\n*All these are projections of the actual derived Hodge group of $A$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },

The unique column has full $\\operatorname{SO}_g$ image. Each eigenspace of the given $E$-action is one copy of this column and has equal Hodge multiplicities $g/2,g/2$.*\n\n3.  *In Type IV, $D=K$ is a CM field. The possibilities are $$f=1,\\quad 3\\leq a=g\\leq6;\n     \\qquad (f,a,g)=(2,2,4),(2,3,6),(3,2,6).$$ Every column has the full $\\operatorname{SL}_a$ image, paired with the dual at the opposite embedding.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-seed",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\geq3$, put $n=d+1$. There exist a smooth nonhyperelliptic genus-three curve $C$, a point $p\\in C$, and cyclic subgroups $G_1,G_2\\subset X=\\operatorname{Pic}^2(C)$ of order $n$, with $G_1\\cap G_2=0$, as follows. There are cosets $\\{s_1,\\ldots,s_n\\}=s_0+G_2$ and $\\{t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\}=t_0+G_1$ such that $$C_i=t_{s_i}C_p,\\qquad \\Sigma_j=t_{t_j}\\Sigma_p,\\qquad\n A=\\coprod_{i=1}^n C_i,\\qquad \\Sigma=\\coprod_{j=1}^n\\Sigma_j\n \\label{p3sw:support-seed-orbits}$$ are disjoint within each of the two indicated unions, and $$H^0(X,I_A\\otimes H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes N)\n =\n H^0(X,I_\\Sigma\\otimes H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes N)=0\n \\quad\\text{for every }N\\in\\widehat X.\n \\label{p3sw:support-uniform-vanishing}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-seed",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\geq3$, put $n=d+1$. There are cosets $\\{s_1,\\ldots,s_n\\}=s_0+G_2$ and $\\{t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\}=t_0+G_1$ such that $$C_i=t_{s_i}C_p,\\qquad \\Sigma_j=t_{t_j}\\Sigma_p,\\qquad\n A=\\coprod_{i=1}^n C_i,\\qquad \\Sigma=\\coprod_{j=1}^n\\Sigma_j\n \\label{p3sw:support-seed-orbits}$$ are disjoint within each of the two indicated unions, and $$H^0(X,I_A\\otimes H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes N)\n =\n H^0(X,I_\\Sigma\\otimes H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes N)=0\n \\quad\\text{for every }N\\in\\widehat X.\n \\label{p3sw:support-uniform-vanishing}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every integer $d\\geq3$, put $n=d+1$. There are cosets $\\{s_1,\\ldots,s_n\\}=s_0+G_2$ and $\\{t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\}=t_0+G_1$ such that $$C_i=t_{s_i}C_p,\\qquad \\Sigma_j=t_{t_j}\\Sigma_p,\\qquad\n A=\\coprod_{i=1}^n C_i,\\qquad \\Sigma=\\coprod_{j=1}^n\\Sigma_j\n \\label{p3sw:support-seed-orbits}$$ are disjoint within each of the two indicated unions, and $$H^0(X,I_A\\otimes H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes N)\n =\n H^0(X,I_\\Sigma\\otimes H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes N)=0\n \\quad\\text{for every }N\\in\\widehat X.\n \\label{p3sw:support-uniform-vanishing}$$",
      "explanation": "There is a genus-three Jacobian with two disjoint Abel curve orbits under cyclic groups whose ideals have no sections after any degree-zero twist of twice the polarization.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-trilinear",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V_1,V_2,V_3$ be two-dimensional vector spaces and let $0\\ne T\\in V_1\\otimes V_2\\otimes V_3$. Suppose a finite indexed collection $$(x_h,y_h,z_h)\\in\n \\mathbb P(V_1^*)\\times\\mathbb P(V_2^*)\\times\\mathbb P(V_3^*)$$ has at most two indices in every fiber of each coordinate projection. At most three indices satisfy all three equations $$T(-,y_h,z_h)=T(x_h,-,z_h)=T(x_h,y_h,-)=0.\n \\label{p3sw:support-singular-marks}$$ Indices, rather than distinct triples, are counted.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },

At most three indices satisfy all three equations $$T(-,y_h,z_h)=T(x_h,-,z_h)=T(x_h,y_h,-)=0.\n \\label{p3sw:support-singular-marks}$$ Indices, rather than distinct triples, are counted.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-elliptic-star",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J_0=E_1\\times E_2\\times E_3$ be a product of elliptic curves with origins, and let $Z_0$ be the reduced union of the three coordinate elliptic axes through zero. Let $K\\subset J_0$ be a finite subgroup of order at least four whose projection to every $E_i$ is injective. The translates $Z(g)=g+Z_0$, $g\\in K$, are pairwise disjoint. For line bundles $D_i$ of degree two on $E_i$, $$H^0\\left(J_0,I_{\\bigcup_{g\\in K}Z(g)}\n       \\otimes(D_1\\boxtimes D_2\\boxtimes D_3)\\right)=0.\n \\label{p3sw:support-star-vanishing}$$ The same vanishing holds for any closed subscheme with that support.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-elliptic-star",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J_0=E_1\\times E_2\\times E_3$ be a product of elliptic curves with origins, and let $Z_0$ be the reduced union of the three coordinate elliptic axes through zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-finite-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a regular Noetherian scheme of finite dimension, let $f:V\\to B$ be projective, and let $\\mathcal F$ be a coherent sheaf flat over $B$. Then $Rf_*\\mathcal F$ is locally represented by a bounded complex $K^\\bullet$ of finite-rank locally free sheaves. For every morphism $h:T\\to B$ there is a derived base-change isomorphism $$Lh^*Rf_*\\mathcal F\n \\simeq Rf_{T,*}(\\mathcal F|_{V\\times_B T}),\n \\label{p3sw:support-derived-base-change}$$ where the restriction on the right is ordinary, and the left side is represented locally by $K^\\bullet\\otimes\\mathcal O_T$. The functions $b\\mapsto\\dim_{\\kappa(b)}H^i(V_b,\\mathcal F_b)$ are upper semicontinuous. If all derived fibers have cohomology in degrees $[c,e]$, the local model can be chosen in precisely those degrees.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-finite-model",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a regular Noetherian scheme of finite dimension, let $f:V\\to B$ be projective, and let $\\mathcal F$ be a coherent sheaf flat over $B$. Then $Rf_*\\mathcal F$ is locally represented by a bounded complex $K^\\bullet$ of finite-rank locally free sheaves. For every morphism $h:T\\to B$ there is a derived base-change isomorphism $$Lh^*Rf_*\\mathcal F\n \\simeq Rf_{T,*}(\\mathcal F|_{V\\times_B T}),\n \\label{p3sw:support-derived-base-change}$$ where the restriction on the right is ordinary, and the left side is represented locally by $K^\\bullet\\otimes\\mathcal O_T$. The functions $b\\mapsto\\dim_{\\kappa(b)}H^i(V_b,\\mathcal F_b)$ are upper semicontinuous. If all derived fibers have cohomology in degrees $[c,e]$, the local model can be chosen in precisely those degrees.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $B$ be a regular Noetherian scheme of finite dimension, let $f:V\\to B$ be projective, and let $\\mathcal F$ be a coherent sheaf flat over $B$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-local-tor",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a smooth threefold and let $D_1,D_2\\subset V$ be smooth curves with finite, possibly empty, intersection. Then $$\\operatorname{Tor}^{\\mathcal O_V}_k(I_{D_1},I_{D_2})=0\n \\quad(k>0),\n \\qquad\n I_{D_1}\\otimes I_{D_2}\\ \\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow\\ \\mathcal O_V\n \\label{p3sw:support-tor-product}$$ by multiplication, with image $I_{D_1}I_{D_2}$. The quotient from $\\mathcal O_V/I_{D_1}I_{D_2}$ to the structure sheaf of the reduced union has zero-dimensional kernel. The same statements hold when each $D_i$ is a disjoint union of smooth curves.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-support-local-tor",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a smooth threefold and let $D_1,D_2\\subset V$ be smooth curves with finite, possibly empty, intersection. The quotient from $\\mathcal O_V/I_{D_1}I_{D_2}$ to the structure sheaf of the reduced union has zero-dimensional kernel. The same statements hold when each $D_i$ is a disjoint union of smooth curves.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be a smooth threefold and let $D_1,D_2\\subset V$ be smooth curves with finite, possibly empty, intersection. The quotient from $\\mathcal O_V/I_{D_1}I_{D_2}$ to the structure sheaf of the reduced union has zero-dimensional kernel. The same statements hold when each $D_i$ is a disjoint union of smooth curves.",
      "explanation": "For smooth curves meeting in finitely many points on a smooth threefold, higher Tor of the ideals vanishes and the tensor product of ideals is the product ideal.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-relative-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "For the seed of Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:seed\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:seed\">17</a>, the derived tensor in <a href=\"#p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf\">[p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf]</a> is the ordinary coherent sheaf $\\mathscr I\\mathscr J\\subset\\mathcal O_{X\\times M}$. Both this ideal and its quotient in $\\mathcal O_{X\\times M}$ are flat over $M$. Ideal multiplication and both ideal sequences commute with every base change $T\\to M$. In particular, $\\mathscr F$ is $M$-flat, and at $m=(x,N)$ it has the ordinary fiber $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathscr F_m&=I_{Z_x}\\otimes\\mathcal M_{x,N},\\\\\n I_{Z_x}&=I_A I_{\\Sigma_x},\\qquad\n \\Sigma_x=\\coprod_jt_{-x}\\Sigma_j,\\qquad\n \\mathcal M_{x,N}=H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes a(-x)\\otimes N^{-1}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-fiber-formula}$$ Moreover $G=R\\pi_*\\mathscr F$ has a local finite locally free model, and for every $h:T\\to M$, $$Lh^*G\\simeq R\\pi_{T,*}(\\mathscr F|_{X\\times T}).\n \\label{p3sw:support-kernel-base-change}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-relative-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "For the seed of Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:seed\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:seed\">17</a>, the derived tensor in <a href=\"#p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf\">[p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf]</a> is the ordinary coherent sheaf $\\mathscr I\\mathscr J\\subset\\mathcal O_{X\\times M}$. Both this ideal and its quotient in $\\mathcal O_{X\\times M}$ are flat over $M$. Ideal multiplication and both ideal sequences commute with every base change $T\\to M$. In particular, $\\mathscr F$ is $M$-flat, and at $m=(x,N)$ it has the ordinary fiber $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathscr F_m&=I_{Z_x}\\otimes\\mathcal M_{x,N},\\\\\n I_{Z_x}&=I_A I_{\\Sigma_x},\\qquad\n \\Sigma_x=\\coprod_jt_{-x}\\Sigma_j,\\qquad\n \\mathcal M_{x,N}=H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes a(-x)\\otimes N^{-1}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-fiber-formula}$$ Moreover $G=R\\pi_*\\mathscr F$ has a local finite locally free model, and for every $h:T\\to M$, $$Lh^*G\\simeq R\\pi_{T,*}(\\mathscr F|_{X\\times T}).\n \\label{p3sw:support-kernel-base-change}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the seed of Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:seed\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:seed\">17</a>, the derived tensor in <a href=\"#p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf\">[p3sw:support-kernel-sheaf]</a> is the ordinary coherent sheaf $\\mathscr I\\mathscr J\\subset\\mathcal O_{X\\times M}$. Both this ideal and its quotient in $\\mathcal O_{X\\times M}$ are flat over $M$. Ideal multiplication and both ideal sequences commute with every base change $T\\to M$. In particular, $\\mathscr F$ is $M$-flat, and at $m=(x,N)$ it has the ordinary fiber $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathscr F_m&=I_{Z_x}\\otimes\\mathcal M_{x,N},\\\\\n I_{Z_x}&=I_A I_{\\Sigma_x},\\qquad\n \\Sigma_x=\\coprod_jt_{-x}\\Sigma_j,\\qquad\n \\mathcal M_{x,N}=H^{\\otimes2}\\otimes a(-x)\\otimes N^{-1}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-fiber-formula}$$ Moreover $G=R\\pi_*\\mathscr F$ has a local finite locally free model, and for every $h:T\\to M$, $$Lh^*G\\simeq R\\pi_{T,*}(\\mathscr F|_{X\\times T}).\n \\label{p3sw:support-kernel-base-change}$$",
      "explanation": "For the seed, the derived tensor kernel is an ordinary flat ideal sheaf and its derived pushforward commutes with every base change.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-coherent-sheaf",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D_M(-)=R\\mathcal Hom_M(-,\\mathcal O_M)$. Every derived fiber of $G$ has cohomology only in degrees one and two, and its degree-two cohomology vanishes on a nonempty open set. Consequently $$E=D_M(G)[-1]\n \\label{p3sw:support-dual-sheaf}$$ is an actual coherent perfect sheaf on $M$, locally with a length-one locally free resolution, of rank $$r=8d>0.\n \\label{p3sw:support-rank}$$ No reflexivity or codimension assertion is required for this claim.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-coherent-sheaf",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D_M(-)=R\\mathcal Hom_M(-,\\mathcal O_M)$. Consequently $$E=D_M(G)[-1]\n \\label{p3sw:support-dual-sheaf}$$ is an actual coherent perfect sheaf on $M$, locally with a length-one locally free resolution, of rank $$r=8d>0.\n \\label{p3sw:support-rank}$$ No reflexivity or codimension assertion is required for this claim.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $D_M(-)=R\\mathcal Hom_M(-,\\mathcal O_M)$.       "explanation": "The shifted derived dual of the transform is an honest coherent perfect sheaf of positive rank.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-coherent-action",
        "exact_quote": "For $g=(g_1,g_2)\\in G_1\\times G_2$, put $$\\begin{split}\n b_g&=(g_1-g_2,\\ a(-g_1-g_2))\\in M,\\\\\n N_g&=\\operatorname{pr}_X^*a(-g_1)\n       \\otimes\\operatorname{pr}_{\\widehat X}^*\\mathcal P_{-g_2},\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal P_v=\\mathcal P|_{\\{v\\}\\times\\widehat X}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-conjugated-action}$$ The conjugation of the source translations by $\\widetilde\\Phi$ has explicit functors $$T_g(-)=N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}(-).\n \\label{p3sw:support-action-functors}$$ There are unital associative line isomorphisms $$N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}N_h\\simeq N_{g+h}\n \\label{p3sw:support-line-compositors}$$ and compatible isomorphisms $T_gG\\simeq G$. Perfect duality gives actual sheaf isomorphisms $$N_g^{-1}\\otimes t_{b_g,*}E\\simeq E\n \\label{p3sw:support-dual-action}$$ with the corresponding unital associative line compositors. The homomorphism $g\\mapsto b_g$ is injective. Its image $\\bar G$ has order $n^2$, acts freely by translations, and $$q:M\\longrightarrow Y_0=M/\\bar G\n \\label{p3sw:support-quotient}$$ is a finite étale isogeny of abelian sixfolds.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-coherent-action",
          "exact_quote": "For $g=(g_1,g_2)\\in G_1\\times G_2$, put $$\\begin{split}\n b_g&=(g_1-g_2,\\ a(-g_1-g_2))\\in M,\\\\\n N_g&=\\operatorname{pr}_X^*a(-g_1)\n       \\otimes\\operatorname{pr}_{\\widehat X}^*\\mathcal P_{-g_2},\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal P_v=\\mathcal P|_{\\{v\\}\\times\\widehat X}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-conjugated-action}$$ The conjugation of the source translations by $\\widetilde\\Phi$ has explicit functors $$T_g(-)=N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}(-).\n \\label{p3sw:support-action-functors}$$ There are unital associative line isomorphisms $$N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}N_h\\simeq N_{g+h}\n \\label{p3sw:support-line-compositors}$$ and compatible isomorphisms $T_gG\\simeq G$. Perfect duality gives actual sheaf isomorphisms $$N_g^{-1}\\otimes t_{b_g,*}E\\simeq E\n \\label{p3sw:support-dual-action}$$ with the corresponding unital associative line compositors. The homomorphism $g\\mapsto b_g$ is injective. Its image $\\bar G$ has order $n^2$, acts freely by translations, and $$q:M\\longrightarrow Y_0=M/\\bar G\n \\label{p3sw:support-quotient}$$ is a finite étale isogeny of abelian sixfolds.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"

      "exact_statement": "For $g=(g_1,g_2)\\in G_1\\times G_2$, put $$\\begin{split}\n b_g&=(g_1-g_2,\\ a(-g_1-g_2))\\in M,\\\\\n N_g&=\\operatorname{pr}_X^*a(-g_1)\n       \\otimes\\operatorname{pr}_{\\widehat X}^*\\mathcal P_{-g_2},\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal P_v=\\mathcal P|_{\\{v\\}\\times\\widehat X}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-conjugated-action}$$ The conjugation of the source translations by $\\widetilde\\Phi$ has explicit functors $$T_g(-)=N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}(-).\n \\label{p3sw:support-action-functors}$$ There are unital associative line isomorphisms $$N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}N_h\\simeq N_{g+h}\n \\label{p3sw:support-line-compositors}$$ and compatible isomorphisms $T_gG\\simeq G$. Perfect duality gives actual sheaf isomorphisms $$N_g^{-1}\\otimes t_{b_g,*}E\\simeq E\n \\label{p3sw:support-dual-action}$$ with the corresponding unital associative line compositors. Its image $\\bar G$ has order $n^2$, acts freely by translations, and $$q:M\\longrightarrow Y_0=M/\\bar G\n \\label{p3sw:support-quotient}$$ is a finite étale isogeny of abelian sixfolds.",
      "explanation": "The sheaf carries a coherent line-twisted translation action of a finite group that acts freely, giving a finite etale isogeny quotient.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-coherent-action",
          "exact_quote": "For $g=(g_1,g_2)\\in G_1\\times G_2$, put $$\\begin{split}\n b_g&=(g_1-g_2,\\ a(-g_1-g_2))\\in M,\\\\\n N_g&=\\operatorname{pr}_X^*a(-g_1)\n       \\otimes\\operatorname{pr}_{\\widehat X}^*\\mathcal P_{-g_2},\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal P_v=\\mathcal P|_{\\{v\\}\\times\\widehat X}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{p3sw:support-conjugated-action}$$ The conjugation of the source translations by $\\widetilde\\Phi$ has explicit functors $$T_g(-)=N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}(-).\n \\label{p3sw:support-action-functors}$$ There are unital associative line isomorphisms $$N_g\\otimes t_{b_g,*}N_h\\simeq N_{g+h}\n \\label{p3sw:support-line-compositors}$$ and compatible isomorphisms $T_gG\\simeq G$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety of dimension $d$, and let $Q\\in\\operatorname{Perf}(A)$. Use the raw first-jet class $a_Q$ from <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension\">[p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension]</a>, and the left-interior and HKR conventions <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-eq:left-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-eq:left-action\">[p3sw:hh-eq:left-action]</a> and <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr\">[p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr]</a>. Define $$\\operatorname{ob}_Q=\\chi_Q\\circ\\operatorname{HKR}_A:\n       HT^2(A)\\longrightarrow\\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q).$$ For $\\alpha=\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}$, this evaluation is $$\\operatorname{ob}_Q(\\alpha)\n       =\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\n                                      \\frac{a_Q^b}{b!}.$$ On the full group $\\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q)$, define $$\\begin{split}\n \\sigma_Q&=(\\sigma_{Q,j})_{0\\leq j\\leq d-2}:\n   \\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q)\n       \\longrightarrow H\\Omega_{-2}(A)\n          =\\bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}H^{j+2}(A,\\Omega_A^j),\\\\\n \\sigma_{Q,j}(v)&=\n   \\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,\\Omega_A^j[j+2]}\n        \\left(\\left(\\frac{a_Q^j}{j!}\\right)[2]\\circ v\\right).\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:sigma}$$ The direct sum is zero if $d<2$. Set $$c_Q(\\alpha)=\\alpha\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\\operatorname{ch}_H(Q)\n       \\in H\\Omega_{-2}(A).$$ Then $$\\sigma_Q\\circ\\operatorname{ob}_Q=c_Q.\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle}$$ More precisely, with $\\operatorname{ch}_{H,k}(Q)=0$ for $k\\notin[0,d]$, each component is $$\\sigma_{Q,j}(\\operatorname{ob}_Q(\\alpha))\n    =\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\n                          \\operatorname{ch}_{H,j+b}(Q)\n       \\quad\\text{in }H^{j+2}(A,\\Omega_A^j).\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle",

Define $$\\operatorname{ob}_Q=\\chi_Q\\circ\\operatorname{HKR}_A:\n       HT^2(A)\\longrightarrow\\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q).$$ For $\\alpha=\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}$, this evaluation is $$\\operatorname{ob}_Q(\\alpha)\n       =\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\n                                      \\frac{a_Q^b}{b!}.$$ On the full group $\\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q)$, define $$\\begin{split}\n \\sigma_Q&=(\\sigma_{Q,j})_{0\\leq j\\leq d-2}:\n   \\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q)\n       \\longrightarrow H\\Omega_{-2}(A)\n          =\\bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}H^{j+2}(A,\\Omega_A^j),\\\\\n \\sigma_{Q,j}(v)&=\n   \\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,\\Omega_A^j[j+2]}\n        \\left(\\left(\\frac{a_Q^j}{j!}\\right)[2]\\circ v\\right).\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:sigma}$$ The direct sum is zero if $d<2$. Set $$c_Q(\\alpha)=\\alpha\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\\operatorname{ch}_H(Q)\n       \\in H\\Omega_{-2}(A).$$ Then $$\\sigma_Q\\circ\\operatorname{ob}_Q=c_Q.\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle}$$ More precisely, with $\\operatorname{ch}_{H,k}(Q)=0$ for $k\\notin[0,d]$, each component is $$\\sigma_{Q,j}(\\operatorname{ob}_Q(\\alpha))\n    =\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\n                          \\operatorname{ch}_{H,j+b}(Q)\n       \\quad\\text{in }H^{j+2}(A,\\Omega_A^j).\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
```
