      "exact_statement": "Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety of dimension $d$, and let $Q\\in\\operatorname{Perf}(A)$. Use the raw first-jet class $a_Q$ from <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension\">[p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension]</a>, and the left-interior and HKR conventions <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-eq:left-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-eq:left-action\">[p3sw:hh-eq:left-action]</a> and <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr\">[p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr]</a>. Set $$c_Q(\\alpha)=\\alpha\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\\operatorname{ch}_H(Q)\n       \\in H\\Omega_{-2}(A).$$ Then $$\\sigma_Q\\circ\\operatorname{ob}_Q=c_Q.\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle}$$ More precisely, with $\\operatorname{ch}_{H,k}(Q)=0$ for $k\\notin[0,d]$, each component is $$\\sigma_{Q,j}(\\operatorname{ob}_Q(\\alpha))\n    =\\sum_{a+b=2}\\alpha^{a,b}\\mathbin{\\lrcorner}\n                          \\operatorname{ch}_{H,j+b}(Q)\n       \\quad\\text{in }H^{j+2}(A,\\Omega_A^j).\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-hh-transport",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed enhanced Fourier–Mukai equivalence $\\Phi:\\operatorname{Perf}(A)\\to\\operatorname{Perf}(B)$, there exists an isomorphism $$\\Phi^H_{HH}:H\\Omega_{-2}(A)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}H\\Omega_{-2}(B)$$ depending only on that enhanced equivalence, not on a choice of perfect complex, such that for every $Q\\in\\operatorname{Perf}(A)$, $$\\begin{split}\n \\Phi_{\\mathrm{Ext}}\\circ\\operatorname{ob}_Q\n     &=\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q}\\circ\\Phi^{HT},\\\\\n \\Phi^H_{HH}\\circ c_Q&=c_{\\Phi Q}\\circ\\Phi^{HT}.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares}$$ Here $\\operatorname{ob}$ and $c$ have the conventions of Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-triangle\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-triangle\">25</a>, and $\\Phi_{\\mathrm{Ext}}$ is the induced enhanced map on degree-two Ext. Consequently $$\\begin{split}\n \\Phi^{HT}(\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_Q)\n       &=\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q},\\\\\n \\Phi^{HT}(\\ker c_Q)&=\\ker c_{\\Phi Q}.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport}$$ In particular $\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_Q=\\ker c_Q$ implies $\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q}=\\ker c_{\\Phi Q}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-hh-transport",
          "exact_quote": "For the fixed enhanced Fourier–Mukai equivalence $\\Phi:\\operatorname{Perf}(A)\\to\\operatorname{Perf}(B)$, there exists an isomorphism $$\\Phi^H_{HH}:H\\Omega_{-2}(A)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}H\\Omega_{-2}(B)$$ depending only on that enhanced equivalence, not on a choice of perfect complex, such that for every $Q\\in\\operatorname{Perf}(A)$, $$\\begin{split}\n \\Phi_{\\mathrm{Ext}}\\circ\\operatorname{ob}_Q\n     &=\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q}\\circ\\Phi^{HT},\\\\\n \\Phi^H_{HH}\\circ c_Q&=c_{\\Phi Q}\\circ\\Phi^{HT}.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares}$$ Here $\\operatorname{ob}$ and $c$ have the conventions of Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-triangle\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-triangle\">25</a>, and $\\Phi_{\\mathrm{Ext}}$ is the induced enhanced map on degree-two Ext. Consequently $$\\begin{split}\n \\Phi^{HT}(\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_Q)\n       &=\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q},\\\\\n \\Phi^{HT}(\\ker c_Q)&=\\ker c_{\\Phi Q}.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport}$$ In particular $\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_Q=\\ker c_Q$ implies $\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q}=\\ker c_{\\Phi Q}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the fixed enhanced Fourier–Mukai equivalence $\\Phi:\\operatorname{Perf}(A)\\to\\operatorname{Perf}(B)$, there exists an isomorphism $$\\Phi^H_{HH}:H\\Omega_{-2}(A)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}H\\Omega_{-2}(B)$$ depending only on that enhanced equivalence, not on a choice of perfect complex, such that for every $Q\\in\\operatorname{Perf}(A)$, $$\\begin{split}\n \\Phi_{\\mathrm{Ext}}\\circ\\operatorname{ob}_Q\n     &=\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q}\\circ\\Phi^{HT},\\\\\n \\Phi^H_{HH}\\circ c_Q&=c_{\\Phi Q}\\circ\\Phi^{HT}.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares}$$ Here $\\operatorname{ob}$ and $c$ have the conventions of Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:hh-triangle\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:hh-triangle\">25</a>, and $\\Phi_{\\mathrm{Ext}}$ is the induced enhanced map on degree-two Ext. Consequently $$\\begin{split}\n \\Phi^{HT}(\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_Q)\n       &=\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q},\\\\\n \\Phi^{HT}(\\ker c_Q)&=\\ker c_{\\Phi Q}.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport}$$ In particular $\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_Q=\\ker c_Q$ implies $\\ker\\operatorname{ob}_{\\Phi Q}=\\ker c_{\\Phi Q}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-abel-character",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subset X$ be an Abel curve. Its normalized Betti class and Chern character are $$[D]_{\\mathrm B}=\\frac{\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2}{2},\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{ch}_{\\mathrm B}(\\mathcal O_D)\n   =\\frac{\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2}{2}\n      -\\frac{\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^3}{3}.$$ Normal restriction of translation-invariant vector fields is an isomorphism $$H^0(X,T_X)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }H^0(D,N_{D/X});$$ both spaces have dimension three. If $Z=\\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ is a disjoint union of $n$ Abel curves, then its ideal satisfies the full normalized Betti identity $$\\label{p3sw:invariant-betti-character}\n \\operatorname{ch}_{\\mathrm B}(I_Z)\n =1-\\frac n2\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2+\\frac n3\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^3.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-abel-character",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subset X$ be an Abel curve. Its normalized Betti class and Chern character are $$[D]_{\\mathrm B}=\\frac{\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2}{2},\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{ch}_{\\mathrm B}(\\mathcal O_D)\n   =\\frac{\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2}{2}\n      -\\frac{\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^3}{3}.$$ Normal restriction of translation-invariant vector fields is an isomorphism $$H^0(X,T_X)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }H^0(D,N_{D/X});$$ both spaces have dimension three. If $Z=\\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ is a disjoint union of $n$ Abel curves, then its ideal satisfies the full normalized Betti identity $$\\label{p3sw:invariant-betti-character}\n \\operatorname{ch}_{\\mathrm B}(I_Z)\n =1-\\frac n2\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2+\\frac n3\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^3.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $D\\subset X$ be an Abel curve. If $Z=\\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ is a disjoint union of $n$ Abel curves, then its ideal satisfies the full normalized Betti identity $$\\label{p3sw:invariant-betti-character}\n \\operatorname{ch}_{\\mathrm B}(I_Z)\n =1-\\frac n2\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^2+\\frac n3\\theta_{\\mathrm B}^3.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma\\subset X$ be a finite subgroup of order $n$, and let $Z=\\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ be a disjoint union of Abel curves permuted transitively by $\\Gamma$. Give $I_Z$ its canonical linearization. There is an isomorphism of $\\Gamma$-representations $$\\label{p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)\n \\simeq H^1(X,\\mathcal O_X)\n       \\oplus\\bigoplus_{j=1}^n H^0(X,T_X).$$ The first summand is trivial, and $\\Gamma$ acts on the second by its regular permutation action on the components and the identity on each translation space. In particular, $$\\dim_{\\mathbb C}\\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\\Gamma=6,\\qquad\n \\dim_{\\mathbb C}\\operatorname{Ext}^2_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\\Gamma=6,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hom}_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\\Gamma=\\mathbb C.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma\\subset X$ be a finite subgroup of order $n$, and let $Z=\\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ be a disjoint union of Abel curves permuted transitively by $\\Gamma$. Give $I_Z$ its canonical linearization. There is an isomorphism of $\\Gamma$-representations $$\\label{p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)\n \\simeq H^1(X,\\mathcal O_X)\n       \\oplus\\bigoplus_{j=1}^n H^0(X,T_X).$$ The first summand is trivial, and $\\Gamma$ acts on the second by its regular permutation action on the components and the identity on each translation space. In particular, $$\\dim_{\\mathbb C}\\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\\Gamma=6,\\qquad\n \\dim_{\\mathbb C}\\operatorname{Ext}^2_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\\Gamma=6,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hom}_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\\Gamma=\\mathbb C.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Gamma\\subset X$ be a finite subgroup of order $n$, and let $Z=\\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ be a disjoint union of Abel curves permuted transitively by $\\Gamma$.         "exact_quote": "For the source $\\mathcal I=I_\\Sigma\\boxtimes I_A$ with its canonical $G_{\\mathrm{src}}=G_1\\times G_2$-linearization, $$\\label{p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion}\n \\operatorname{im}(o_{\\mathcal I})\n   =\\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\\mathcal I,\\mathcal I)^{G_{\\mathrm{src}}},\n \\qquad\n \\ker(o_{\\mathcal I})=\\ker(c_{\\mathcal I}^2),$$ and $\\sigma_{\\mathcal I}$ is injective on this full invariant group.*\n\n\n*Let $M=X\\times\\widehat X$ and use the fixed enhanced equivalence $$\\widetilde\\Phi=\\Phi\\circ\\bigl((H\\boxtimes H)\\otimes-\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal G=\\widetilde\\Phi(\\mathcal I)[-3].$$ Give $\\mathcal G$ the coherent line-translation action from Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:coherent-action\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:coherent-action\">24</a>, and denote its underlying translation group by $\\bar G\\simeq G_{\\mathrm{src}}$.         "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-spin-identity",
        "exact_quote": "For every algebraic spin element $g$, one has the exact equality of rational representations $$\\label{p3sw:spin-eq:identity}\n \\rho'_g=e^{p/2}\\Lambda\\rho(g)e^{-p/2}\n       =e^{(p-\\Lambda^2\\rho(g)p)/2}\\Lambda\\rho(g).$$ The exponentials denote exterior multiplication.         "source_id": "PM-032.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-polarized-seed",
        "exact_quote": "On the actual sixfold $M=X\\times\\widehat X$, define $$\\eta(u)(x,N)=\\bigl(a^{-1}(N),[-d]a(x)\\bigr)\n             =\\bigl(-\\lambda^{-1}(N),[d]\\lambda(x)\\bigr).$$ This gives a unital $K$-action. The ample line $$\\mathscr L_M=\\operatorname{pr}_X^*H^{\\otimes d}\n          \\otimes\\operatorname{pr}_{\\widehat X}^*(a^{-1})^*H$$ has a class $\\theta_M=c_{1,\\mathrm B}(\\mathscr L_M)$ fixed by the vector images of $J$ and $\\widetilde L_K$. The action is of Weil type $(3,3)$, is compatible with this polarization, and its actual homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic.*\n\n\n*More precisely, the pullback $f=\\eta(u)^*$ on $V=L\\oplus U$ and its two eigenspaces are $$\\label{p3sw:spin-eq:oriented-action}\n \\begin{split}\n f(w,y)&=(d\\vartheta y,-\\vartheta^{-1}w),\\qquad f^2=-d,\\\\\n W_+&=\\operatorname{Ann}(\\lambda_+)\n       =\\{(-u\\vartheta y,y):y\\in U_K\\},\\\\\n W_-&=\\operatorname{Ann}(\\lambda_-)\n       =\\{(u\\vartheta y,y):y\\in U_K\\}.\n \\end{split}$$ The eigenvalue on $W_+$ is $+u$, and that on $W_-$ is $-u$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-polarized-seed",
          "exact_quote": "On the actual sixfold $M=X\\times\\widehat X$, define $$\\eta(u)(x,N)=\\bigl(a^{-1}(N),[-d]a(x)\\bigr)\n             =\\bigl(-\\lambda^{-1}(N),[d]\\lambda(x)\\bigr).$$ This gives a unital $K$-action. The action is of Weil type $(3,3)$, is compatible with this polarization, and its actual homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic.*\n\n\n*More precisely, the pullback $f=\\eta(u)^*$ on $V=L\\oplus U$ and its two eigenspaces are $$\\label{p3sw:spin-eq:oriented-action}\n \\begin{split}\n f(w,y)&=(d\\vartheta y,-\\vartheta^{-1}w),\\qquad f^2=-d,\\\\\n W_+&=\\operatorname{Ann}(\\lambda_+)\n       =\\{(-u\\vartheta y,y):y\\in U_K\\},\\\\\n W_-&=\\operatorname{Ann}(\\lambda_-)\n       =\\{(u\\vartheta y,y):y\\in U_K\\}.\n \\end{split}$$ The eigenvalue on $W_+$ is $+u$, and that on $W_-$ is $-u$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On the actual sixfold $M=X\\times\\widehat X$, define $$\\eta(u)(x,N)=\\bigl(a^{-1}(N),[-d]a(x)\\bigr)\n             =\\bigl(-\\lambda^{-1}(N),[d]\\lambda(x)\\bigr).$$ This gives a unital $K$-action. The ample line $$\\mathscr L_M=\\operatorname{pr}_X^*H^{\\otimes d}\n          \\otimes\\operatorname{pr}_{\\widehat X}^*(a^{-1})^*H$$ has a class $\\theta_M=c_{1,\\mathrm B}(\\mathscr L_M)$ fixed by the vector images of $J$ and $\\widetilde L_K$. The action is of Weil type $(3,3)$, is compatible with this polarization, and its actual homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic.*\n\n\n*More precisely, the pullback $f=\\eta(u)^*$ on $V=L\\oplus U$ and its two eigenspaces are $$\\label{p3sw:spin-eq:oriented-action}\n \\begin{split}\n f(w,y)&=(d\\vartheta y,-\\vartheta^{-1}w),\\qquad f^2=-d,\\\\\n W_+&=\\operatorname{Ann}(\\lambda_+)\n       =\\{(-u\\vartheta y,y):y\\in U_K\\},\\\\\n W_-&=\\operatorname{Ann}(\\lambda_-)\n       =\\{(u\\vartheta y,y):y\\in U_K\\}.\n \\end{split}$$ The eigenvalue on $W_+$ is $+u$, and that on $W_-$ is $-u$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-ordered-stabilizer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W=W_+$. For $0\\leq j\\leq6$, $$\\label{p3sw:spin-eq:invariants}\n H^{2j}(M,\\mathbb Q)^J=\n \\begin{cases}\n  \\mathbb Q\\theta_M^j,&j\\ne3,\\\\\n  \\mathbb Q\\theta_M^3\\oplus\\mathcal W_K(M),&j=3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad H^{\\mathrm{odd}}(M,\\mathbb Q)^J=0 .$$ The ordered line stabilizer fixes every $\\theta_M^j$ and acts on the two scalar Weil lines by $t^2,t^{-2}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-ordered-stabilizer",

For $0\\leq j\\leq6$, $$\\label{p3sw:spin-eq:invariants}\n H^{2j}(M,\\mathbb Q)^J=\n \\begin{cases}\n  \\mathbb Q\\theta_M^j,&j\\ne3,\\\\\n  \\mathbb Q\\theta_M^3\\oplus\\mathcal W_K(M),&j=3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad H^{\\mathrm{odd}}(M,\\mathbb Q)^J=0 .$$ The ordered line stabilizer fixes every $\\theta_M^j$ and acts on the two scalar Weil lines by $t^2,t^{-2}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-descent-duality",
        "exact_quote": "Even transpose induces an isomorphism $$d_2:\\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\\mathcal G,\\mathcal G)^{\\bar G}\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       \\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(E,E)^{\\bar G}.$$ With the convention <a href=\"#p3sw:descent-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:descent-character\">[p3sw:descent-character]</a>, its components obey $$\\label{p3sw:descent-dual-sign}\n \\sigma_{E,j}(d_2v)=(-1)^{j+1}\\sigma_{\\mathcal G,j}(v)\n \\quad\\text{for every }j\\geq0.$$ Consequently, injectivity of $\\sigma_{\\mathcal G}$ on the full $\\bar G$-invariant $\\operatorname{Ext}^2$ implies injectivity of $\\sigma_E$ on the full $\\bar G$-invariant $\\operatorname{Ext}^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-descent-duality",
          "exact_quote": "Even transpose induces an isomorphism $$d_2:\\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\\mathcal G,\\mathcal G)^{\\bar G}\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       \\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(E,E)^{\\bar G}.$$ With the convention <a href=\"#p3sw:descent-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:descent-character\">[p3sw:descent-character]</a>, its components obey $$\\label{p3sw:descent-dual-sign}\n \\sigma_{E,j}(d_2v)=(-1)^{j+1}\\sigma_{\\mathcal G,j}(v)\n \\quad\\text{for every }j\\geq0.$$ Consequently, injectivity of $\\sigma_{\\mathcal G}$ on the full $\\bar G$-invariant $\\operatorname{Ext}^2$ implies injectivity of $\\sigma_E$ on the full $\\bar G$-invariant $\\operatorname{Ext}^2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Even transpose induces an isomorphism $$d_2:\\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\\mathcal G,\\mathcal G)^{\\bar G}\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       \\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(E,E)^{\\bar G}.$$ With the convention <a href=\"#p3sw:descent-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:descent-character\">[p3sw:descent-character]</a>, its components obey $$\\label{p3sw:descent-dual-sign}\n \\sigma_{E,j}(d_2v)=(-1)^{j+1}\\sigma_{\\mathcal G,j}(v)\n \\quad\\text{for every }j\\geq0.$$ Consequently, injectivity of $\\sigma_{\\mathcal G}$ on the full $\\bar G$-invariant $\\operatorname{Ext}^2$ implies injectivity of $\\sigma_E$ on the full $\\bar G$-invariant $\\operatorname{Ext}^2$.",
      "explanation": "Even transpose gives an invariant isomorphism of second Ext groups, changing semiregularity components by an explicit sign, so injectivity transfers.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-descent-duality",
          "exact_quote": "Even transpose induces an isomorphism $$d_2:\\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\\mathcal G,\\mathcal G)^{\\bar G}\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       \\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(E,E)^{\\bar G}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-descent-duality-sign",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-descent-duality",
          "exact_quote": "\\text{for every }j\\geq0",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-p3sw-descent-duality"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-twisted-descent",
        "exact_quote": "There are a class $\\alpha\\in H^2(Y_0,\\mu_r)$, a coherent $\\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\\mathcal B$ of rank $r$ with $\\det\\mathcal B\\simeq\\mathcal O_{Y_0}$, and an invertible $q^*\\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\\mathcal L$ on $M$, such that $$\\label{p3sw:descent-twisted-pullback}\n q^*\\mathcal B\\simeq E\\otimes\\mathcal L.$$ Let $\\mathfrak Y_0$ be the $\\mu_r$-gerbe of $\\alpha$. For every integer $i$, this isomorphism induces $$\\label{p3sw:descent-ext}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^i_{\\mathfrak Y_0}(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)\n   \\simeq\\operatorname{Ext}^i_M(E,E)^{\\bar G},\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Ext}^i_0(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)\n   \\simeq\\operatorname{Ext}^i_0(E,E)^{\\bar G},$$ where the subscript $0$ denotes the kernel of the perfect trace to $H^i(\\mathcal O)$. Put $$\\lambda_H=\\frac{a_{\\det E}}r=\\frac{\\operatorname{tr}(a_E)}r.$$ Under the first isomorphism $\\phi$ in degree two, the raw Hodge square $$\\label{p3sw:descent-square}\n\\begin{array}{ccc}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^2(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)&\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\phi\\ }&\\operatorname{Ext}^2(E,E)^{\\bar G}\\\\\n \\big\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle\\sigma_{\\mathcal B}}&&\n       \\big\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle e^{-\\lambda_H}\\sigma_E}\\\\\n \\mathcal H_{-2}(Y_0)&\\xrightarrow{\\ q^*\\ }&\\mathcal H_{-2}(M)\n\\end{array}$$ commutes, both on full Ext and on its trace-free summand. In particular, full invariant injectivity for $E$ makes $\\mathcal B$ semiregular. By Lemma <a href=\"#p3sw:descent-duality\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:descent-duality\">39</a>, full invariant injectivity for $\\mathcal G$ is sufficient for this conclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-twisted-descent",
          "exact_quote": "There are a class $\\alpha\\in H^2(Y_0,\\mu_r)$, a coherent $\\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\\mathcal B$ of rank $r$ with $\\det\\mathcal B\\simeq\\mathcal O_{Y_0}$, and an invertible $q^*\\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\\mathcal L$ on $M$, such that $$\\label{p3sw:descent-twisted-pullback}\n q^*\\mathcal B\\simeq E\\otimes\\mathcal L.$$ Let $\\mathfrak Y_0$ be the $\\mu_r$-gerbe of $\\alpha$. For every integer $i$, this isomorphism induces $$\\label{p3sw:descent-ext}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^i_{\\mathfrak Y_0}(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)\n   \\simeq\\operatorname{Ext}^i_M(E,E)^{\\bar G},\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Ext}^i_0(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)\n   \\simeq\\operatorname{Ext}^i_0(E,E)^{\\bar G},$$ where the subscript $0$ denotes the kernel of the perfect trace to $H^i(\\mathcal O)$. Put $$\\lambda_H=\\frac{a_{\\det E}}r=\\frac{\\operatorname{tr}(a_E)}r.$$ Under the first isomorphism $\\phi$ in degree two, the raw Hodge square $$\\label{p3sw:descent-square}\n\\begin{array}{ccc}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^2(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)&\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\phi\\ }&\\operatorname{Ext}^2(E,E)^{\\bar G}\\\\\n \\big\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle\\sigma_{\\mathcal B}}&&\n       \\big\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle e^{-\\lambda_H}\\sigma_E}\\\\\n \\mathcal H_{-2}(Y_0)&\\xrightarrow{\\ q^*\\ }&\\mathcal H_{-2}(M)\n\\end{array}$$ commutes, both on full Ext and on its trace-free summand. In particular, full invariant injectivity for $E$ makes $\\mathcal B$ semiregular. By Lemma <a href=\"#p3sw:descent-duality\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:descent-duality\">39</a>, full invariant injectivity for $\\mathcal G$ is sufficient for this conclusion.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.3"

      "exact_statement": "There are a class $\\alpha\\in H^2(Y_0,\\mu_r)$, a coherent $\\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\\mathcal B$ of rank $r$ with $\\det\\mathcal B\\simeq\\mathcal O_{Y_0}$, and an invertible $q^*\\alpha$-twisted sheaf $\\mathcal L$ on $M$, such that $$\\label{p3sw:descent-twisted-pullback}\n q^*\\mathcal B\\simeq E\\otimes\\mathcal L.$$ Let $\\mathfrak Y_0$ be the $\\mu_r$-gerbe of $\\alpha$. Put $$\\lambda_H=\\frac{a_{\\det E}}r=\\frac{\\operatorname{tr}(a_E)}r.$$ Under the first isomorphism $\\phi$ in degree two, the raw Hodge square $$\\label{p3sw:descent-square}\n\\begin{array}{ccc}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^2(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)&\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\phi\\ }&\\operatorname{Ext}^2(E,E)^{\\bar G}\\\\\n \\big\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle\\sigma_{\\mathcal B}}&&\n       \\big\\downarrow{\\scriptstyle e^{-\\lambda_H}\\sigma_E}\\\\\n \\mathcal H_{-2}(Y_0)&\\xrightarrow{\\ q^*\\ }&\\mathcal H_{-2}(M)\n\\end{array}$$ commutes, both on full Ext and on its trace-free summand.       "explanation": "The sheaf descends along the isogeny to a determinant-trivial twisted sheaf whose full Ext groups match the invariant ones upstairs, and it is semiregular.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-ordinary-conversion",
        "exact_quote": "For the class $\\alpha$ and the semiregular twisted coherent sheaf $\\mathcal B$ in Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:twisted-descent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:twisted-descent\">40</a>, put $m=r^{12}$ and $N=6+m-1$. There exist a holomorphic $\\mathbb P^{m-1}$-bundle $p:Z\\to Y$, a determinant-trivial $\\alpha$-twisted vector bundle $Q_0$ of rank $m$ on $Y_0$, and an inverse-twisted tautological line $\\ell_0=\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P(Q_0)}(1)$, with $Z_0=\\mathbb P(Q_0)$. The ordinary line $$\\label{p3sw:descent-anticanonical}\n H=\\omega_{Z/Y}^{-1}=\\det T_{Z/Y}$$ satisfies $H|_{Z_0}\\simeq\\ell_0^m$ and restricts to $\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P^{m-1}}(m)$ on every $p$-fiber. The normalized Betti class and raw Hodge class $$\\label{p3sw:descent-zeta}\n \\zeta_{B,s}=\\frac{c_1^B(H|_{Z_s})}{m},\\qquad\n \\zeta_{H,s}=\\frac{a_{H|_{Z_s}}}{m},\\qquad\n \\iota_{Z_s}(\\zeta_{H,s})=(2\\pi i)\\zeta_{B,s}$$ have the following properties: $\\zeta_B$ is a horizontal rational class of type $(1,1)$, and its restriction to every $p_s$-fiber is the normalized hyperplane class. Every $Z_s$ and $p_s$ is projective algebraic, and $a\\circ p$ has a relative ample line.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*The sheaf $$F_0=p_0^*\\mathcal B\\otimes\\ell_0$$ is ordinary coherent on $Z_0$, and it is semiregular. More precisely, there is a full derived isomorphism $$\\label{p3sw:descent-ordinary-ext}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^2_{Z_0}(F_0,F_0)\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Ext}^2_{\\mathfrak Y_0}(\\mathcal B,\\mathcal B)$$ under which $$\\label{p3sw:descent-ordinary-sigma}\n \\sigma_{F_0}(p_0^*v)\n       =e^{\\zeta_{H,0}}p_0^*\\sigma_{\\mathcal B}(v).$$ If a rational horizontal section $\\kappa_B=\\sum_{k=0}^6\\kappa_{B,k}$ specializes to $\\operatorname{ch}_B(\\mathcal B)$ and has type $(k,k)$ in degree $2k$, then $$\\label{p3sw:descent-horizontal-character}\n A_B=p^*\\kappa_B e^{\\zeta_B}=\\sum_{k=0}^{N}A_{B,k}$$ is rational, horizontal, and of type $(k,k)$ in degree $2k$. Its raw Hodge section in that degree is the edge of $(2\\pi i)^kA_{B,k}$, and its central value is $\\operatorname{ch}_{H,k}(F_0)$. Finally, on every fiber, $$\\label{p3sw:descent-extraction}\n p_{s*}^B\\bigl(\\zeta_{B,s}^{\\,m-1}e^{-\\zeta_{B,s}}A_B(s)\\bigr)\n       =\\kappa_B(s),$$ where $p_{s*}^B$ is normalized Betti fiber integration.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-ordinary-conversion",

There exist a holomorphic $\\mathbb P^{m-1}$-bundle $p:Z\\to Y$, a determinant-trivial $\\alpha$-twisted vector bundle $Q_0$ of rank $m$ on $Y_0$, and an inverse-twisted tautological line $\\ell_0=\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P(Q_0)}(1)$, with $Z_0=\\mathbb P(Q_0)$.       "exact_statement": "For the class $\\alpha$ and the semiregular twisted coherent sheaf $\\mathcal B$ in Proposition <a href=\"#p3sw:twisted-descent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"p3sw:twisted-descent\">40</a>, put $m=r^{12}$ and $N=6+m-1$. There exist a holomorphic $\\mathbb P^{m-1}$-bundle $p:Z\\to Y$, a determinant-trivial $\\alpha$-twisted vector bundle $Q_0$ of rank $m$ on $Y_0$, and an inverse-twisted tautological line $\\ell_0=\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P(Q_0)}(1)$, with $Z_0=\\mathbb P(Q_0)$. Every $Z_s$ and $p_s$ is projective algebraic, and $a\\circ p$ has a relative ample line.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*The sheaf $$F_0=p_0^*\\mathcal B\\otimes\\ell_0$$ is ordinary coherent on $Z_0$, and it is semiregular. Finally, on every fiber, $$\\label{p3sw:descent-extraction}\n p_{s*}^B\\bigl(\\zeta_{B,s}^{\\,m-1}e^{-\\zeta_{B,s}}A_B(s)\\bigr)\n       =\\kappa_B(s),$$ where $p_{s*}^B$ is normalized Betti fiber integration.",
      "explanation": "A projective bundle cancels the twist, giving an ordinary semiregular coherent sheaf and a rational extraction identity for the character.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-projective-germ",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S=(S,0)$ be a smooth complex analytic germ, and let $\\pi:\\mathcal X\\to S$ be a proper holomorphic submersion of relative dimension $n$, equipped with a holomorphic $\\pi$-ample line $\\mathcal L_{\\mathcal X}$. Let $F_0$ be an ordinary coherent sheaf on $X_0$. For $I\\subseteq\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$, suppose the following raw semiregularity map is injective: $$\\label{p3sw:bf-seed-sigma}\n \\begin{split}\n \\sigma_{I,0}:\\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X_0}(F_0,F_0)&\\longrightarrow\n       \\bigoplus_{k\\in I}H^{k+1}(X_0,\\Omega_{X_0}^{k-1}),\\\\\n v&\\longmapsto\n       \\left(\\operatorname{Tr}_{F_0,\\Omega_{X_0}^{k-1}[k+1]}\n       \\left(\\left(\\frac{a_{F_0}^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\\right)[2]\\circ v\\right)\\right)_{k\\in I}\n \\end{split}$$ Suppose there are rational flat sections $\\beta_{B,k}$ of $R^{2k}\\pi_*\\mathbb Q$, for $k\\in I$, such that $$\\beta_{B,k}(0)=\\operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F_0),\\qquad\n \\beta_{B,k}\\in F^k(R^{2k}\\pi_*\\mathbb C\\otimes\\mathcal O_S).$$ The second condition means that the flat de Rham class itself lies in the indicated Hodge filtration. Then, after shrinking $S$, there is a coherent $S$-flat sheaf $F$ on $\\mathcal X$, with $F|_{X_0}\\simeq F_0$, for which $$\\label{p3sw:bf-fiber-character}\n \\operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F|_{X_s})=\\beta_{B,k}(s)\n       \\quad(s\\text{ near }0,\\ k\\in I).$$ These classes are rational algebraic cycle classes. No rank, simplicity, stability, local-freeness, reflexivity, or fixed-support hypothesis is imposed on $F_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },

Then, after shrinking $S$, there is a coherent $S$-flat sheaf $F$ on $\\mathcal X$, with $F|_{X_0}\\simeq F_0$, for which $$\\label{p3sw:bf-fiber-character}\n \\operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F|_{X_s})=\\beta_{B,k}(s)\n       \\quad(s\\text{ near }0,\\ k\\in I).$$ These classes are rational algebraic cycle classes.       "exact_statement": "Let $S=(S,0)$ be a smooth complex analytic germ, and let $\\pi:\\mathcal X\\to S$ be a proper holomorphic submersion of relative dimension $n$, equipped with a holomorphic $\\pi$-ample line $\\mathcal L_{\\mathcal X}$. Let $F_0$ be an ordinary coherent sheaf on $X_0$. For $I\\subseteq\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$, suppose the following raw semiregularity map is injective: $$\\label{p3sw:bf-seed-sigma}\n \\begin{split}\n \\sigma_{I,0}:\\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X_0}(F_0,F_0)&\\longrightarrow\n       \\bigoplus_{k\\in I}H^{k+1}(X_0,\\Omega_{X_0}^{k-1}),\\\\\n v&\\longmapsto\n       \\left(\\operatorname{Tr}_{F_0,\\Omega_{X_0}^{k-1}[k+1]}\n       \\left(\\left(\\frac{a_{F_0}^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\\right)[2]\\circ v\\right)\\right)_{k\\in I}\n \\end{split}$$ Suppose there are rational flat sections $\\beta_{B,k}$ of $R^{2k}\\pi_*\\mathbb Q$, for $k\\in I$, such that $$\\beta_{B,k}(0)=\\operatorname{ch}_{B,k}(F_0),\\qquad\n \\beta_{B,k}\\in F^k(R^{2k}\\pi_*\\mathbb C\\otimes\\mathcal O_S).$$ The second condition means that the flat de Rham class itself lies in the indicated Hodge filtration. No rank, simplicity, stability, local-freeness, reflexivity, or fixed-support hypothesis is imposed on $F_0$.",
      "explanation": "A semiregular coherent sheaf on a smooth projective germ deforms to a flat sheaf realizing prescribed flat Hodge-filtered characters, which are then algebraic.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-bf-coefficients",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A=A_\\nu$, $X=X_\\nu$, and let $E$ be an $A$-flat coherent sheaf on $X$ whose central fiber is $F_0$. Then $E$ is absolutely perfect as an $\\mathcal O_X$-complex, with local projective dimension at most $n$. If $W$ is a finite $A$-module killed by the maximal ideal and $W_X=\\mathcal O_X\\otimes_AW$, there are natural identifications $$\\label{p3sw:bf-coefficient-identities}\n \\begin{split}\n \\operatorname{Ext}^2_X(E,E\\otimes_AW)\n   &\\simeq\\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X_0}(F_0,F_0)\\otimes_\\mathbb CW,\\\\\n H^q(X,\\Omega^j_{X/A}\\otimes_AW)\n   &\\simeq H^q(X_0,\\Omega_{X_0}^j)\\otimes_\\mathbb CW.\n \\end{split}$$ Under them the coefficient semiregularity map $$\\sigma_{I,W}(v)=\n \\left(\\operatorname{Tr}_{E,(\\Omega_{X/A}^{k-1}\\otimes_AW)[k+1]}\n \\left(\\left(\\frac{a_{X/A}(E)^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\n       \\otimes_A^{\\mathbf L}1_W\\right)[2]\\circ v\\right)\\right)_{k\\in I}$$ is exactly $\\sigma_{I,0}\\otimes1_W$, and is therefore injective.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p3sw-bf-coefficients",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **action of an imaginary quadratic field** ([source](#p3:sec:introduction)): An action of an imaginary quadratic field $E$ on an abelian variety $A$ means a unital embedding $E\hookrightarrow\operatorname{End}^0(A)$.
- **compatible Riemann form** ([source](#p3:sec:introduction)): A rational Riemann form $e$ is compatible with the action if $e(ax,y)=e(x,\bar a y)$ for every $a\in E$.
- **hyperbolic rank-six Hermitian form** ([source](#p3:sec:introduction)): A rank-six Hermitian form is hyperbolic if it contains a totally isotropic three-dimensional $E$-subspace.
- **CM abelian variety** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Here and below a CM abelian variety means an abelian variety whose degree-one Hodge group is a torus.
- **CM-supplied subspace** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We say that a subspace of $H^d(X,k)$ is *CM-supplied* if it is contained in $\mathcal C^d(X)\otimes_\mathbb Qk$.
- **admissible subspace** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A subspace $W\subset H^1(X,k)$ is *admissible* if it is the image of a $k$-linear idempotent $e$ such that every $\gamma e$ preserves the Hodge decomposition after extension to $\mathbb C$.
- **pair tensor** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *pair tensor* between $W$ and $W'$ is a tensor $t\in W\otimes W'$ whose associated map $W^*\to W'$ is an isomorphism and for which every $\gamma t$ has Hodge type $(1,1)$.
- **balanced tensor** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A tensor in $H^{\otimes 2p}$ is called *balanced* if it has Hodge type $(p,p)$.

      "exact_definition": "A tensor in $H^{\\otimes 2p}$ is called *balanced* if it has Hodge type $(p,p)$.",
      "interpretation": "A tensor of the middle Hodge type, the tensor form of a Hodge class.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "balanced tensor",
      "scope": "Tensor criterion for powers."
    }
  ],

}

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cm-supply** [claim](#stmt-p3-cm-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3-cm-operations): Stability of supply under correspondences plus the CM Hodge theorem and a rational Hodge splitting -> CM-supplied Hodge classes are algebraic
- **step-admissible-algebraic** [claim](#stmt-p3-admissible-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3-admissible-operations): Scalar descent and the Lefschetz one-one theorem -> Admissible maps and pair tensors are algebraic
- **step-hodge-group-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-p3-tensor-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3-tensor-stabilizer): Chevalley line and complete reducibility -> Hodge group as stabilizer of balanced tensors
- **step-toric-cm-factor** [claim](#stmt-p3-toric-cm-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3-toric-cm-factor): Rational Hodge projector realized by an endomorphism and positivity of the involution -> Toric summand is a CM isogeny factor
- **step-tensor-criterion** [claim](#stmt-p3-tensor-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3-tensor-criterion): First fundamental theorems for classical groups plus simultaneous balance -> All balanced tensors algebraic under determinant supply
- **step-transfer** [claim](#stmt-p3-correspondence-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3-correspondence-transfer): Kunneth surjection and Hodge splitting -> Hodge conjecture on products from spanning correspondences
- **step-hyperbolic-test** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-hyperbolic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-hyperbolic): Isotropic vector splitting and Meyer theorem -> Hyperbolicity criterion by determinant class
- **step-weil-plane-input** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-weil-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-endpoint-all-lattices-2): Seed construction, deformation, spreading and isogeny -> Weil plane algebraic on hyperbolic sixfolds
- **step-determinant-supply** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-determinant-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-determinant-supply): Weil plane to separate slots, then padding by CM elliptic curves and contraction -> Eigenspace determinant lines are CM-supplied
- **step-albert-columns** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-albert-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-albert-columns): Albert classification and positivity of the Rosati involution -> Columns with pair tensors
- **step-minuscule** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-minuscule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-minuscule): Weyl orbit and root string argument on two circle weights -> Minuscule factor representations
- **step-small-projections** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-small-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-small-projections): Minuscule table and Schur lemma -> Full classical derived images
- **step-small-columns** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-small-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-small-columns): Case analysis by Albert type and divisibility -> Short list of columns
- **step-independence** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-independence): Exclusion of graph links by circle weight and Galois arguments -> Product derived image
- **step-main-theorems** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-split-sixfolds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-independence-3): Balance equations and application of the tensor criterion -> Hodge conjecture on all self-powers
- **step-low-dimension** [claim](#stmt-p3ab-quadratic-five) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3ab-independence-2): Same criterion in low dimension -> Hodge conjecture for small quadratic actions
- **step-seed** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-seed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-support-finite-model-2): Compact-type degeneration and vanishing on the elliptic star -> Abel orbits with uniform vanishing
- **step-trilinear** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-support-trilinear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-support-trilinear): Pencil of matrices and determinant derivative -> Bound on singular marked points
- **step-star** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-support-elliptic-star) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-support-elliptic-star): Reduce sections to trilinear forms -> Vanishing on the star
- **step-finite-model** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-support-finite-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-support-finite-model): Cech complex of flat modules -> Finite model with base change
- **step-relative-kernel** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-relative-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-relative-kernel): Local flatness criterion and Tor vanishing -> Flat ideal kernel
- **step-dual-sheaf** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-coherent-sheaf) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-coherent-sheaf): Fiber cohomology vanishing and dualizing a two-term model -> Coherent perfect sheaf
- **step-quotient-action** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-coherent-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-coherent-action): Conjugation of source translations by the transform -> Line-twisted action and quotient
- **step-trace-identity** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle): Normalized HKR on the formal diagonal -> Trace identity
- **step-transport** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-hh-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-hh-transport): Serre pairings and kernel conjugation -> Transport of kernels
- **step-invariant-rank** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-injectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-injectivity): Equality of dimension and rank -> Injectivity on the full invariant group
- **step-ext-dimension** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-ext) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-ext): Kunneth for derived Hom and local-to-global spectral sequence -> Dimension of invariant Ext
- **step-abel-orbit-ext** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext): Local Ext computation and equivariant splitting -> Orbit Ext representation
- **step-abel-character** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-abel-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-abel-character): Riemann-Roch and normal sequence -> Character and normal sections
- **step-product-rank** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-product-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-product-rank): Kunneth blocks in distinct imbalance summands -> Rank of product contraction
- **step-one-factor-rank** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-invariant-one-factor-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-one-factor-rank): Linear algebra with the polarization class -> Rank of one-factor contraction
- **step-spin** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-spin-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-spin-identity): Finite Clifford calculation -> Spin identity
- **step-fourier** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-fourier-matrix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-fourier-matrix): Sign count on exterior monomials -> Fourier matrix
- **step-polarized-seed** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-polarized-seed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-polarized-seed): Annihilators of exponential spinors -> Action, polarization, hyperbolic form
- **step-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-ordered-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-ordered-stabilizer): Weight calculation for special linear group -> Invariants
- **step-exceptional** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-exceptional-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-exceptional-character): Source tensor not fixed by ordered stabilizer -> Nonzero Weil component
- **step-hodge-circle** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-hodge-circle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-hodge-circle): Hodge circle lies in the spinor stabilizer image -> Character is Hodge
- **step-twisted-descent** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-twisted-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-twisted-descent): Determinant normalization and etale descent -> Twisted semiregular sheaf
- **step-descent-duality** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-descent-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-descent-duality): Transpose and Atiyah sign count -> Dual invariant isomorphism
- **step-ordinary** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-ordinary-conversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-ordinary-conversion): Projective bundle from a finite Heisenberg-type representation -> Ordinary semiregular sheaf
- **step-bf-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-bf-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-bf-coefficients): Minimal resolutions and flatness -> Coefficient obstruction groups
- **step-bf-one-step** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-bf-one-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-bf-one-step): Atiyah obstruction and Gauss-Manin square -> Square-zero lift
- **step-bf-character** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-bf-artin-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-bf-artin-character): Smooth jets and Chern-Weil -> Character of every lift
- **step-germ** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-projective-germ) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-bf-artin-character-2): Compatible quotients, analytic Quot space and analytic approximation -> Analytic flat deformation
- **step-local** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-local-algebraicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-local-algebraicity): Deform on the projective bundle then extract by pushforward -> Local algebraicity
- **step-two-generators** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-endpoint-two-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-endpoint-two-generators): Algebraic endomorphism separates the two eigenlines -> Plane from one vector
- **step-spread** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-spread) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-spread): Relative Hilbert schemes, proper image and Baire -> Algebraicity everywhere on the domain
- **step-all-lattices** [claim](#stmt-p3sw-endpoint-all-lattices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p3sw-endpoint-all-lattices): Hyperbolic isometry and a lattice-clearing isogeny -> All hyperbolic lattices

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-hyperbolic-norm-test**: A nondegenerate Hermitian form over an imaginary quadratic field, of even rank and split signature, is hyperbolic if and only if its determinant class matches the alternating sign class in the determinant classes modulo norms from the field.
- **inv-hyperbolic-plane-sum**: Every hyperbolic form of this rank is isometric to an orthogonal sum of $b$ hyperbolic planes.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-abstract-form**: Apply the determinant test to an abstract form of the same complex signature instead of the Hermitian form coming from the actual polarization. -> The verdict can differ from that of the actual homological form, so the Weil-plane hypothesis is not established.
- **anti-signature**: Use the criterion on a degenerate form or on a form whose signature is not of type $(b,b)$. -> The equivalence between hyperbolicity and the determinant class is not asserted outside this regime.
- **anti-field**: Run the norm test with a field that is not imaginary quadratic, such as a real quadratic field. -> The norm form is then indefinite and the lemma gives no verdict.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The lemma gives a finite decision test for hyperbolicity of a split-signature Hermitian form over an imaginary quadratic field: decide whether the signed determinant is a rational norm from the field. This is computed exactly with Hilbert symbols at the finitely many primes dividing twice the field parameter times the determinant. Only this lemma is made executable; the existence theorems of the paper are not finite procedures.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin one line 'd b a_1 ... a_2b' with d a positive integer, b >= 1, a_i nonzero integers (diagonal of a Hermitian form over Q(sqrt(-d)))
# PRECONDITIONS: exactly b of the a_i are positive and b are negative (signature (b,b)); form nondegenerate
# OUTPUTS: one line HYPERBOLIC or NOT_HYPERBOLIC, the Lemma 10 test that (-1)^b times the determinant is a norm from E
# TIME: trial division of 2*d*|det| plus one Hilbert symbol per prime factor
# SPACE: O(number of prime factors)
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers; Hilbert symbols at the primes dividing 2*d*det
# FAILURE: exit status 2 with no output on malformed input or violated precondition
import sys

def split(x, p):
    k = 0
    while x % p == 0:
        x //= p
        k += 1
    return k, x

def leg(u, p):
    return 1 if pow(u % p, (p - 1) // 2, p) == 1 else -1

def ep(t):
    return ((t - 1) // 2) % 2

def om(t):
    return ((t * t - 1) // 8) % 2

def hilbert(a, c, p):
    al, u = split(a, p)
    be, v = split(c, p)
    if p == 2:
        s = (ep(u) * ep(v) + al * om(v) + be * om(u)) % 2
        return -1 if s else 1
    s = (al * be * ((p - 1) // 2)) % 2
    return (-1) ** s * leg(u, p) ** be * leg(v, p) ** al

def prime_factors(n):
    n = abs(n)
    out = []
    q = 2
    while q * q <= n:
        if n % q == 0:
            out.append(q)
            while n % q == 0:
                n //= q
        q += 1
    if n > 1:
        out.append(n)
    return out

def main():
    try:
        t = [int(w) for w in sys.stdin.read().split()]
        d, b, a = t[0], t[1], t[2:]
        ok = d > 0 and b >= 1 and len(a) == 2 * b and all(x != 0 for x in a)
        ok = ok and sum(1 for x in a if x > 0) == b
    except Exception:
        sys.exit(2)
    if not ok:
        sys.exit(2)
    prod = 1
    for x in a:
        prod *= x
    sgn = (-1) ** b
    n = sgn * prod
    primes = prime_factors(2 * d * n)
    syms = [hilbert(n, -d, p) for p in primes]
    print('HYPERBOLIC' if all(h == 1 for h in syms) else 'NOT_HYPERBOLIC')

main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main results assert that every Hodge class on self-powers of the stated abelian varieties is algebraic. The proofs use sheaf deformation, descent and Hilbert-scheme spreading, and give no finite procedure that a program could execute. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is pure complex algebraic geometry on abelian varieties and has no dynamical system, state update or physical model that a simulation could use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A computer-algebra tool that computes the algebraic cycle space cannot be built from this paper. Algebraicity is shown by non-effective coherent-sheaf deformation and a Baire-category spreading argument rather than by equations of cycles. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Some steps are computable on their own, such as the determinant-class test for hyperbolicity of a Hermitian form. They are auxiliary lemmas inside an existence proof, and the paper states no algorithm, input contract or complexity for them. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data organisation, index or container is defined. The objects are Hodge groups, tensors and determinant lines, which are mathematical structures and not storage layouts. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no numerical kernel, arithmetic circuit or parallel workload that hardware could accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although CM abelian varieties and imaginary quadratic fields appear in cryptography elsewhere, the paper makes no hardness, security or protocol claim. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, data distribution, loss or model. The paper only proves statements about cycle classes and Hodge structures. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective function, constraint system or feasibility problem is posed. The statements are existence and algebraicity results. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Galois conjugation and group actions on sheaves are algebraic notions, not communication, consensus or replication mechanisms. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to pure mathematics, specifically the Hodge conjecture for abelian varieties. It is a theoretical foundation for further algebraic geometry and has no engineering construction attached. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-actual-hyperbolic-form** (hypothesis): The split sixfold theorem needs a compatible polarization whose rational Hermitian form, the one coming from the actual polarization, is hyperbolic of split signature. A form that is only abstractly of the same complex signature does not qualify. Blocked use: ['Applying the all-powers theorem to a sixfold whose actual Hermitian form is not hyperbolic', 'Replacing the actual homological form by an abstract form of the same complex signature', 'Applying it to a sixfold with a different signature']
- **limit-dimension-cap-quadratic-action** (hypothesis): The second theorem covers only abelian varieties of dimension at most five that admit an action of an imaginary quadratic field. No hyperbolicity condition is needed there. Blocked use: ['Applying it to abelian varieties of dimension six or more without the hyperbolic hypothesis', 'Applying it to abelian varieties of any dimension with no imaginary quadratic action']
- **limit-self-powers-of-given-variety** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns every codimension on every self-power of the given variety. It is not a statement about arbitrary products with other varieties, apart from the CM factors the criterion explicitly allows. Blocked use: ['Reading the theorem as a Hodge-conjecture statement for products with arbitrary non-CM varieties', 'Reading it as a statement for varieties outside the two stated families']
- **limit-rational-coefficients** (formal_scope): The Hodge conjecture is proved only for rational coefficients and rational algebraic cycles. Nothing is asserted about integral classes or torsion. Blocked use: ['Citing the results as the integral Hodge conjecture for these varieties', 'Using them to conclude algebraicity of torsion cohomology classes']
- **limit-cm-input-dependence** (hypothesis): Every algebraicity conclusion rests on the companion theorem for CM abelian varieties, and the proof of CM-supply algebraicity applies it directly. The results are conditional on that input. Blocked use: ['Using the all-powers theorems independently of the companion CM theorem', 'Treating CM-supplied classes as algebraic without that input']
- **limit-balanced-products-only** (hypothesis): The determinant criterion requires CM supply only for lists of determinant lines that satisfy the balance equations in every Galois conjugate. It does not give supply for an arbitrary individual determinant, and it requires the stated data on the derived image and the CM lines to exist. Blocked use: ['Assuming each individual determinant line is CM-supplied', 'Applying the criterion without checking the independence of the derived images', 'Applying it without checking the balance in every conjugate']
- **limit-determinant-supply-ranges** (edge_case): Quadratic determinant supply by padding with CM elliptic curves is proved for the split hyperbolic sixfold and for dimension below six with both signature entries at most three. Odd-rank factors outside that range are not supplied individually, and the paper treats them by cancellation of net exponents. Blocked use: ['Claiming individual determinant supply for a factor with a signature entry exceeding three', 'Using the padding construction when the padded form cannot be made hyperbolic']
- **limit-weil-plane-not-all-classes** (counterexample_scope): The Weil-plane theorem supplies the rational Weil two-plane and not all Hodge classes at a special period. The remaining classes need the tensor criterion and the CM input. Blocked use: ['Citing the Weil-plane theorem alone as the Hodge conjecture on the sixfold itself', 'Using it for classes outside the Weil plane at periods with extra endomorphisms']
- **limit-hyperbolicity-test-scope** (hypothesis): The determinant-class test for hyperbolicity is stated for an imaginary quadratic base field and for nondegenerate forms of split signature. It is not claimed for other fields or signatures. Blocked use: ['Running the norm-class test over a real quadratic or other field', 'Running it on degenerate forms or forms without split signature']
- **limit-seed-parameter-range** (hypothesis): The geometric seed is constructed only for an integer parameter at least three. Other imaginary quadratic fields are reached by rescaling the generator, which preserves the isotropic subspaces. Blocked use: ['Using the seed construction directly for a parameter below three', 'Rescaling the generator in a way that does not preserve the isotropic subspaces']
- **limit-germ-deformation-local** (approximation): The deformation theorem is local. It produces a flat sheaf only after shrinking the base germ, and it needs a smooth germ with a proper submersion carrying a relatively ample line. Blocked use: ['Reading the deformation as a global family over the whole period domain', 'Quoting an explicit radius of the neighbourhood where it works', 'Applying it to non-submersive or non-projective families']
- **limit-spread-connected-base** (nonconstructive): Spreading algebraicity uses a connected complex manifold as base and algebraicity on a nonempty open subset. The conclusion is by Baire category over countably many Hilbert scheme choices, so it gives existence and no cycle-producing procedure. Blocked use: ['Applying the spreading statement to a disconnected base', 'Extracting explicit cycles or equations at a given period from it']
- **limit-no-explicit-cycle-bounds** (unknown): The paper gives no explicit equations, degrees or complexity bounds for the algebraic cycles whose existence it proves. Blocked use: ['Quoting degree or complexity bounds for the cycles', 'Using the existence proofs as a cycle-finding algorithm']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the rational Hodge conjecture in every codimension on every self-power of a complex abelian sixfold with an imaginary-quadratic action and a compatible polarization whose rational homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic of signature $`(3,3)`$. We also prove it in every codimension on every self-power of a complex abelian variety of dimension at most five admitting an imaginary-quadratic action. Both results include nonsimple varieties and special periods with additional endomorphisms. The proof uses the companion theorem on the rational Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties.

<a id="p3:sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the rational Hodge conjecture asks whether every class in $$H^{2p}(X,\mathbb Q)\cap H^{p,p}(X)$$ is the class of a rational algebraic cycle of codimension $p$. For an abelian variety, the question on every self-power is naturally a question about tensors in its first cohomology: classes can couple several copies even when the classes on the variety itself are already understood. This paper proves the conjecture on all self-powers in two families with an imaginary-quadratic action.

An action of an imaginary quadratic field $E$ on an abelian variety $A$ means a unital embedding $E\hookrightarrow\operatorname{End}^0(A)$. Write $E=\mathbb Q(u)$, with $u^2=-d$ and $d\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$. A rational Riemann form $e$ is compatible with the action if $e(ax,y)=e(x,\bar a y)$ for every $a\in E$. Its associated homological Hermitian form is $$h_E(x,y)=e(x,uy)+u e(x,y),\qquad x,y\in H_1(A,\mathbb Q),$$ conjugate-linear in the first variable and linear in the second. A rank-six Hermitian form is hyperbolic if it contains a totally isotropic three-dimensional $E$-subspace. The form in the next theorem is the one coming from the actual polarization, not merely an abstract form of the same complex signature.

<a id="stmt-p3ab-split-sixfolds"></a>
<div id="p3ab:split-sixfolds" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Split Weil sixfolds). *Let $E$ be an imaginary quadratic field and let $A$ be a complex abelian sixfold with an $E$-action. Suppose a compatible polarization has hyperbolic rational Hermitian form of signature $(3,3)$. Then the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on $A^m$, for every $m\geq1$.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-p3ab-quadratic-five"></a>
<div id="p3ab:quadratic-five" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Quadratic actions in dimension at most five). *Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety of dimension at most five admitting an action of an imaginary quadratic field. The rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on $A^m$, for every $m\geq1$.*

</div>

Both statements include nonsimple varieties and periods with additional endomorphisms. The quadratic action need not be the full endomorphism algebra or be central on a repeated simple factor. The second theorem requires no separately chosen compatible polarization: one is obtained by averaging any rational Riemann form, as shown in Section <a href="#p3:sec:abelian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:abelian">3</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Weil classes and earlier results

Weil’s determinant construction <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Weil1979](#ref-Weil1979)] provides a basic test of the Hodge conjecture beyond divisor classes. For an abelian variety of dimension $2n$ with a compatible imaginary-quadratic action of signature $(n,n)$, the two embedding determinants form a rational plane of classes of type $(n,n)$. For a general such variety with $n>1$, these classes are not generated by divisors; see <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[vanGeemenWeilNotes](#ref-vanGeemenWeilNotes), Sections 4.9–4.11]. Early geometric constructions used automorphisms of curves and Prym varieties <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Schoen1988](#ref-Schoen1988)]. Schoen’s addendum corrected the polarization hypothesis in the earlier fourfold statement and established algebraicity of the Weil plane for every fourfold with balanced action by $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})$, without a discriminant restriction <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Schoen1998Addendum](#ref-Schoen1998Addendum)].

Markman’s secant-sheaf construction proves algebraicity of the whole Weil plane for every split Weil sixfold. The preprint states the result for discriminant $-1$, while the published account uses the equivalent split condition on the rational Hermitian form of the compatible polarization <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Markman2025Secant](#ref-Markman2025Secant), Theorem 1.5.1]<a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Markman2026ICM](#ref-Markman2026ICM), Theorem 1.2]. The published account also proves the Hodge conjecture for every individual abelian variety of dimension at most five, with no quadratic-action hypothesis <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Markman2026ICM](#ref-Markman2026ICM), Corollary 1.3]. These are all-period statements. The further question in our main theorems concerns all Hodge classes on every self-power, including the extra tensors that can occur when the Hodge group drops at a special period.

The all-powers question also has substantial precedents. Moonen and Zarhin’s low-dimensional classification includes all-powers divisor-generation results, as well as a fourfold case whose square has exceptional classes <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[MoonenZarhin1999](#ref-MoonenZarhin1999), Theorems 0.1–0.2]. For every Weil fourfold of discriminant one, Floccari proves the Hodge conjecture on all powers through the Kuga–Satake correspondence <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Floccari2026K3](#ref-Floccari2026K3), Theorems 1.3 and 5.12]. Floccari and Fu give another proof using singular varieties of O’Grady’s six-dimensional type <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[FloccariFu2026](#ref-FloccariFu2026), Theorem 1.2]. Our argument addresses the two stated families by combining the split-sixfold Weil plane with a determinant criterion for the actual Hodge group and the CM algebraicity input specified below.

The geometric proof is a detailed treatment of Markman’s construction, rather than an extension of its period range. It uses Mukai’s Fourier–Mukai duality and the algebraic spin interpretation of Golyshev–Lunts–Orlov <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[P3SWMukai1981](#ref-P3SWMukai1981), [GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001](#ref-GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001)]. We work out the coherent seed, the fixed transform conventions, the full invariant derived obstruction space, and its passage through quotient descent and projective-bundle conversion. The full-group comparison and descent are already part of Markman’s method <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Markman2025Secant](#ref-Markman2025Secant), Section 7.4.2 and Lemmas 9.3.2 and 9.3.11]<a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Markman2026ICM](#ref-Markman2026ICM), Sections 11.3–11.5]. The Hochschild–Atiyah comparison follows the character and evaluation identities of Căldăraru, Buchweitz–Flenner and Huang <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[P3HHCaldararuII](#ref-P3HHCaldararuII), [P3HHBF](#ref-P3HHBF), [P3HHHuang](#ref-P3HHHuang)], with the abelian specialization of Todd-corrected compatibility <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[P3HHCRVdB](#ref-P3HHCRVdB)]. The deformation argument reconstructs the smooth-projective-germ case of the coherent-sheaf argument in Buchweitz–Flenner’s Theorem 5.1 and its proof <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[P3SWBuchweitzFlenner2003](#ref-P3SWBuchweitzFlenner2003)]. The needed injectivity is on the full obstruction group, not merely the image of Hochschild evaluation.

The separate passage from the Weil plane to all powers uses classical invariant theory to reduce tensors to pairings and determinants <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[LehrerZhang2015](#ref-LehrerZhang2015), [LehrerZhang2017](#ref-LehrerZhang2017), [Schrijver2008](#ref-Schrijver2008)]. The two-weight restriction is the standard minuscule argument <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Serre1979HodgeTate](#ref-Serre1979HodgeTate), Section 3.2, Lemmas 4–5]; comparison of product Hodge groups through graph links has a close predecessor in Moonen–Zarhin <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[MoonenZarhin1999](#ref-MoonenZarhin1999), Section 3.1 and Lemmas 3.3–3.4]. Adjoining auxiliary factors and extracting lower-dimensional Weil classes also appears in Schoen’s Proposition 10 . Here CM elliptic factors supply smaller quadratic determinants, while the actual-group analysis identifies the products selected by the simultaneous balance equations. Those supply and balance steps, rather than the invariant spanning theorems alone, give the all-powers conclusions.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Let $H=H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$. A tensor in $H^{\otimes 2p}$ is balanced if it has type $(p,p)$. For $A^m$, antisymmetrization lifts a Hodge class to a balanced tensor in $(H^{\oplus m})^{\otimes 2p}$, with the copies labelled by the factors of the power. Diagonal pullback returns the exterior class. Section <a href="#p3:sec:tensors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:tensors">2</a> gives a criterion for algebraicity of these tensors. The multilinear invariant theorems for symplectic, orthogonal, and special linear groups reduce the tensors to pairings and top exterior lines <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[LehrerZhang2015](#ref-LehrerZhang2015), [LehrerZhang2017](#ref-LehrerZhang2017), [Schrijver2008](#ref-Schrijver2008)]. Pairings are divisor classes. The required products of the remaining lines are supplied by algebraic correspondences from the full cohomology of CM abelian varieties. A rational Hodge class in such a supplied subspace lifts to rational Hodge classes on the CM sources; Theorem 1.1 of *The rational Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties*, dated October 6, 2026, makes those lifts algebraic <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[OpenAICM2026](#ref-OpenAICM2026)]. This is the precise CM input used in this paper.

Section <a href="#p3:sec:abelian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:abelian">3</a> proves the two main theorems from the Weil-plane input, whose geometric proof follows in Sections <a href="#p3sw:appendix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:appendix">4</a>–<a href="#p3:sec:all-periods" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:all-periods">8</a>. Adding CM elliptic curves supplies smaller quadratic determinants whose signature entries are at most three. The Albert decomposition, the two Hodge-circle weights, and the small minuscule representations determine the possible constituents of the actual derived Hodge group. A separate independence argument identifies their simultaneous product. The balance equations then cancel opposite determinants or combine them into a supplied quadratic determinant. This is how the proof accommodates additional endomorphisms at special periods.

The cycle construction in Section <a href="#p3sw:appendix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:appendix">4</a> begins with a genus-three Jacobian $X$. Disjoint orbits of Abel curves give an ideal sheaf on $X^2$; a fixed Fourier–Mukai transform and duality produce a coherent perfect sheaf $\mathcal E$ on $X\times\widehat X$, together with a coherent finite translation action twisted by line bundles. The normalized Chern character of this sheaf is the candidate cycle class. Two properties are needed: it must contain a nonzero Weil component and remain of Hodge type as the Weil period varies; and, after descent and a projective-bundle construction, an associated ordinary sheaf must deform so that the normalized character is algebraic near the initial point.

Section <a href="#p3:sec:obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:obstruction">5</a> proves the exact Hochschild–Atiyah trace identity and its transport for the fixed transform. A direct equivariant Ext calculation then shows that the evaluation image equals the entire invariant obstruction space, on which the trace map is injective. Section <a href="#p3:sec:spin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:spin">6</a> computes the rational Betti character by a finite Clifford calculation. It identifies the actual ample polarization, proves that its homological Hermitian form is hyperbolic, and shows that the character is Hodge throughout the compatible marked domain.

Section <a href="#p3:sec:deformation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:deformation">7</a> descends the sheaf through the finite quotient and converts the resulting twisted sheaf into an ordinary sheaf on a projective bundle without losing any obstruction class. Its semiregularity theorem produces the required local deformations. Section <a href="#p3:sec:all-periods" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:sec:all-periods">8</a> extracts the normalized character by algebraic projective-bundle operations, spreads the resulting Weil plane through proper Hilbert parameter spaces, and clears rational lattice denominators by isogenies. This proves algebraicity of the whole rational Weil plane at every compatible hyperbolic period.

<a id="p3:sec:tensors"></a>
### Algebraic supply and invariant tensors

The cohomology of an abelian variety is generated by its first cohomology. We will therefore prove algebraicity first for tensors in degree one. Classical invariant theory reduces these tensors to pairings and top exterior powers. Pairings are divisor classes. The remaining task in each application is to supply the required products of top exterior powers by algebraic correspondences from CM abelian varieties.

All varieties are complex, and cohomology is singular cohomology with the indicated coefficients. An algebraic correspondence has rational coefficients unless otherwise stated. We fix $k=\overline{\mathbb Q}\subset\mathbb C$. An automorphism of $k/\mathbb Q$ acts only on coefficients in the cohomology of the fixed complex variety; it does not replace that variety by a conjugate. For a pure Hodge structure $V$, our convention is $V(r)^{p,q}=V^{p+r,q+r}$, so the Tate twist $V(r)$ has weight $\operatorname{wt}(V)-2r$. A class is *algebraic over $k$* if it belongs to the $k$-linear span of rational algebraic cycle classes.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Correspondences from CM abelian varieties

For a smooth projective variety $X$, let $$\mathcal C^d(X)=
 \sum_{B,j,\Gamma}
 \operatorname{im}\bigl(
 \Gamma_*:H^{d-2j}(B,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^d(X,\mathbb Q)\bigr),
 \qquad
 \Gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^{\dim B+j}(B\times X)_\mathbb Q,$$ where $B$ ranges over CM abelian varieties, including a point, and $j$ ranges over integers for which the displayed groups and cycles exist. Here and below a CM abelian variety means an abelian variety whose degree-one Hodge group is a torus. The displayed map is a morphism of weight-$d$ Hodge structures from $H^{d-2j}(B,\mathbb Q)(-j)$. We say that a subspace of $H^d(X,k)$ is *CM-supplied* if it is contained in $\mathcal C^d(X)\otimes_\mathbb Qk$. Thus supply concerns the full cohomology of the CM source, not only its Hodge classes.

We use the companion theorem that the rational Hodge conjecture holds for every complex CM abelian variety in every codimension <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[OpenAICM2026](#ref-OpenAICM2026), Theorem 1.1]. This is the only Hodge-conjecture input in the following lemma.

<a id="stmt-p3-cm-operations"></a>
<div id="p3:cm-operations" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Operations and Hodge lifts). *CM supply is preserved by external products, permutations of factors, cup products, and rational algebraic correspondences. It is also preserved by $k$-linear combinations of such correspondences and by scalar conjugation. Every rational Hodge class in a CM-supplied subspace is algebraic.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3-cm-operations"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3-cm-operations</summary>

*Proof.* External products of correspondence cycles realize products of their maps, and a product of CM abelian varieties is CM. Composition is realized by pulling two cycles to the triple product, intersecting, and pushing forward. Graphs give pullback and pushforward. Diagonal pullback gives cup product; adding integration gives contraction. These operations are defined for the smooth projective varieties in question. Linearity proves the scalar assertion, and $\mathcal C^d(X)\otimes k$ is invariant under scalar conjugation because $\mathcal C^d(X)$ is rational.

Let $\alpha\in H^{2p}(X,\mathbb Q)$ have type $(p,p)$ and belong after scalar extension to $\mathcal C^{2p}(X)\otimes k$. Membership in a rational subspace descends under a field extension, so $\alpha$ already belongs to $\mathcal C^{2p}(X)$. Finitely many of the defining correspondence maps have an image containing $\alpha$. They give a morphism $$f:\bigoplus_{\nu=1}^s
 H^{2p-2j_\nu}(B_\nu,\mathbb Q)(-j_\nu)
 \longrightarrow H^{2p}(X,\mathbb Q)$$ of polarizable pure Hodge structures. This morphism splits onto its image as a rational Hodge morphism: the orthogonal complement of $\ker f$ for a polarization on the source is a Hodge substructure and maps isomorphically to $\operatorname{im}f$. Nondegeneracy on $\ker f$ follows from the positive Hermitian form obtained using the Weil operator. Hence $\alpha$ has a rational Hodge lift. Its $\nu$th component before twisting has type $(p-j_\nu,p-j_\nu)$ on $B_\nu$. The CM Hodge theorem makes this component algebraic. Applying the defining correspondence cycles and adding proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Degree-one summands and pairings

<a id="p3:eq:scalar-descent"></a>
We record the coefficient descent needed to pass from complex eigenspaces to rational algebraic maps. If $T$ is a finite-dimensional rational vector space, $P\subset T_\mathbb C$ is complex-linear, and $t\in T_k$ satisfies $\gamma t\in P$ for every $\gamma\in\operatorname{Gal}(k/\mathbb Q)$, then $$\label{p3:eq:scalar-descent}
 t\in (T\cap P)\otimes_\mathbb Qk.$$ Indeed, choose a finite Galois field $K$ containing its coefficients. For $a\in K$, the trace $\operatorname{Tr}_{K/\mathbb Q}(at)=
\sum_{\tau\in\operatorname{Gal}(K/\mathbb Q)}\tau(a)\tau(t)$ is rational and belongs to $P$. If $b_j$ is a basis of $K$ and $b_j^*$ is its trace-dual basis, these traces for $a=b_j^*$ are exactly the rational coefficients of $t$ in the basis $b_j$. Here each $\tau$ is extended to $k$ when applying the hypothesis. This proves <a href="#p3:eq:scalar-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3:eq:scalar-descent">[p3:eq:scalar-descent]</a>. Conditions that a tensor have a specified Hodge type, or that a map preserve Hodge types, are complex-linear conditions, so the observation applies to them.

Let $X$ be an abelian variety or a smooth connected projective curve. A subspace $W\subset H^1(X,k)$ is *admissible* if it is the image of a $k$-linear idempotent $e$ such that every $\gamma e$ preserves the Hodge decomposition after extension to $\mathbb C$. A $k$-linear map between such spaces, or between finite external direct sums of them, is *admissible* if it preserves the Hodge decomposition after every scalar conjugation. We write $\overline W$ for the scalar complex conjugate of $W$.

A *pair tensor* between $W$ and $W'$ is a tensor $t\in W\otimes W'$ whose associated map $W^*\to W'$ is an isomorphism and for which every $\gamma t$ has Hodge type $(1,1)$. For a tensor on $W$ itself, symmetry or alternation refers to the ordinary interchange of tensor factors, before the Koszul sign in product cohomology.

<a id="stmt-p3-admissible-operations"></a>
<div id="p3:admissible-operations" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Algebraic maps and contractions). *Every admissible map is induced on the indicated summands by a $k$-linear combination of rational algebraic correspondences. Every pair tensor is algebraic over $k$, and contraction by its inverse pairing is an algebraic cohomological operation. If $W$ is admissible, so is $\overline W$, and there is a pair tensor between $W$ and $\overline W$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3-admissible-operations"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3-admissible-operations</summary>

*Proof.* For an admissible map, compose with the source projector and the target inclusion to obtain a map on the ambient degree-one spaces. Equation <a href="#p3:eq:scalar-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3:eq:scalar-descent">[p3:eq:scalar-descent]</a> expresses it as a $k$-linear combination of rational Hodge maps. A rational Hodge map between the first cohomologies of abelian varieties is induced by a rational homomorphism in the contravariant direction: dualize to homology, multiply to preserve the lattices, and use the resulting complex-linear map of complex tori. A homomorphism of abelian varieties is algebraic. For a curve target use its Abel map to its Jacobian. For a curve source, the cup pairing identifies a degree-one Hodge map with a rational $(1,1)$ class on the product, which is a divisor class by the Lefschetz $(1,1)$ theorem. These arguments also apply componentwise to external direct sums. In particular an admissible projector commutes with the scalar extension of the rational Hodge group.

A pair tensor lies in the $H^1\otimes H^1$ summand of degree two on the product. Scalar descent and the Lefschetz $(1,1)$ theorem make it algebraic over $k$. Let $b:W\otimes W'\to k$ be its inverse pairing, with its bidegree $(-1,-1)$ understood. Extend $b$ by the admissible projectors. For a rational polarization pairing $\psi_X$ on the source $H^1(X,\mathbb Q)$ there is a unique map $u:H^1(X',k)\to H^1(X,k)$ satisfying $b(x,y)=\psi_X(x,u(y))$. In every scalar conjugate, $b$ pairs only complementary Hodge degrees; nondegeneracy of $\psi_X$ therefore makes $u$ an admissible map. The first part algebraizes $u$. The remaining contraction by $\psi_X$ is cup product, an ample power if needed, and integration. It is algebraic.

The conjugate projector shows that $\overline W$ is admissible. The restriction of $\psi_X$ to $W\times\overline W$ is perfect. After extension to $\mathbb C$, the positive Hermitian form associated with the polarization detects every nonzero vector of the Hodge-stable space $W_\mathbb C$ by pairing it with the conjugate of a vector of $W_\mathbb C$. Its inverse gives a tensor in $W\otimes\overline W$. For an arbitrary $\gamma$, conjugate the nonzero determinant of this restricted pairing. The resulting pairing on $\gamma W\times\gamma\overline W$ is still perfect and pairs complementary Hodge degrees, since both spaces are Hodge-stable and $\psi_X$ is rational. Its inverse has type $(1,1)$. This argument does not assume that $\gamma$ commutes with complex conjugation. ◻

</details>

For an admissible space $W$ of dimension $r$, write $$D(W)=\bigwedge^r W\subset W^{\otimes r}
       \subset H^r(X^r,k)$$ for its top alternating line on separate tensor slots; put $D(0)=k$ on a point. Ordinary permutations differ from geometric permutations only by rational Koszul signs. Direct-sum factorization and cancellation of these determinant lines can therefore be performed algebraically. More precisely, if an admissible isomorphism identifies $W$ with $\bigoplus_j W_j$, alternation and the admissible inclusions and projections induce nonzero maps in both directions between $D(W)$ and $\bigotimes_jD(W_j)$. If $W,W'$ have a pair tensor, the determinant of the inverse pairing contracts $D(W)\otimes D(W')$ nontrivially to the unit line. Conversely, alternating the appropriate tensor power of the pair tensor supplies $D(W)\otimes D(W')$ algebraically. For an alternating self-pair on a $2r$-space, the $r$th exterior power supplies $D(W)$ algebraically. These statements follow in bases from the nonzero determinant, or Pfaffian, of the pair tensor and from Lemma <a href="#p3:admissible-operations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3:admissible-operations">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The tensor criterion

For a polarizable rational Hodge structure $H$ of weight one whose only types are $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$, let $J=\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ be the smallest rational algebraic subgroup of $\operatorname{GL}(H)$ containing the Hodge circle, which acts on $H^{1,0}$ and $H^{0,1}$ by $z$ and $\bar z$ for $|z|=1$. It lies in the symplectic group of the polarization. A tensor in $H^{\otimes 2p}$ is called *balanced* if it has Hodge type $(p,p)$. The group $J$ is connected: its identity component is rational and contains the connected circle, so minimality applies. It is reductive: a rational invariant subspace is a Hodge substructure, and polarizability supplies a rational Hodge complement, which is again invariant by minimality. The faithful representation on $H$ is thus completely reducible.

<a id="stmt-p3-tensor-stabilizer"></a>
<div id="p3:tensor-stabilizer" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Hodge tensors determine the Hodge group). *Inside the symplectic group of $H$, the group $\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is the pointwise stabilizer of all rational tensors of type $(a,a)$ in $H^{\otimes 2a}$, for all $a\ge0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3-tensor-stabilizer"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3-tensor-stabilizer</summary>

*Proof.* By minimality, the rational $J$-invariant tensors are precisely the rational tensors fixed by the Hodge circle. To see that these determine $J$, use Chevalley’s theorem to choose a rational representation $T$ of $\operatorname{GL}(H)$ and a line $L\subset T$ whose stabilizer is $J$ <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[MilneAG2022](#ref-MilneAG2022), Theorem 4.27]. Reductivity gives a $J$-equivariant projector $e:T\to L$. Rational representations of $\operatorname{GL}(H)$ are subquotients of sums of mixed tensor powers of $H$ <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[MilneAG2022](#ref-MilneAG2022), Theorem 4.14].
