On $K^{-1}$, the extension <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension">[p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension]</a> is represented by $e_i\mapsto dz_i$. The sign can be checked by mapping $K^0=\mathcal R$ to $\mathcal R/(z)^2$ and $K^{-1}$ to $(z)/(z)^2$ by $e_i\mapsto z_i$. The square with the Koszul differential commutes, and the conormal identification is exactly the one in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension">[p3sw:hh-eq:jet-extension]</a>. A closed lift is $$\mathcal A(k)=\sum_i C_i(k)\otimes dz_i:
       K\longrightarrow(K\otimes V^*)[1].$$ Successive application, with the forms appended in the order fixed above, followed by exterior projection, is the lift <a id="p3sw:hh-eq:jet-power-lift"></a>$$\mathcal A^{(b)}(k)=
 \sum_{i_1,\ldots,i_b}C_{i_b}\cdots C_{i_1}(k)
       \otimes dz_{i_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{i_b}.
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:jet-power-lift}$$ It represents the spliced $b$-fold first-jet class: composing the next closed lift splices the next extension, while the displayed form order is the appended order. On $e_{j_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{j_b}$, its nonzero augmented terms are indexed by permutations. The contraction and exterior factors have the same permutation sign, which cancels. The result is $b!\,dz_{j_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{j_b}$. The augmentation is zero on other Koszul degrees.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:universal-exponential"></a>
On the other hand, the degree-$b$ component of $\Delta_*\mathbf I_A\circ\eta_\Delta$ is the projection from $K^{-b}$ followed by $e_I\mapsto dz_I$. The preceding calculation and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:completion-hom" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:completion-hom">[p3sw:hh-eq:completion-hom]</a> therefore prove, on the original diagonal, the identity of morphisms $$\Delta_*\mathbf I_A\circ\eta_\Delta
    =\sum_{b=0}^d\frac{a_\Delta^b}{b!}:
       \mathcal O_\Delta\longrightarrow
                   \bigoplus_{b=0}^d\Delta_*\Omega_A^b[b].
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:universal-exponential}$$ This is a direct abelian proof of the universal exponential identity of <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[P3HHCaldararuII](#ref-P3HHCaldararuII), Proposition 4.4], with its sign and divided powers specified by the same Koszul comparison.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential"></a>
Let $$AH_Q=\Phi_{\eta_\Delta}(Q):Q\longrightarrow Q\otimes H_A .$$ The projection formula for a diagonal kernel identifies its target as written. Applying the derived integral-kernel operation to <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:universal-exponential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:universal-exponential">[p3sw:hh-eq:universal-exponential]</a> gives $$(1_Q\otimes\mathbf I_A)\circ AH_Q
      =\sum_{b=0}^d\frac{a_Q^b}{b!}
          :Q\longrightarrow Q\otimes\mathcal S_A .
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential}$$ For a vector bundle, the first infinitesimal diagonal is finite over the second factor, so its evaluation is the ordinary first jet sequence. For a perfect complex the same equality is the derived kernel operation, computed locally on a bounded complex of vector bundles. It commutes with splicing and exterior projection. Consequently the powers in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential">[p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential]</a> are the raw powers $a_Q^b$ already defined, also when $Q$ is shifted.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation"></a>
Equations <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr">[p3sw:hh-eq:adjoint-hkr]</a> and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential">[p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential]</a> give $$\chi_Q(\operatorname{HKR}_A(\alpha))
       =(1_Q\otimes\alpha)\circ
                          \sum_{b=0}^d\frac{a_Q^b}{b!}.
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation}$$ If $\alpha^{a,b}\in H^a(A,\bigwedge^bT_A)$, its morphism from $\mathcal S_A$ is zero on all summands except $\Omega_A^b[b]$. Its value in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation">[p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation]</a> is exactly $\alpha^{a,b}\mathbin{\lrcorner}(a_Q^b/b!)$, of Ext degree $a+b$, with the graded dual and total-complex signs fixed above. This proves the evaluation identity in the present perfect abelian setting; compare <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[P3HHHuang](#ref-P3HHHuang), Theorem A].

The components in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character">[p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character]</a> are the unscaled $d\log$ Hodge components. To identify them with the ordinary character, first consider a line bundle. Its first jet extension is the $d\log$ transition cocycle; thus <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character">[p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character]</a> is the exponential of that first Hodge Chern class. Perfect trace is additive for a bounded locally free exact triangle, as is seen from supertrace on its cone. Pullback naturality of the first-jet class and the projective bundle formula allow a vector bundle to be pulled to its flag bundle, where its successive line quotients give the line-bundle calculation. Pullback on the Hodge cohomology in question is injective by that formula. Finally, the resolution property of a smooth projective variety represents every perfect class by an alternating sum of vector bundles; additivity treats $Q$. This gives the character identification of <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[P3HHCaldararuII](#ref-P3HHCaldararuII), Theorem 4.5] in the present convention.

Let $\operatorname{ch}_{B,j}(Q)\in H^{2j}(A,\mathbb Q)$ denote the rational Betti Chern component. Under the Dolbeault–de Rham identification $H^j(A,\Omega_A^j)=H^{j,j}(A)\subset H^{2j}(A,\mathbb C)$, the raw comparison is <a id="p3sw:hh-eq:tate-normalization"></a>$$\operatorname{ch}_{H,j}(Q)
       \longmapsto (2\pi i)^j\operatorname{ch}_{B,j}(Q).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:tate-normalization}$$ Indeed, the exponential sequence with kernel $2\pi i\mathbb Z$ sends the $d\log$ cocycle to $2\pi i\,c_1^B$, and the line splitting and additivity just used give the formula in every weight. Dividing by $(2\pi i)^j$ is the Tate-normalized comparison. The trace identity <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:triangle">[p3sw:hh-eq:triangle]</a> uses $\operatorname{ch}_H$ throughout.

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
##### Proof of the trace identity

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-hh-triangle</summary>

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:unit-module"></a>
*Proof of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>.* For $\beta:\mathcal O_\Delta\to\mathcal O_\Delta[2]$, write $a_A(\beta)=L\Delta^*\beta:H_A\to H_A[2]$. Naturality of the adjunction unit, with its canonical shift isomorphisms, is the equality $$\Delta_*a_A(\beta)\circ\eta_\Delta
       =\eta_\Delta[2]\circ\beta.$$ Evaluate this equality of diagonal morphisms on $Q$. The projection formula gives $$(1_Q\otimes a_A(\beta))\circ AH_Q
       =AH_Q[2]\circ\chi_Q(\beta).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:unit-module}$$ This is an identity of derived morphisms before any trace is taken.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace"></a>
Define $$h_Q=\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,H_A}(AH_Q)\in\mathbb H^0(A,H_A),
 \qquad
 \sigma_Q^H(v)
   =\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,H_A[2]}(AH_Q[2]\circ v)
        \in\mathbb H^2(A,H_A).$$ The perfect partial trace commutes with a morphism of coefficients: for $f:C\to C'$ and $w:Q\to Q\otimes C$, $$\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,C'}((1_Q\otimes f)\circ w)
      =f\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,C}(w).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace}$$ This follows directly from its coevaluation–braiding–evaluation definition: $f$ can be placed after evaluation since it acts only on the coefficient factor. Apply it to <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:unit-module" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:unit-module">[p3sw:hh-eq:unit-module]</a>. We obtain $$\sigma_Q^H(\chi_Q(\beta))=a_A(\beta)\circ h_Q.$$ There is no odd exchange sign, since the degree here is two.

By <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential">[p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential]</a> and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace">[p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace]</a>, $$\mathbf I_A(h_Q)=\operatorname{ch}_H(Q),\qquad
 \mathbf I_A(\sigma_Q^H(v))
     =\operatorname{Tr}_Q
          \left(\left(\sum_{j=0}^d\frac{a_Q^j}{j!}\right)[2]
                         \circ v\right).$$ In the second equality the component with coefficient $\Omega_A^j[j+2]$ is $\sigma_{Q,j}$; those with $j>d-2$ vanish because their sheaf cohomology degree exceeds $d$. Take $\beta=\operatorname{HKR}_A(\alpha)$ and apply <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr">[p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr]</a>. This proves <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:triangle">[p3sw:hh-eq:triangle]</a>. For the component statement, contraction of $\operatorname{ch}_{H,j+b}(Q)\in
 H^{j+b}(A,\Omega_A^{j+b})$ removes $b$ holomorphic forms and adds cochain degree $a$. Its target is $H^{j+b+a}(A,\Omega_A^j)=H^{j+2}(A,\Omega_A^j)$. This gives <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components">[p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components]</a>. In particular the factors $1/b!$ in evaluation, $1/j!$ in $\sigma_{Q,j}$, and $1/(j+b)!$ in the Chern component have already been accounted for; there is no further scalar. ◻

</details>

The factorization just proved is the perfect-complex specialization of the character and module identities in <a id="pm-cite-65"></a>[[P3HHBF](#ref-P3HHBF), Proposition 6.2.1, Corollary 6.3.2, and Proposition 6.4.4]. Their differential-operator convention has $a_Q=-\operatorname{At}^{BF}_Q$. Thus their terms $(-1)^j(\operatorname{At}^{BF}_Q)^j/j!$ are exactly $a_Q^j/j!$. There is no residual sign in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components">[p3sw:hh-eq:triangle-components]</a>. The proof above establishes the identity from the diagonal unit and the fixed bar comparison, rather than importing a compatibility between two unspecified HKR normalizations.

<a id="p3sw:hh-fourier-mukai"></a>
#### Fourier–Mukai transport of evaluation and contraction

We now show that a specified Fourier–Mukai equivalence preserves the equality $\ker\operatorname{ob}_Q=\ker c_Q$. Evaluation is transported by convolution of kernels. For character contraction we construct a homological map using perfect Serre pairings; this also records the sign of the shift in the fixed transform.

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
##### Enhanced convolution and evaluation

Let $A$ and $B$ be smooth projective complex abelian varieties of dimensions $d_A,d_B$. Let $\Phi=\Phi_K:\operatorname{Perf}(A)\to\operatorname{Perf}(B)$ be a Fourier–Mukai equivalence with perfect kernel $K$, equipped with a fixed enhanced inverse kernel $L$, inverse adjunction units and counits, and their triangle identities. Here *enhanced* means that convolution, morphisms, and the isomorphisms commuting with cohomological shifts are those of the derived enhancement, not just chosen identifications of objects in the triangulated category.

For kernels $P$ from $X$ to $Y$ and $R$ from $Y$ to $Z$, write $$R\star P
  =Rp_{XZ*}\bigl(Lp_{XY}^*P\otimes^{\mathbf L}Lp_{YZ}^*R\bigr).$$ Thus $\Phi_{R\star P}=\Phi_R\circ\Phi_P$. We use the standard enhanced associativity and projection formula for this convolution. The products are smooth proper and the projections are proper perfect morphisms, so these operations send perfect kernels to perfect kernels. The fixed adjunctions give $$K\star L\simeq\mathcal O_{\Delta_B},
 \qquad L\star K\simeq\mathcal O_{\Delta_A}.$$ Conjugation $$\mathcal C_\Phi(M)=K\star M\star L
       :\operatorname{Perf}(A\times A)
           \longrightarrow\operatorname{Perf}(B\times B)$$ is an exact enhanced equivalence, with the opposite conjugation as inverse. Let $t:\mathcal C_\Phi(\mathcal O_{\Delta_A})
       \xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal O_{\Delta_B}$ be its counit identification. For $\beta\in HH^r(A)$, define <a id="p3sw:hh-eq:hh-conjugation"></a>$$\Phi^{HH}(\beta)
       =t[r]\circ\mathcal C_\Phi(\beta)\circ t^{-1}
       \in HH^r(B).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:hh-conjugation}$$ It is a graded isomorphism and preserves our Yoneda convention, because every displayed shifted morphism uses the enhanced shift isomorphism.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square"></a>
For $Q\in\operatorname{Perf}(A)$, let $\Phi_{\mathrm{Ext}}:\operatorname{Ext}^2_A(Q,Q)
\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Ext}^2_B(\Phi Q,\Phi Q)$ be the induced map with the same shift structure. Associativity of convolution, evaluated on $Q$, gives $$\chi_{\Phi Q}(\Phi^{HH}\beta)
       =\Phi_{\mathrm{Ext}}(\chi_Q(\beta)),
       \qquad \beta\in HH^2(A).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square}$$ Indeed, evaluation on $\Phi Q$ is the composite $K\star\beta\star L\star K$ evaluated on $Q$. The inverse unit and counit cancel the adjacent $L\star K$ by the adjunction triangle identities. The outer $t$ then gives exactly the morphism obtained by applying $\Phi$ to the evaluation on $Q$. This proves the equality on morphisms, including their shifts, rather than only identifying the dimensions of Ext groups.

Use the fixed HKR maps of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a> to put $$\Phi^{HT}
   =(\operatorname{HKR}_B)^{-1}\Phi^{HH}\operatorname{HKR}_A:
       HT^2(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}HT^2(B).$$ The evaluation square is already established. The contraction map has the corresponding compatibility, with the homological comparison map constructed below.

<a id="stmt-p3sw-hh-transport"></a>
<div id="p3sw:hh-transport" class="proposition">

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares"></a>
<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport"></a>
**Proposition 26** (Transport of evaluation and contraction). *For the fixed enhanced Fourier–Mukai equivalence $\Phi:\operatorname{Perf}(A)\to\operatorname{Perf}(B)$, there exists an isomorphism $$\Phi^H_{HH}:H\Omega_{-2}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}H\Omega_{-2}(B)$$ depending only on that enhanced equivalence, not on a choice of perfect complex, such that for every $Q\in\operatorname{Perf}(A)$, $$\begin{split}
 \Phi_{\mathrm{Ext}}\circ\operatorname{ob}_Q
     &=\operatorname{ob}_{\Phi Q}\circ\Phi^{HT},\\
 \Phi^H_{HH}\circ c_Q&=c_{\Phi Q}\circ\Phi^{HT}.
\end{split}
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares}$$ Here $\operatorname{ob}$ and $c$ have the conventions of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>, and $\Phi_{\mathrm{Ext}}$ is the induced enhanced map on degree-two Ext. Consequently $$\begin{split}
 \Phi^{HT}(\ker\operatorname{ob}_Q)
       &=\ker\operatorname{ob}_{\Phi Q},\\
 \Phi^{HT}(\ker c_Q)&=\ker c_{\Phi Q}.
\end{split}
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport}$$ In particular $\ker\operatorname{ob}_Q=\ker c_Q$ implies $\ker\operatorname{ob}_{\Phi Q}=\ker c_{\Phi Q}$.*

</div>

We construct $\Phi^H_{HH}$ using perfect Serre pairings and then prove the two squares. The construction will also retain the Chern-character sign of a shift in the equivalence.

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
##### Serre pairings and the homological map

We state explicitly the duality used to define homological transport. On a smooth proper variety $X$, Serre duality is the perfect natural pairing $$\operatorname{Hom}(P,R)^*
      \simeq\operatorname{Hom}(R,\mathsf S_XP),\qquad
 \mathsf S_XP=P\otimes\omega_X[\dim X],$$ for perfect $P,R$; write $\operatorname{Tr}_{X,P}:\operatorname{Hom}(P,\mathsf S_XP)
\to\mathbb C$ for its trace. We use its standard expression as the Serre trace on $\mathcal O_X$ after the perfect partial trace. In particular it is supertrace on a shifted complex.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology"></a>
Put $$\Sigma_A=\Delta_*(\omega_A[d_A]),\qquad
 \Sigma_A^{-1}=\Delta_!\mathcal O_A
               =\Delta_*(\omega_A^{-1}[-d_A]).$$ Here $\Delta_!=\mathsf S_{A\times A}^{-1}\Delta_*\mathsf S_A$ is left adjoint to $L\Delta^*$. This is the adjunction obtained by applying Serre duality to $L\Delta^*\dashv\Delta_*$. The displayed formula for $\Delta_!\mathcal O_A$ follows from $\Delta^*\omega_{A\times A}=\omega_A^{\otimes2}$. Define Hochschild homology by $$HH_m(A)=\operatorname{Hom}_{A\times A}
              (\Sigma_A^{-1}[m],\mathcal O_{\Delta_A})
          \simeq\mathbb H^{-m}(A,H_A),
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology}$$ where the isomorphism is this adjunction. For $\beta\in HH^2(A)$ and $h:\Sigma_A^{-1}[m]\to\mathcal O_{\Delta_A}$, set $$\beta\cap h=(\beta\circ h)[-2]\in HH_{m-2}(A).$$ Naturality of the adjunction in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology">[p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology]</a> identifies this with the action of $L\Delta^*\beta$ used in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr">[p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr]</a>.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-pairing"></a>
For $m=0,-2$, let $$\mathscr T^m(A)=\operatorname{Hom}_{A\times A}
                 (\mathcal O_{\Delta_A},\Sigma_A[m]).$$ The Serre functor of $A\times A$ takes $\Sigma_A^{-1}[m]$ to $\Sigma_A[m]$. Therefore Serre duality gives the perfect pairing $$\langle h,\delta\rangle_{A,m}
    =\operatorname{Tr}_{A\times A,\,\Sigma_A^{-1}[m]}
                      (\delta\circ h):
 HH_m(A)\otimes\mathscr T^m(A)\longrightarrow\mathbb C.
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:serre-pairing}$$ This kernel description and its categorical character are the ones studied in <a id="pm-cite-66"></a>[[P3HHCW](#ref-P3HHCW), Theorems 6 and 10, Section 6.2]. We give the required transport and its cap verification in these pairings.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence"></a>
The Grothendieck-duality adjoint formulas for a perfect kernel on smooth proper varieties are $$K^R=K^\vee\otimes p_A^*\omega_A[d_A],
 \qquad
 K^L=K^\vee\otimes p_B^*\omega_B[d_B],$$ viewed after exchange of the two factors as kernels from $B$ to $A$. We use these formulas with their adjunction units and counits. They give $$K^R\simeq\Sigma_A\star K^L\star\Sigma_B^{-1}.$$ For an equivalence the fixed inverse $L$ carries both inverse adjunctions. Taking the mate of the last isomorphism with these adjunctions gives the kernel Serre isomorphism $$\theta:K\star\Sigma_A\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma_B\star K.$$ Its evaluation is the trace-preserving Serre isomorphism: for $f:P\to\mathsf S_AP$, $$\operatorname{Tr}_{B,\Phi P}(\theta_P\circ\Phi f)
       =\operatorname{Tr}_{A,P}(f).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence}$$ To see why the coherence matters, transport the natural Serre duality pairing through the equivalence. Its uniqueness determines the isomorphism $\Phi\mathsf S_A\to\mathsf S_B\Phi$ making those pairings commute; evaluation at the identity gives <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence">[p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence]</a>. The adjoint formulas with the same units and counits realize this isomorphism by $\theta$. An isomorphism between $K^R$ and $K^L$ as unstructured objects would not specify it. Smoothness, properness, and perfection are exactly the hypotheses for these duality and adjunction statements.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:test-composition"></a>
<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-evaluation"></a>
Let $u:\mathcal C_\Phi(\Sigma_A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma_B$ be $\theta\star L$ followed by $\Sigma_B\star t$, and set $$\Psi^m(\delta)
   =u[m]\circ\mathcal C_\Phi(\delta)\circ t^{-1}:
       \mathscr T^m(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr T^m(B)
       \quad(m=0,-2).$$ For $\delta\in\mathscr T^{-2}(A)$ and $\beta\in HH^2(A)$, associativity and the middle cancellation $t^{-1}t$ give $$\Psi^0(\delta[2]\circ\beta)
      =(\Psi^{-2}\delta)[2]\circ\Phi^{HH}(\beta).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:test-composition}$$ For $\delta\in\mathscr T^0(A)$, the same associativity and the inverse adjunction triangle identities give the evaluation identity $$(\Psi^0\delta)_{\Phi Q}
       =\theta_Q\circ\Phi(\delta_Q).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:serre-evaluation}$$ Here $\delta_Q:Q\to\mathsf S_AQ$ is evaluation of the Serre-valued diagonal morphism. In this second equality the triangles cancel the adjacent $L\star K$ in the evaluation on $Q$. Thus both identities use the same $t$, $u$, and inverse adjunctions.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport"></a>
Define the homological isomorphisms for $m=0,-2$ by the perfect pairings: $$\bigl\langle\Phi_*^{HH}h,\Psi^m\delta\bigr\rangle_{B,m}
       =\langle h,\delta\rangle_{A,m}
       \quad(h\in HH_m(A),\ \delta\in\mathscr T^m(A)).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport}$$ Existence, uniqueness, and invertibility follow from the nondegeneracy of <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:serre-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-pairing">[p3sw:hh-eq:serre-pairing]</a> and the invertibility of $\Psi^m$.

The homological comparison map asserted in Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-transport">26</a> is now defined by <a id="p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-transport"></a>$$\Phi^H_{HH}
    =\mathbf I_B\,\Phi_*^{HH}\,\mathbf I_A^{-1}:
       H\Omega_{-2}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}H\Omega_{-2}(B).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-transport}$$ The adjunctions <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology">[p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology]</a> are understood. This map depends only on the fixed enhanced equivalence and may mix the individual Hodge bidegrees within the displayed spaces.

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-hh-transport"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-hh-transport</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-transport">26</a>.* The first equality in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares">[p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares]</a> follows from <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square">[p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square]</a>, <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation">[p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation]</a>, and the definition of $\Phi^{HT}$.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:cap-transport"></a>
For the second square, we verify compatibility with the cap action and the categorical character. If $\beta\in HH^2(A)$, $h\in HH_0(A)$, and $\delta\in\mathscr T^{-2}(A)$, the definition of cap and the trace of a shifted perfect object give $$\langle\beta\cap h,\delta\rangle_{A,-2}
       =\langle h,\delta[2]\circ\beta\rangle_{A,0}.$$ The shift is even, so its supertrace sign is $+1$. Applying this on $A$ and $B$, and using <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:test-composition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:test-composition">[p3sw:hh-eq:test-composition]</a> and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport]</a>, gives $$\begin{split}
 \langle\Phi_*^{HH}(\beta\cap h),\Psi^{-2}\delta\rangle_{B,-2}
  &=\langle\beta\cap h,\delta\rangle_{A,-2}\\
  &=\langle h,\delta[2]\circ\beta\rangle_{A,0}\\
  &=\langle\Phi_*^{HH}h,
             (\Psi^{-2}\delta)[2]\circ\Phi^{HH}\beta\rangle_{B,0}\\
  &=\langle\Phi^{HH}\beta\cap\Phi_*^{HH}h,
                                      \Psi^{-2}\delta\rangle_{B,-2}.
\end{split}$$ The tests $\Psi^{-2}\delta$ exhaust $\mathscr T^{-2}(B)$. Nondegeneracy proves the needed transport, and only in this degree: $$\Phi_*^{HH}(\beta\cap h)
       =\Phi^{HH}(\beta)\cap\Phi_*^{HH}(h),
       \qquad \beta\in HH^2(A),\quad h\in HH_0(A).
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:cap-transport}$$

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:character-trace"></a>
Let $\mathfrak h_Q\in HH_0(A)$ correspond, under <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology">[p3sw:hh-eq:hh-homology]</a>, to $h_Q=\operatorname{Tr}_{Q,H_A}(AH_Q)$. We verify its trace characterization using the same $AH_Q$ as in Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>. For $\delta:\mathcal O_\Delta\to\Sigma_A$, let $\delta':H_A\to\omega_A[d_A]$ be its adjoint, so $\delta=\Delta_*\delta'\circ\eta_\Delta$. The adjunction $\Delta_!\dashv L\Delta^*$, defined by Serre duality above, is trace-compatible. Thus <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace">[p3sw:hh-eq:partial-trace]</a> and the standard partial-trace expression of the Serre trace give $$\begin{split}
 \langle\mathfrak h_Q,\delta\rangle_{A,0}
  &=\operatorname{Tr}_{A,\mathcal O_A}(\delta'\circ h_Q)\\
  &=\operatorname{Tr}_{A,\mathcal O_A}
       \left(\operatorname{Tr}_Q
              ((1_Q\otimes\delta')\circ AH_Q)\right)\\
  &=\operatorname{Tr}_{A,Q}(\delta_Q).
\end{split}
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:character-trace}$$ The last equality uses evaluation of $\Delta_*\delta'\circ\eta_\Delta$. It includes the supertrace when $Q$ is shifted. Because the pairing is perfect, this characterizes $\mathfrak h_Q$ uniquely; it is the categorical Chern character in $HH_0(A)$. Its image under $\mathbf I_A$ is $\operatorname{ch}_H(Q)$ by <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential">[p3sw:hh-eq:perfect-exponential]</a>.

<a id="p3sw:hh-eq:character-transport"></a>
For a test $\delta_B=\Psi^0\delta_A$, equations <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport]</a>, <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:character-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:character-trace">[p3sw:hh-eq:character-trace]</a>, <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:serre-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-evaluation">[p3sw:hh-eq:serre-evaluation]</a>, and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence">[p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence]</a> yield $$\begin{split}
 \langle\Phi_*^{HH}\mathfrak h_Q,\delta_B\rangle_{B,0}
  &=\langle\mathfrak h_Q,\delta_A\rangle_{A,0}\\
  &=\operatorname{Tr}_{A,Q}((\delta_A)_Q)\\
  &=\operatorname{Tr}_{B,\Phi Q}((\delta_B)_{\Phi Q})\\
  &=\langle\mathfrak h_{\Phi Q},\delta_B\rangle_{B,0}.
\end{split}$$ The tests exhaust $\mathscr T^0(B)$, so uniqueness gives $$\Phi_*^{HH}(\mathfrak h_Q)=\mathfrak h_{\Phi Q}.
\label{p3sw:hh-eq:character-transport}$$

Now take $\alpha\in HT^2(A)$. By <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr">[p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr]</a>, the class $c_Q(\alpha)$ is the $\mathbf I_A$-image of $\operatorname{HKR}_A(\alpha)\cap\mathfrak h_Q$. Equations <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:cap-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:cap-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:cap-transport]</a> and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:character-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:character-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:character-transport]</a> take its homological transport to $$\Phi^{HH}(\operatorname{HKR}_A(\alpha))
                    \cap\mathfrak h_{\Phi Q}.$$ Apply $\mathbf I_B$ and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr">[p3sw:hh-eq:cap-hkr]</a> on $B$. The result is $c_{\Phi Q}(\Phi^{HT}\alpha)$, as required. The three comparison maps in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares">[p3sw:hh-eq:transport-squares]</a> are isomorphisms. Taking kernels proves <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:kernel-transport]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-37"></a>
###### Source identification and shifts.

The Serre-pairing definition also makes the source identification of homological transport explicit. Let $\theta_{\mathcal C}$ be the trace-preserving Serre isomorphism of the equivalence $\mathcal C_\Phi$ between the two kernel categories. There is a unique $$v:\mathcal C_\Phi(\Sigma_A^{-1})
                \xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma_B^{-1}$$ such that $$u=\mathsf S_{B\times B}(v)\circ
             (\theta_{\mathcal C})_{\Sigma_A^{-1}}:
      \mathcal C_\Phi(\Sigma_A)\longrightarrow\Sigma_B.$$ Indeed, apply the inverse Serre functor to $u\circ(\theta_{\mathcal C})_{\Sigma_A^{-1}}^{-1}$. Preservation of the Serre trace by $\theta_{\mathcal C}$ then shows that <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport]</a> is exactly $$h\longmapsto t\circ\mathcal C_\Phi(h)\circ v[m]^{-1}.$$ The source identification is obtained from Serre and left–right adjunction coherence, whereas the target identification is $t$.

The distinction fixes the sign of a shift. Test it in $\operatorname{Perf}(\mathbb C)$ with $K=\mathbb C[s]$ and $L=\mathbb C[-s]$. The categorical trace of the identity of $\mathbb C[s]$ is $\mathrm{ev}\circ\mathrm{braid}\circ\mathrm{coev}=(-1)^s$. Thus <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence">[p3sw:hh-eq:serre-coherence]</a> forces $\theta=(-1)^s$ in this test. It follows that $\Psi^0$ is multiplication by $(-1)^s$, and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport">[p3sw:hh-eq:homology-transport]</a> makes $\Phi_*^{HH}$ on $HH_0$ multiplication by $(-1)^s$ as well. In agreement with perfect trace, $$\operatorname{ch}_H(Q[s])=(-1)^s\operatorname{ch}_H(Q).$$ In particular a shift $[-3]$ reverses this character. Cancelling $K$ and $L$ on both ends of a homology morphism without the Serre source identification would lose this sign. Degree-two evaluation has an even shift, so no additional odd exchange sign occurs there; it still uses the enhanced shift isomorphisms in <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square">[p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square]</a>.

The transported maps have source $HT^2$; no naturality of $\sigma_Q$ on arbitrary Ext classes is asserted. The map $\Phi^H_{HH}$ is defined by Hochschild homology and the fixed HKR maps, not identified with an independently normalized Betti integral transform.

<a id="p3sw:invariant-section"></a>
#### The invariant obstruction space of the Abel-orbit seed

Retain the seed of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:seed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:seed">17</a>. Thus $C$ is a smooth nonhyperelliptic curve of genus three, $X=\operatorname{Pic}^2(C)$ has the specified group origin and is identified with its Jacobian, and $H=\mathcal O_X(\Theta)$ is its principal theta line. Put $$\theta_{\mathrm B}=c_{1,\mathrm B}(H)\in H^2(X,\mathbb Q),
 \qquad \int_X\theta_{\mathrm B}^3=6,\qquad n=d+1\geq4.$$ The two subschemes supplied there are disjoint unions $$A=\coprod_{j=1}^n C_j,\qquad
 \Sigma=\coprod_{j=1}^n\Sigma_j$$ of translates of an Abel curve and of its inverse, respectively. The cyclic groups $G_2,G_1\subset X$, each of order $n$, act transitively on the components of $A,\Sigma$, respectively. Write $$\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A\quad\text{on }X^2,\qquad
 G_{\mathrm{src}}=G_1\times G_2.$$ Each ideal has its canonical translation linearization as the ideal of an invariant subscheme, and $\mathcal I$ has the product linearization. On $M=X\times\widehat X$, the fixed transform of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:coherent-action" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:coherent-action">24</a> is $$\widetilde\Phi=\Phi\circ\bigl((H\boxtimes H)\otimes-\bigr),
 \qquad \mathcal G=\widetilde\Phi(\mathcal I)[-3].$$ Write $\bar G\simeq G_{\mathrm{src}}$ for the underlying translation group of its coherent line-translation action.

We will prove that the semiregularity map of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a> is injective on the full invariant derived group $\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G}$. The proof has two computations: the invariant group for $\mathcal I$ has dimension $48$, and contraction with its raw Hodge Chern character has rank $48$. The exact Hochschild–Atiyah identity then identifies the entire invariant group with the evaluation image. This calculation concerns the Abel-orbit seed in Markman’s secant-sheaf construction <a id="pm-cite-67"></a>[[Markman2025Secant](#ref-Markman2025Secant), Sections 8.3–8.4]; we give the invariant calculation directly, using the independently movable orbit components.

<a id="pm-h-39"></a>
##### The Abel class and its normal directions

An *Abel curve* in $X$ will mean a translate of the image of an Abel embedding $C\hookrightarrow X$, or a translate of its image under $[-1]$, with the Jacobian and polarization conventions of Section <a href="#p3sw:support-component" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:support-component">[p3sw:support-component]</a>.

<a id="stmt-p3sw-abel-character"></a>
<div id="p3sw:abel-character" class="lemma">

<a id="p3sw:invariant-betti-character"></a>
**Lemma 27**. *Let $D\subset X$ be an Abel curve. Its normalized Betti class and Chern character are $$[D]_{\mathrm B}=\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^2}{2},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(\mathcal O_D)
   =\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^2}{2}
      -\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^3}{3}.$$ Normal restriction of translation-invariant vector fields is an isomorphism $$H^0(X,T_X)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^0(D,N_{D/X});$$ both spaces have dimension three. If $Z=\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ is a disjoint union of $n$ Abel curves, then its ideal satisfies the full normalized Betti identity $$\label{p3sw:invariant-betti-character}
 \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(I_Z)
 =1-\frac n2\theta_{\mathrm B}^2+\frac n3\theta_{\mathrm B}^3.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-abel-character"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-abel-character</summary>

*Proof.* The fundamental-class formula is <a href="#p3sw:support-abel-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:support-abel-class">[p3sw:support-abel-class]</a>, proved there from the Jacobian lattice. Translation acts trivially on cohomology, and inversion acts by $+1$ on $H^4$, so it holds for every Abel curve in the stated sense.

For the inclusion $i:D\hookrightarrow X$, use Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch in its smooth proper form $$\operatorname{ch}(i_*F)\operatorname{td}(X)
   =i_*\bigl(\operatorname{ch}(F)\operatorname{td}(D)\bigr)$$ for a closed immersion of smooth projective varieties; this is the form of the Riemann–Roch theorem recorded by Borel and Serre <a id="pm-cite-68"></a>[[p3swBorelSerreRR](#ref-p3swBorelSerreRR)]. The tangent bundle of $X$ is trivial, and $\operatorname{td}(D)=1+\tfrac12c_1(T_D)$. Since $\deg T_D=2-2\cdot3=-4$ and $[\mathrm{pt}]_{\mathrm B}=\theta_{\mathrm B}^3/6$, it follows that $$\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(\mathcal O_D)
 =i_*\operatorname{td}(D)
 =[D]_{\mathrm B}-2[\mathrm{pt}]_{\mathrm B}
 =\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^2}{2}
     -\frac{\theta_{\mathrm B}^3}{3}.$$ For disjoint $Z$, one has $\mathcal O_Z=\bigoplus_j\mathcal O_{D_j}$. Additivity in $0\to I_Z\to\mathcal O_X\to\mathcal O_Z\to0$ gives <a href="#p3sw:invariant-betti-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-betti-character">[p3sw:invariant-betti-character]</a>.

<a id="p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence"></a>
It remains to calculate the normal sections. For the original Abel embedding the normal sequence is $$\label{p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow T_C\longrightarrow
 \mathcal O_C\otimes H^1(C,\mathcal O_C)
 \longrightarrow N_{C/X}\longrightarrow0.$$ Here the middle bundle is $T_X|_C$. The dual of the first arrow is the evaluation map $H^0(C,K_C)\otimes\mathcal O_C\to K_C$: differentiating the Abel integral sends a tangent vector $v$ to the functional $\omega\mapsto\omega(v)$. Consequently, under Serre duality on $C$, the dual of the map $$H^1(C,T_C)\longrightarrow
 H^1(C,\mathcal O_C)\otimes H^1(C,\mathcal O_C)$$ in the long exact sequence of <a href="#p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence">[p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence]</a> is the multiplication map $$H^0(C,K_C)\otimes H^0(C,K_C)\longrightarrow H^0(C,K_C^2).$$ This is the usual cohomological Serre duality <a id="pm-cite-69"></a>[[p3swSerreDuality](#ref-p3swSerreDuality)], applied to the dual evaluation map.

For completeness, multiplication is onto here by an elementary genus-three argument. For an effective divisor $D_2$ of length two, curve Riemann–Roch, in the form $h^0(L)-h^0(K_C\otimes L^{-1})=\deg L+1-g$, gives $$h^0(K_C-D_2)=h^0(D_2)=1.$$ The last equality uses nonhyperellipticity. Two independent sections of a degree-two effective line, after removing their common base divisor, would give a map to $\mathbb P^1$ of degree at most two. Degree one would make $C$ rational, and degree two would make it hyperelliptic. Thus $K_C$ separates every length-two subscheme. It has three sections and degree four, so it embeds $C$ as an integral plane quartic. Such a curve is not contained in a conic. Restriction of quadrics therefore makes $\operatorname{Sym}^2H^0(K_C)\to H^0(K_C^2)$ injective. Both spaces have dimension six, the second by curve Riemann–Roch and $\deg K_C^2=8>2g-2$. The multiplication map is consequently onto, and its dual is injective.

Since $H^0(C,T_C)=0$, the long exact sequence of <a href="#p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence">[p3sw:invariant-normal-sequence]</a> now identifies $H^0(C,N_{C/X})$ with the middle bundle’s constant sections $H^1(C,\mathcal O_C)=H^0(X,T_X)$. This is exactly normal restriction of translation-invariant vector fields. Translation and inversion carry the normal sequence and this isomorphism to every Abel curve. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
##### The full invariant derived Ext group

We next compute the derived deformation space of one orbit ideal. The calculation keeps the local-to-global contribution of the normal bundle; it does not replace derived Ext by the cohomology of an endomorphism sheaf.

<a id="stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext"></a>
<div id="p3sw:invariant-orbit-ext" class="lemma">

<a id="p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation"></a>
**Lemma 28**. *Let $\Gamma\subset X$ be a finite subgroup of order $n$, and let $Z=\coprod_{j=1}^nD_j$ be a disjoint union of Abel curves permuted transitively by $\Gamma$. Give $I_Z$ its canonical linearization. There is an isomorphism of $\Gamma$-representations $$\label{p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation}
 \operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)
 \simeq H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)
       \oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^n H^0(X,T_X).$$ The first summand is trivial, and $\Gamma$ acts on the second by its regular permutation action on the components and the identity on each translation space. In particular, $$\dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\Gamma=6,\qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{Ext}^2_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\Gamma=6,\qquad
 \operatorname{Hom}_X(I_Z,I_Z)^\Gamma=\mathbb C.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-invariant-orbit-ext</summary>

<a id="p3sw:invariant-local-ext"></a>
*Proof.* At a point of $Z$, write its regular codimension-two ideal in the regular local ring $R=\mathcal O_{X,x}$ as $I=(f,g)$, where $f,g$ are part of a regular system of parameters. It has the resolution $$0\longrightarrow R\xrightarrow{(-g,f)}R^2
   \xrightarrow{(f,g)}I\longrightarrow0.$$ If an endomorphism sends $f,g$ to $u,v$, its relation is $gu=fv$. Regularity of $f,g$ gives $u=fr$, $v=gr$ for a unique $r\in R$, so $\operatorname{Hom}_R(I,I)=R$. Applying $\operatorname{Hom}_R(-,I)$ to the resolution shows that the degree-one cokernel is $I/I^2$. Under a change of generators, the dual of the leftmost generator acquires the inverse determinant. Consequently the global degree-one sheaf is $$(I_Z/I_Z^2)\otimes\det(I_Z/I_Z^2)^{-1}
 \simeq (I_Z/I_Z^2)^\vee=N_{Z/X}.$$ The rank-two isomorphism sends $v\otimes\ell^\vee$ to the functional $w\mapsto\ell^\vee(v\wedge w)$, so it is compatible with changes of generators. We view $N_{Z/X}$ here as a sheaf on $X$ supported on $Z$. Away from $Z$ the ideal is $\mathcal O_X$, and the resolution has length one. Thus, globally, $$\label{p3sw:invariant-local-ext}
 \mathcal{H}om_X(I_Z,I_Z)=\mathcal O_X,\qquad
 \mathcal{E}xt_X^1(I_Z,I_Z)=N_{Z/X},\qquad
 \mathcal{E}xt_X^q(I_Z,I_Z)=0\quad(q\geq2).$$

<a id="p3sw:invariant-local-global"></a>
The standard local-to-global spectral sequence $$E_2^{p,q}=H^p\!\left(X,\mathcal{E}xt_X^q(I_Z,I_Z)\right)
 \Longrightarrow \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_X(I_Z,I_Z)$$ is the hypercohomology spectral sequence of $R\mathcal{H}om_X(I_Z,I_Z)$. Its five-term sequence and <a href="#p3sw:invariant-local-ext" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-local-ext">[p3sw:invariant-local-ext]</a> give $$\label{p3sw:invariant-local-global}
 0\longrightarrow H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)
 \longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)
 \xrightarrow{\,e\,}H^0(Z,N_{Z/X})
 \longrightarrow H^2(X,\mathcal O_X).$$ The first arrow is also the map induced by the perfect unit $\mathcal O_X\to R\mathcal{H}om(I_Z,I_Z)$: the perfect trace composed with this unit is multiplication by the rank, which is one.

Translate the $n$ curves independently. The locus in $X^n$ where these translated proper curves remain disjoint is an open neighborhood of the original tuple. Over it their union is a disjoint union of flat translated families. Its ideal is flat as well, by the exact sequence from the structure sheaf of the union. Restriction of this family to first-order tangent vectors gives a linear map $$s:\bigoplus_{j=1}^nH^0(X,T_X)
   \longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z).$$ Indeed a flat deformation over $\mathbb C[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^2)$ gives the extension $0\to I_Z\to I_{Z,\epsilon}\to I_Z\to0$. For a vector field $\xi$, the moving generators are $f-\epsilon\xi(f)$ and $g-\epsilon\xi(g)$. Lifting the displayed relation $(-g,f)$ gives $$(-g)\bigl(f-\epsilon\xi(f)\bigr)
 +f\bigl(g-\epsilon\xi(g)\bigr)
 =\epsilon\bigl(g\xi(f)-f\xi(g)\bigr).$$ Thus the local Ext cocycle is represented by $\overline{g\xi(f)-f\xi(g)}\in I/I^2$, up to the single sign in the chosen Ext boundary convention. Choose $\ell=\bar f\wedge\bar g$ and $\ell^\vee(\ell)=1$. Under the written determinant identification $v\otimes\ell^\vee\mapsto
(w\mapsto\ell^\vee(v\wedge w))$, this displayed representative sends $\bar f,\bar g$ to $-\overline{\xi(f)},-\overline{\xi(g)}$, respectively. Reversing the boundary convention reverses both signs. Consequently the edge map $e$ sends the geometric deformation to its normal restriction up to one fixed sign, independent of the vector field and the component. By Lemma <a href="#p3sw:abel-character" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:abel-character">27</a>, $e\circ s$ is therefore the direct sum of the normal-restriction isomorphisms multiplied by that fixed sign. It follows that <a href="#p3sw:invariant-local-global" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-local-global">[p3sw:invariant-local-global]</a> reduces to the split short exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)
 \longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_Z,I_Z)
 \xrightarrow{e}H^0(Z,N_{Z/X})\longrightarrow0,$$ with splitting given by $s$ after this signed normal-restriction identification.

This splitting is equivariant. Translation acts trivially on $H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)$, as is seen from translation-invariant Dolbeault representatives, and it acts as the identity on $H^0(X,T_X)$. It sends the deformation of $D_j$ to the deformation of its translated component. Since a group of order $n$ acts transitively on $n$ components, that permutation action is regular. This proves <a href="#p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation">[p3sw:invariant-orbit-representation]</a>; its invariant summands have dimensions three and three.

We also use Serre duality for perfect complexes on a smooth projective threefold: $$\operatorname{Ext}^i(F,G)^\vee
 \simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{3-i}(G,F\otimes\omega_X).$$ This is the extension of the usual locally free Serre duality <a id="pm-cite-70"></a>[[p3swSerreDuality](#ref-p3swSerreDuality)] by finite locally free resolutions. Choose a nonzero translation-invariant holomorphic three-form on $X$. For $F=G=I_Z$, the resulting perfect pairing is $$\operatorname{Ext}^1(I_Z,I_Z)\otimes
 \operatorname{Ext}^2(I_Z,I_Z)
 \longrightarrow H^3(X,\mathcal O_X)\simeq\mathbb C.$$ It is $\Gamma$-invariant: translations preserve the chosen form, composition, and the perfect trace. Thus these two Ext groups are dual $\Gamma$-representations. Averaging over the finite group in characteristic zero identifies invariants of the dual with the dual of the invariants, so the invariant $\operatorname{Ext}^2$ also has dimension six. Finally <a href="#p3sw:invariant-local-ext" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-local-ext">[p3sw:invariant-local-ext]</a> gives $\operatorname{Hom}(I_Z,I_Z)=H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)=\mathbb C$, with trivial action. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-p3sw-invariant-ext"></a>
<div id="p3sw:invariant-ext" class="proposition">

**Proposition 29**. *For the source object $\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A$ and its canonical $G_{\mathrm{src}}=G_1\times G_2$ linearization, $$\dim_{\mathbb C}
 \operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)^{G_{\mathrm{src}}}
 =6+36+6=48.$$ This is the invariant subspace of the full derived Ext group.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-ext"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-invariant-ext</summary>

<a id="p3sw:invariant-product-ext"></a>
*Proof.* The ideal sheaves are perfect by their local length-one resolutions. The perfect Künneth isomorphism for their derived global Hom complexes is $$\mathbf R\operatorname{Hom}_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)
 \simeq
 \mathbf R\operatorname{Hom}_X(I_\Sigma,I_\Sigma)
 \otimes_{\mathbb C}^{\mathbf L}
 \mathbf R\operatorname{Hom}_X(I_A,I_A).$$ Taking degree two gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)
 \simeq{}&
 \operatorname{Ext}^2_X(I_\Sigma,I_\Sigma)
       \otimes\operatorname{Hom}_X(I_A,I_A) \notag\\
 &\oplus\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_\Sigma,I_\Sigma)
       \otimes\operatorname{Ext}^1_X(I_A,I_A) \notag\\
 &\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_X(I_\Sigma,I_\Sigma)
       \otimes\operatorname{Ext}^2_X(I_A,I_A).
 \label{p3sw:invariant-product-ext}
\end{aligned}$$ Here the isomorphism follows by taking cohomology of the tensor product over $\mathbb C$ of the two derived global Hom complexes; over the field $\mathbb C$ there are no additional Tor terms. It is equivariant for the independent actions of $G_1,G_2$. Averaging in each factor gives $(V_1\otimes V_2)^{G_1\times G_2}
=V_1^{G_1}\otimes V_2^{G_2}$. Applying Lemma <a href="#p3sw:invariant-orbit-ext" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-orbit-ext">28</a> to the three summands of <a href="#p3sw:invariant-product-ext" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-product-ext">[p3sw:invariant-product-ext]</a> gives $6$, $6\cdot6$, and $6$. Because <a href="#p3sw:invariant-product-ext" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-product-ext">[p3sw:invariant-product-ext]</a> is a decomposition of derived Ext itself, the count retains every local-to-global contribution. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-41"></a>
##### The rank of contraction with the raw Hodge character

We now compute the map that Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a> factors through this $48$-dimensional invariant group. Use the gradings <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:gradings" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:gradings">[p3sw:hh-eq:gradings]</a>; in particular, for an abelian variety $B$, $$H\Omega_{-k}(B)=\bigoplus_{q-p=k}H^q(B,\Omega_B^p)
 \qquad(k\in\mathbb Z).$$ For a perfect object $Q$ on $B$, extend character contraction to every degree $k\geq0$ by $$c_Q^k:HT^k(B)\longrightarrow H\Omega_{-k}(B),\quad
 c_Q^k(\alpha)=\alpha\mathbin{\lrcorner}
                    \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm H}(Q).$$ Here $\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm H}$ is the raw first-jet character <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character">[p3sw:hh-eq:hodge-character]</a>, and contraction uses the left-interior action <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:left-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:left-action">[p3sw:hh-eq:left-action]</a>, including its Dolbeault Koszul sign. Thus $c_Q^2=c_Q$ in Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>.

<a id="p3sw:invariant-weight-dictionary"></a>
<a id="p3sw:invariant-hodge-character"></a>
The normalization of the character matters for that identity. Put $\theta_{\mathrm H}=a_H\in H^1(X,\Omega_X^1)$. Under the raw de Rham comparison, $$\label{p3sw:invariant-weight-dictionary}
 \theta_{\mathrm H}\longmapsto(2\pi i)\theta_{\mathrm B},\qquad
 \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm H,k}(Q)
   \longmapsto(2\pi i)^k\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B,k}(Q)
 \quad\text{in codimension }k.$$ This is the weightwise dictionary of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>; the raw first-jet class is the negative of the Buchweitz–Flenner Atiyah convention. In particular Lemma <a href="#p3sw:abel-character" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:abel-character">27</a> gives for either orbit ideal $$\label{p3sw:invariant-hodge-character}
 f_n:=\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm H}(I_Z)
 =1-\frac n2\theta_{\mathrm H}^2+\frac n3\theta_{\mathrm H}^3.$$ There is no codimension-one component. Each term in <a href="#p3sw:invariant-hodge-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-hodge-character">[p3sw:invariant-hodge-character]</a> has the raw weight specified by <a href="#p3sw:invariant-weight-dictionary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-weight-dictionary">[p3sw:invariant-weight-dictionary]</a>.

Use the abelian Hodge-cohomology identifications $$\mathsf U=H^1(X,\mathcal O_X),\qquad
 \mathsf W=H^0(X,\Omega_X^1),\qquad
 \mathsf T=H^0(X,T_X)=\mathsf W^\vee,
 \qquad
 H^q(X,\Omega_X^p)=\wedge^q\mathsf U\otimes\wedge^p\mathsf W.$$ These follow from the invariant Dolbeault forms on the complex torus. The spaces $\mathsf U,\mathsf W,\mathsf T$ each have dimension three. The polarization class $\theta_{\mathrm H}\in\mathsf U\otimes\mathsf W$ is nondegenerate, so in the fixed contraction convention $$j:\mathsf T\longrightarrow\mathsf U,\qquad
 j(\xi)=\xi\mathbin{\lrcorner}\theta_{\mathrm H}$$ is an isomorphism.

<a id="stmt-p3sw-invariant-one-factor-rank"></a>
<div id="p3sw:invariant-one-factor-rank" class="lemma">

**Lemma 30**. *For either orbit ideal $I_Z$, the map $c_{I_Z}^1$ is injective of rank six, and $c_{I_Z}^2$ is surjective of rank six.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-one-factor-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-invariant-one-factor-rank</summary>

<a id="p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections"></a>
*Proof.* An element of $HT^1(X)=\mathsf U\oplus\mathsf T$ is a pair $(\eta,\xi)$. Using <a href="#p3sw:invariant-hodge-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-hodge-character">[p3sw:invariant-hodge-character]</a>, its projections under $c_{I_Z}^1$ to $H^1(\mathcal O_X)$ and $H^2(\Omega_X^1)$ are respectively $$\label{p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections}
 \eta,\qquad -n\,j(\xi)\wedge\theta_{\mathrm H}.$$ The first uses the rank term of the character; the second uses $-n\theta_{\mathrm H}^2/2$. There is no contribution from $\xi$ to the first projection or from $\eta$ to the second, because the codimension-one character is zero. The signs in <a href="#p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections">[p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections]</a> are those of the specified left-interior convention.

The map $v\mapsto v\wedge\theta_{\mathrm H}$ on $\mathsf U$ is injective. If $v\ne0$, choose bases with $v=u_1$ and $\theta_{\mathrm H}=\sum_{i=1}^3u_i\otimes w_i$, absorbing the fixed contraction signs in the dual basis. The terms of $v\wedge\theta_{\mathrm H}$ with $i=2,3$ are linearly independent nonzero tensors. Therefore the two projections in <a href="#p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections">[p3sw:invariant-degree-one-projections]</a> determine $\eta$ and $\xi$, proving the first assertion.

<a id="p3sw:invariant-degree-two-target"></a>
<a id="p3sw:invariant-degree-two-map"></a>
The only summands of $H\Omega_{-2}(X)$ are $$\label{p3sw:invariant-degree-two-target}
 H^2(X,\mathcal O_X)=\wedge^2\mathsf U,\qquad
 H^3(X,\Omega_X^1)=\wedge^3\mathsf U\otimes\mathsf W.$$ Each has dimension three. The $H^2(\mathcal O_X)=\wedge^2\mathsf U$ block of $HT^2(X)$ maps identically to the first summand, using the rank term $f_{n,0}=1$. The $H^1(T_X)=\mathsf U\otimes\mathsf T$ block has no contribution there, since $f_{n,1}=0$, and its projection to the second summand is $$\label{p3sw:invariant-degree-two-map}
 \eta\otimes\xi\longmapsto
 -n\,\eta\wedge j(\xi)\wedge\theta_{\mathrm H}.$$ For each $k\in\{1,2,3\}$, take $\eta$ and $j(\xi)$ to be the two basis elements $u_i,u_j$ complementary to $u_k$. Their wedge with $\theta_{\mathrm H}$ is a nonzero multiple of $(u_1\wedge u_2\wedge u_3)\otimes w_k$. Hence <a href="#p3sw:invariant-degree-two-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-degree-two-map">[p3sw:invariant-degree-two-map]</a> spans the second summand. These two source blocks already give rank six; the target in <a href="#p3sw:invariant-degree-two-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-degree-two-target">[p3sw:invariant-degree-two-target]</a> has dimension six. This proves surjectivity. The bivector block $H^0(\wedge^2T_X)$ is not needed for this lower bound. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-p3sw-invariant-product-rank"></a>
<div id="p3sw:invariant-product-rank" class="lemma">

**Lemma 31**. *For $\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A$, the map $$c_{\mathcal I}^2:HT^2(X^2)\longrightarrow H\Omega_{-2}(X^2)$$ has rank $48$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-product-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-invariant-product-rank</summary>

*Proof.* Künneth gives the domain decomposition $$HT^2(X^2)=
 \bigl(HT^2(X)\otimes HT^0(X)\bigr)
 \oplus\bigl(HT^1(X)\otimes HT^1(X)\bigr)
 \oplus\bigl(HT^0(X)\otimes HT^2(X)\bigr).$$ The product character is $f_n\boxtimes f_n$, and $HT^0(X)=\mathbb C$ acts by $c_{I_Z}^0(1)=f_n\ne0$. The written left-action and product formulas <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:left-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:left-action">[p3sw:hh-eq:left-action]</a> and <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:products">[p3sw:hh-eq:products]</a>, applied to the Künneth decomposition, give the external-product contraction rule with its total-complex signs. They therefore send the three blocks onto $$\operatorname{im}(c_{I_\Sigma}^2)\otimes\mathbb C f_n,\qquad
 \operatorname{im}(c_{I_\Sigma}^1)\otimes
       \operatorname{im}(c_{I_A}^1),\qquad
 \mathbb C f_n\otimes\operatorname{im}(c_{I_A}^2).$$ Their dimensions are $6,36,6$, by Lemma <a href="#p3sw:invariant-one-factor-rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-one-factor-rank">30</a>. The target has the direct Künneth decomposition $$H\Omega_{-2}(X^2)
 =\bigoplus_{r+s=2}H\Omega_{-r}(X)\otimes H\Omega_{-s}(X).$$ The first image lies in the factorwise Hodge-imbalance summand $(2,0)$, the second in $(1,1)$, and the third in $(0,2)$. Here the imbalance of $H^q(X,\Omega_X^p)$ is $q-p$; the two-factor Hodge Künneth decomposition is a direct sum for these ordered pairs. Hence the three images cannot cancel, regardless of their Künneth signs. Their ranks add to $6+36+6=48$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-42"></a>
##### The exact rank comparison and the fixed transform

The two calculations now have the same dimension. We use the exact local Hochschild results to turn this equality into injectivity on the whole invariant group. Write $o_Q=\operatorname{ob}_Q$ for evaluation and $\sigma_Q$ for the perfect-trace map of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>. For a perfect object $Q$ on an abelian variety $B$, their domains and targets are $$HT^2(B)\xrightarrow{\ o_Q\ }\operatorname{Ext}^2_B(Q,Q)
        \xrightarrow{\ \sigma_Q\ }H\Omega_{-2}(B).$$

<a id="stmt-p3sw-invariant-injectivity"></a>
<div id="p3sw:invariant-injectivity" class="proposition">

<a id="p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion"></a>
**Proposition 32**. *For the source $\mathcal I=I_\Sigma\boxtimes I_A$ with its canonical $G_{\mathrm{src}}=G_1\times G_2$-linearization, $$\label{p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion}
 \operatorname{im}(o_{\mathcal I})
   =\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)^{G_{\mathrm{src}}},
 \qquad
 \ker(o_{\mathcal I})=\ker(c_{\mathcal I}^2),$$ and $\sigma_{\mathcal I}$ is injective on this full invariant group.*

<a id="p3sw:invariant-target-conclusion"></a>
*Let $M=X\times\widehat X$ and use the fixed enhanced equivalence $$\widetilde\Phi=\Phi\circ\bigl((H\boxtimes H)\otimes-\bigr),
 \qquad
 \mathcal G=\widetilde\Phi(\mathcal I)[-3].$$ Give $\mathcal G$ the coherent line-translation action from Proposition <a href="#p3sw:coherent-action" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:coherent-action">24</a>, and denote its underlying translation group by $\bar G\simeq G_{\mathrm{src}}$. Then $$\label{p3sw:invariant-target-conclusion}
 \operatorname{im}(o_{\mathcal G})
   =\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G},
 \qquad
 \ker(o_{\mathcal G})=\ker(c_{\mathcal G}^2),$$ and $$\sigma_{\mathcal G}\big|_{
       \operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G}}
 \quad\text{is injective}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-invariant-injectivity"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-invariant-injectivity</summary>

*Proof.* Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a> proves for every perfect $Q$ the exact identity $$\sigma_Q\circ o_Q=c_Q^2$$ with the definitions just given. In particular it is an identity for the perfect trace on derived Ext, with the same contraction convention as the rank computation. The components with $q>\dim B-2$ vanish because $H^{q+2}(B,\Omega_B^q)=0$.

Translations act trivially on $HT^2(X^2)$: they fix the invariant tangent fields and the invariant Dolbeault representatives of $H^a(\mathcal O_{X^2})$. The translation-invariant diagonal-kernel HKR construction identifies this with their trivial action on $HH^2(X^2)$. Apply the diagonal-kernel evaluation square <a href="#p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square">[p3sw:hh-eq:evaluation-square]</a> to each translation equivalence, and identify its translate of $\mathcal I$ with $\mathcal I$ by the canonical linearization. Naturality of evaluation then gives $g\cdot o_{\mathcal I}(\alpha)=o_{\mathcal I}(g\cdot\alpha)
=o_{\mathcal I}(\alpha)$ for $g\in G_{\mathrm{src}}$. Hence $$\operatorname{im}(o_{\mathcal I})
 \subset
 W_{\mathrm{inv}}
 :=\operatorname{Ext}^2_{X^2}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)^{G_{\mathrm{src}}}.$$ Proposition <a href="#p3sw:invariant-ext" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-ext">29</a> gives $\dim W_{\mathrm{inv}}=48$, while Lemma <a href="#p3sw:invariant-product-rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-product-rank">31</a> and the exact identity give $$48=\operatorname{rank}(c_{\mathcal I}^2)
 \leq\operatorname{rank}(o_{\mathcal I})
 \leq\dim W_{\mathrm{inv}}=48.$$ Thus the inclusion is equality and both maps have rank $48$. The identity also gives $\ker(o_{\mathcal I})\subset\ker(c_{\mathcal I}^2)$; equality of ranks on their common domain makes the kernels equal. If $w=o_{\mathcal I}(\alpha)\in W_{\mathrm{inv}}$ and $\sigma_{\mathcal I}(w)=0$, then $c_{\mathcal I}^2(\alpha)=0$, so the kernel equality gives $w=0$. This proves <a href="#p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion">[p3sw:invariant-source-conclusion]</a> and source injectivity.

<a id="p3sw:invariant-transport-squares"></a>
Apply Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-transport">26</a> to the fixed enhanced equivalence $Q\mapsto\widetilde\Phi(Q)[-3]$. It includes tensoring by $H\boxtimes H$, the fixed Fourier–Mukai kernel, and the displayed shift. That proposition gives isomorphisms $$u:HT^2(X^2)\xrightarrow{\sim}HT^2(M),\quad
 v:\operatorname{Ext}^2(\mathcal I,\mathcal I)
       \xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Ext}^2(\mathcal G,\mathcal G),
 \quad
 \gamma:H\Omega_{-2}(X^2)\xrightarrow{\sim}H\Omega_{-2}(M)$$ such that $$\label{p3sw:invariant-transport-squares}
 v\,o_{\mathcal I}=o_{\mathcal G}\,u,\qquad
 \gamma\,c_{\mathcal I}^2=c_{\mathcal G}^2\,u.$$ The map $\gamma$ is the HKR expression of the enhanced Hochschild-homology isomorphism: its cap and character naturality, including the shift sign, is exactly what gives the second equality. No identification of $\gamma$ with a separately normalized Betti integral transform is used.

Proposition <a href="#p3sw:coherent-action" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:coherent-action">24</a> constructs the target action by coherent conjugation of the canonical source action by this same equivalence. Hence $v$ is an isomorphism of the corresponding representations. The lines in the target line-translation action cancel on derived endomorphisms, and the underlying group is the specified $\bar G\simeq G_{\mathrm{src}}$. Therefore $$v(W_{\mathrm{inv}})
 =\operatorname{Ext}^2_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G}.$$ The first equality of <a href="#p3sw:invariant-transport-squares" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-transport-squares">[p3sw:invariant-transport-squares]</a> transports the source image equality to the image equality in <a href="#p3sw:invariant-target-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:invariant-target-conclusion">[p3sw:invariant-target-conclusion]</a>. Both $u$ and $\gamma$ are isomorphisms, so its second equality transports the source kernel equality to the target kernel equality. Finally apply $\sigma_{\mathcal G}o_{\mathcal G}=c_{\mathcal G}^2$, the target instance of Proposition <a href="#p3sw:hh-triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:hh-triangle">25</a>. An invariant $z$ has the form $o_{\mathcal G}(\alpha)$; if $\sigma_{\mathcal G}(z)=0$, the target kernel equality gives $z=0$. This proves injectivity on the full target invariant Ext group. ◻

</details>

The fixed support construction identifies $$\mathcal E=R\mathcal{H}om_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal O_M)[-1]$$ as the actual coherent perfect sheaf of positive rank, with the dual coherent action on the same underlying translation group. Proposition <a href="#p3sw:twisted-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:twisted-descent">40</a> uses the full injection just proved, transfers it by perfect duality, and then performs finite étale twisted descent. The exact dual sign and that derived descent are left to that proposition. The output here is injection on $\operatorname{Ext}^2(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)^{\bar G}$ itself, not only on a chosen Hochschild subspace. No identification of that derived Ext group with $H^2(M,\mathcal H^0(R\mathcal{H}om_M(\mathcal G,\mathcal G)))$ is used.

<a id="p3:sec:spin"></a>
### The spin character and the polarized quadratic seed

<span id="p3sw:spin-seed" label="p3sw:spin-seed"></span>

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:normalized-character"></a>
The preceding construction gives a coherent perfect sheaf $\mathcal E$ of rank $r=8d$ on $M=X\times\widehat X$. Its normalized rational Betti character is $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:normalized-character}
 \kappa_M
   =\exp\!\left(-\frac{c_{1,\mathrm B}(\mathcal E)}r\right)
             \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(\mathcal E),$$ where $\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}$ and $c_{1,\mathrm B}$ are the ordinary rational Betti Chern character and first Chern class. We compute $\kappa_M$ and identify the polarized quadratic structure on this same sixfold. The calculation uses the secant spinors of Markman <a id="pm-cite-71"></a>[[Markman2025Secant](#ref-Markman2025Secant), Sections 2.4 and 6]. We give a finite Clifford proof of the required transform identity, so the identity applies to the algebraic spin group, including its elements that do not preserve the initial Hodge structure.

<a id="pm-h-44"></a>
#### The fixed transform and its source character

Put $\ell_X=6=2g_X$, with $g_X=3$, and set $$L=H^1(X,\mathbb Q),\qquad U=L^\vee=H^1(\widehat X,\mathbb Q),\qquad V=L\oplus U.$$ Choose $e_1,\ldots,e_{\ell_X}$ with $\int_X e_1\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{\ell_X}=1$, and let $f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell_X}$ be the dual basis of $U$. Our normalized Poincaré line has class $$p=c_{1,\mathrm B}(\mathcal P)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell_X} e_i\wedge f_i.$$ For an ordered subset $P\subset\{1,\ldots,\ell_X\}$, write $e_P$ and $f_P$ for the corresponding increasing products. For disjoint ordered subsets $I,J$, let $\epsilon_{I,J}$ be the sign in $e_Ie_J=\epsilon_{I,J}e_{I\cup J}$, and set it to zero if they overlap. Define reversal by $$\tau(e_P)=(-1)^{|P|(|P|-1)/2}e_P .$$

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:fourier"></a>
We use Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch in the following standard form: if $f:B\to C$ is a proper morphism of smooth projective complex varieties and $F$ is perfect, then $$\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(Rf_*F)\operatorname{td}(C)
   =f_*\bigl(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(F)\operatorname{td}(B)\bigr).$$ This is the Riemann–Roch theorem of Borel and Serre <a id="pm-cite-72"></a>[[p3swBorelSerreRR](#ref-p3swBorelSerreRR)]. In a Fourier transform between abelian varieties the Todd classes are one. This gives the usual cohomological Poincaré transform; compare the calculation in <a id="pm-cite-73"></a>[[GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001](#ref-GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001), Section 4.2.3]. The second-factor transform in the fixed functor $\Phi$ of <a href="#p3sw:support-fixed-transform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:support-fixed-transform">[p3sw:support-fixed-transform]</a> uses the inverse Poincaré line and the shift $[g_X]$. It is therefore $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:fourier}
 F_-(\alpha)=(-1)^{g_X}(\pi_{\widehat X})_*
            \bigl(e^{-p}\pi_X^*\alpha\bigr).$$ The factor $(-1)^{g_X}$ is the Chern-character sign of the shift.

<a id="stmt-p3sw-fourier-matrix"></a>
<div id="p3sw:fourier-matrix" class="lemma">

**Lemma 33** (The fixed Fourier matrix). *For $P\subset\{1,\ldots,\ell_X\}$, $k=|P|$, one has $$F_-(e_P)=(-1)^{k(k+3)/2}\epsilon_{P,P^c}f_{P^c}.$$ In particular $F_-$ is a rational isomorphism preserving parity.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-fourier-matrix"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-fourier-matrix</summary>

*Proof.* In <a href="#p3sw:spin-eq:fourier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:spin-eq:fourier">[p3sw:spin-eq:fourier]</a>, only the complementary indices in $e^{-p}$ can contribute to integration on $X$. Moving their $f$-factors past the $e$-factors gives the coefficient $$(-1)^{\,g_X+(\ell_X-k)(\ell_X-k+1)/2}\epsilon_{P,P^c}.$$ The difference of the displayed exponent and $k(k+3)/2$ is $2(g_X+1)(g_X-k)$, since $\ell_X=2g_X$, and hence is even. Every basis vector goes to a signed complementary basis vector, proving the remaining assertions. ◻

</details>

For $\mu(x,y)=(x+y,y)$ define the graded Künneth maps <a id="p3sw:spin-eq:fixed-maps"></a>$$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:fixed-maps}
 \phi=(1\otimes F_-)\mu^*,\qquad
 \nu=(F_-\otimes1)\mu^*,\qquad
 D=F_-^{-1}\otimes F_-,\qquad
 \phi'=\phi(1\otimes\tau)=D\nu(1\otimes\tau).$$ The input of $D$ is written in the order $U\oplus L$, and its output in the order $L\oplus U$; all identifications use the graded shuffle. These are fixed rational isomorphisms, determined only by the oriented lattice, addition, and $p$. Equivalently, $$\phi'(s\otimes t)=(-1)^{g_X}\int_{X_y}
          s(x+y)\tau(t)(y)e^{-\sum_i y_i z_i}.$$ This is finite exterior-algebra coefficient extraction in $y_1\cdots y_{\ell_X}$; the output variables $x,z$ represent $L,U$. In particular the maps do not vary with a complex period having the given marking.

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:source-character"></a>
<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:sheaf-transform"></a>
<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:sheaf-duality"></a>
We now identify the tensor to which this transform applies. Write $\Theta=c_{1,\mathrm B}(H)\in\Lambda^2L$ for the principal class of the seed. Both orbit ideals have $n=d+1$ disjoint Abel components, so Lemma <a href="#p3sw:abel-character" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:abel-character">27</a> gives their common Betti character $$f_n=1-\tfrac n2\Theta^2+\tfrac n3\Theta^3.$$ Thus the sheaves $F_1=I_A(H)$ and $F_2=I_\Sigma(H)$ used in $\Phi$ have common character $$v=e^\Theta f_{d+1}
   =1+\Theta-\tfrac d2\Theta^2-\tfrac d6\Theta^3.$$ Fix $K=\mathbb Q(u)$, where $u^2=-d$ and $d\geq3$ is the integer of the seed construction, and put $$\lambda_+=e^{u\Theta},\qquad \lambda_-=e^{-u\Theta}
       \quad\text{in }(\Lambda L)_K.$$ Expanding the exponentials in powers of $\Theta$ gives $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:source-character}
 v=\tfrac12\bigl[(1+u^{-1})\lambda_+
                    +(1-u^{-1})\lambda_-\bigr].$$ The two exponential classes are linearly independent: both have constant term $1$, and their degree-two terms are opposite and nonzero. Reversal acts by $(-1)^j$ on $\Theta^j$, so it interchanges $\lambda_+$ and $\lambda_-$. The rational tensor $T=v\otimes\tau v$ therefore lies in the tensor square of their two-dimensional $K$-span. Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch gives $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:sheaf-transform}
 \beta:=\operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}\Phi(F_2\boxtimes F_1)
       =\phi(v\otimes v)=\phi'(T).$$ To relate this class to the original sheaf, let $\iota$ be the ring involution acting by $(-1)^j$ on $H^{2j}(M,\mathbb Q)$. The shifts in $\mathcal G=\Phi(F_2\boxtimes F_1)[-3]$ and $\mathcal E=D_M(\mathcal G)[-1]$ give $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:sheaf-duality}
 \mathcal E=D_M\bigl(\Phi(F_2\boxtimes F_1)\bigr)[2],\qquad
 \operatorname{ch}_{\mathrm B}(\mathcal E)=\iota\beta,\qquad
 \beta_0=r=8d.$$ Here the rank is supplied by Proposition <a href="#p3sw:coherent-sheaf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:coherent-sheaf">23</a>. We next introduce the Clifford action on $\Lambda L$ and compare its transport through $\phi'$ with the graded action on $H^*(M)$. The exponential correction will disappear under the normalization defining $\kappa_M$.

<a id="pm-h-45"></a>
#### The exact Clifford identity

Give $V$ the symmetric split form $$Q(e_i,f_j)=\delta_{ij},\qquad Q(e_i,e_j)=Q(f_i,f_j)=0.$$ Use the Clifford algebra with relation $vw+wv=Q(v,w)$. We use the standard Clifford construction of the spin group in this normalization: the norm-one even Clifford normalizer $\operatorname{Spin}(V,Q)$ acts on $V$ by conjugation through $\rho:\operatorname{Spin}(V,Q)\to SO(V,Q)$, and acts on a Clifford module by multiplication. The norm is Clifford reversion times the element. For this split rational construction, see <a id="pm-cite-74"></a>[[GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001](#ref-GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001), Sections 3.2 and 3.4–3.5]. Only this construction, not a realization of arbitrary spin elements by derived autoequivalences, is used here.

On $S=\Lambda L$ the Fock action is $$c(e)=\epsilon_e=e\wedge-,\qquad c(f)=\iota_f.$$ Write $m_g=c(g)$. Conjugation by the fixed $\phi'$ defines the rational algebraic representation $$\rho'_g=\phi'(m_g\otimes m_g)(\phi')^{-1}
       \quad\text{on }\Lambda V.$$ The following calculation is the matrix-unit Clifford realization underlying <a id="pm-cite-75"></a>[[GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001](#ref-GolyshevLuntsOrlov2001), Proposition 3.2.1(e)], with the signs of the fixed transform retained.

<a id="stmt-p3sw-spin-identity"></a>
<div id="p3sw:spin-identity" class="proposition">

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:identity"></a>
**Proposition 34** (Fixed Clifford identity). *For every algebraic spin element $g$, one has the exact equality of rational representations $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:identity}
 \rho'_g=e^{p/2}\Lambda\rho(g)e^{-p/2}
       =e^{(p-\Lambda^2\rho(g)p)/2}\Lambda\rho(g).$$ The exponentials denote exterior multiplication. In particular $$[\rho'_g(1)]_0=1,\qquad
 [\rho'_g(1)]_2=\tfrac12\bigl(p-\Lambda^2\rho(g)p\bigr).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-p3sw-spin-identity"><summary>Proof of stmt-p3sw-spin-identity</summary>

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:rank-one"></a>
*Proof.* Exterior multiplication and contraction give every matrix unit between the monomial basis vectors of $S$: first project onto a specified monomial by products of the commuting occupation projections $\epsilon_{e_i}\iota_{f_i}$ and $\iota_{f_i}\epsilon_{e_i}$, and then remove and insert the required generators. The ordered Clifford monomials span at most $2^{2\ell_X}$ dimensions, which equals $\dim\operatorname{End}(S)$. Thus $$C(V,Q)\simeq\operatorname{End}(S).$$ Its even part is the parity-preserving part: the inclusion follows from parity of the generators, and both sides have dimension $2^{2\ell_X-1}$. Put $F=f_1\cdots f_{\ell_X}$ in the Clifford algebra and define $$\varphi:S\otimes S\longrightarrow C(V,Q),\qquad
 \varphi(s\otimes t)=sF\tau(t).$$ On $S$ this is the rank-one operator $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:rank-one}
 c(\varphi(s\otimes t))(r)
   =(-1)^{g_X}[\tau(t)\wedge r]_{\mathrm{top}}\,s.$$ Indeed $F$ contracts the full oriented monomial with sign $(-1)^{\ell_X(\ell_X-1)/2}=(-1)^{g_X}$. The pairing $\langle t,r\rangle=[\tau(t)\wedge r]_{\mathrm{top}}$ is perfect. Exterior multiplication is self-adjoint for it by reversal of products. Contraction is self-adjoint as well: apply $\iota_f$ to a zero product in exterior degree $\ell_X+1$ and use $$\tau\iota_f|_{\Lambda^kL}
     =(-1)^{k-1}\iota_f\tau|_{\Lambda^kL}.$$ Consequently Clifford reversion is adjunction for this pairing. Every spin element has reversion norm one, and therefore preserves the pairing. Formula <a href="#p3sw:spin-eq:rank-one" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:spin-eq:rank-one">[p3sw:spin-eq:rank-one]</a> now proves that $\varphi$ is an isomorphism intertwining the diagonal spin action with conjugation on $C(V,Q)$.

For any bilinear form $B$ on $V$ with $B(v,v)=Q(v,v)/2$, set $$c_B(v)=\epsilon_v+\iota_{B(v,-)}
       \quad\text{on }\Lambda V,\qquad
 \psi_B(x)=c_B(x)1 .$$ The Clifford relations follow directly from the exterior and contraction relations. On each ordered Clifford monomial $\psi_B$ has that exterior monomial as its leading term, so $\psi_B$ is a triangular isomorphism. Take $$B_0(e_i,f_j)=\delta_{ij},\quad B_0(f_i,e_j)=0,\qquad
 B_s=Q/2,\qquad
 A_0=B_0-B_s=\tfrac12\sum_i e^i\wedge f^i,$$ with other entries of $B_0$ zero. Here $e^i,f^i$ are the covectors dual to $e_i,f_i$.

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:symbol"></a>
The fixed map $\nu$ is exactly the nonsymmetric Clifford symbol: $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:symbol}
 \nu(1\otimes\tau)=\psi_{B_0}\varphi .$$ To check every sign, let $I'=P\setminus I$, and let $j=|J|$. Both sides on $e_P\otimes e_J$ equal <a id="p3sw:spin-eq:symbol-basis"></a>$$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:symbol-basis}
 (-1)^{j(j-1)/2}
 \sum_{I\subseteq P}
   \epsilon_{I',I}\epsilon_{I,J}
   (-1)^{\sum_{i\in I'}i-|I'|}
   f_{(I')^c}\wedge e_{I\cup J}.$$ For the left side, expand $\mu^*e_P$ and apply Lemma <a href="#p3sw:fourier-matrix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:fourier-matrix">33</a>. For the right side use $$c_{B_0}(e_i)=\epsilon_{e_i}+\iota_{f^i},\qquad
 c_{B_0}(f_i)=\epsilon_{f_i},$$ and $$\iota_{f^{i_1}}\cdots\iota_{f^{i_s}}f_{\{1,\ldots,\ell_X\}}
       =(-1)^{\sum_{i\in I'}i-|I'|}f_{(I')^c}.$$ Moving the remaining $e_I$ past the $f$’s changes $\epsilon_{I,I'}$ into $\epsilon_{I',I}$. This proves <a href="#p3sw:spin-eq:symbol" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:spin-eq:symbol">[p3sw:spin-eq:symbol]</a>.

For this finite exterior calculation define $\iota_{\alpha\wedge\beta}=\iota_\beta\iota_\alpha$ for covectors on $V$. This fixes the contraction convention locally; the Hochschild contraction used earlier was specified independently. The ordinary commutator identity $$[\iota_{\alpha\wedge\beta},\epsilon_v]
   =\alpha(v)\iota_\beta-\beta(v)\iota_\alpha
   =\iota_{(\alpha\wedge\beta)(v,-)}$$ and commutation of even contractions with contractions give $$c_{B_0}(v)=e^{\iota_{A_0}}c_{B_s}(v)e^{-\iota_{A_0}},
 \qquad
 \psi_{B_0}=e^{\iota_{A_0}}\psi_{B_s}.$$ The second equality has no scalar, since $e^{-\iota_{A_0}}1=1$. All exponentials are finite. If $R=\Lambda\rho(g)$, preservation of $Q$ gives $$Rc_{B_s}(v)R^{-1}=c_{B_s}(\rho(g)v),
 \qquad
 R\psi_{B_s}(x)=\psi_{B_s}(gxg^{-1}).$$ Thus the symmetric symbol is spin-equivariant.

<a id="p3sw:spin-eq:duality"></a>
It remains to conjugate $\iota_{A_0}$ by $D$. On the input $\Lambda(U\oplus L)$ fix the orientation $\Omega=f_1\cdots f_{\ell_X} e_1\cdots e_{\ell_X}$. The exterior determinant pairing of $Q$ defines $PD_Q$ by $$(PD_Q\eta,\zeta)_Q=[\eta\wedge\zeta]_\Omega.$$ For $|P|=k$, $|J|=j$, and $\delta=k+j$, direct evaluation gives $$PD_Q(f_Je_P)=(-1)^{kj}\epsilon_{J,J^c}\epsilon_{P,P^c}
                      e_{J^c}f_{P^c}.$$ Applying Lemma <a href="#p3sw:fourier-matrix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p3sw:fourier-matrix">33</a> to the two factors of $D$ therefore yields $$\label{p3sw:spin-eq:duality}
 D|_{\Lambda^\delta V}
      =(-1)^{\,g_X+\delta(\delta+3)/2}PD_Q .$$ For example, before parity reduction the exponent in the coefficient of $D(f_Je_P)$ is $$(\ell_X-j)(\ell_X-j+3)/2+k(k+3)/2+j.$$ Its difference from $g_X+\delta(\delta+3)/2+kj$ is even; this verifies the factor order as well as the shift.

Raising a covector by $Q$ is denoted by a superscript $\sharp$. The exterior derivation rule, applied twice, gives $$PD_Q(\iota_{A_0}\eta)=A_0^\sharp\wedge PD_Q(\eta).$$ The signs in <a href="#p3sw:spin-eq:duality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:spin-eq:duality">[p3sw:spin-eq:duality]</a> in degrees $\delta-2$ and $\delta$ have ratio $-1$. Moreover $Q$ raises $e^i$ to $f_i$ and $f^i$ to $e_i$, so $$A_0^\sharp=\tfrac12\sum_i f_i\wedge e_i=-p/2,\qquad
 D\iota_{A_0}D^{-1}=\epsilon_{p/2}.$$ Thus $De^{\iota_{A_0}}D^{-1}=e^{p/2}$. The map $PD_Q$, and hence $D$, commutes with $R$: $R$ preserves $Q$, degree, and orientation, since $\det\rho(g)=1$. It follows that $F_s=D\psi_{B_s}\varphi$ intertwines the diagonal spin action with $R$. Combining the displayed identities gives $$\phi'=D\psi_{B_0}\varphi=e^{p/2}F_s,$$ which proves <a href="#p3sw:spin-eq:identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p3sw:spin-eq:identity">[p3sw:spin-eq:identity]</a>. Every map used is a rational algebraic map, so the equality is one of algebraic representations. Applying it to $1$ proves the two stated components. ◻

</details>
